## Summary

Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query is the headless, integer-only simulation record of every light in the world (the chest beam, ship fixtures, machinery lanterns, thrown glowsticks, alarm beacons and fault glows) plus the one question it answers: how much of that light reaches a registered point, and is it enough to work by. Read this GDD if you design or build anything that must ask "is this lit" (Interaction's lit-only verbs, Attention's light stimulus, Horror Entities, Suit's beam charge, 2D Lighting's drawn edge, Derelict Authoring's lamps): it fixes the emitter records and their commands and mutators, the shape table, the lamp, glowstick, beacon and fault-glow formulas, the cone and occlusion tests, the hysteresis, the caps and the acceptance criteria, and it never reads a `Light2D`, a physics space or a `PERCEPTION_OUT` view.

**Two `lit`s, kept verbatim (EM-2):** the *record field* `emitter.state.lit` means an emitter is emitting (`output_pm > 0`, true for any kind and read by the Presenter on the seat's beam); the *query* `lit(ref)` means a registered subject's exposure is above the technical-artist's threshold with hysteresis (`LIT_ON_PM` 150 / `LIT_OFF_PM` 120). Neither is ever renamed.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` (systems-index enumeration row 5, Recommended Design Order 7; Category Core) · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer`, `Life-Support Graph`, `Player Controller & Input`, Layout, Item & Module Database, Suit & Equipment, Ship Body (all designed; the counterparts that were undesigned at first draft — Layout, Suit, Ship Body, Interaction, 2D Lighting, Attention, Horror, Derelict Authoring — are now Status Designed, so the contracts below are two-sided and an item still tagged provisional waits on a counterpart's text, not on a missing GDD) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Depended on by 6 systems on the index's graph (2D Lighting & Occlusion, Interaction, Suit & Equipment, Derelict Authoring, Attention, Horror Entities), and read without an index edge by Web Map & Transit (hard: E1 at `init_world`, E10, `lit` for the pod console), Failure & Trail, Adaptive Audio & Tells and the HUD & Suit Layer (soft; Open Question 15) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

## Overview

Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query is the simulation's record of every light in the world and the one question it answers about them: *how much of that light reaches this point, and is it enough to work by?* In one sentence: it is a scene-tree-free, integer-only `SIM` block, `emitter.state`, that holds an **emitter record** for each chest beam, ship fixture, machinery lantern, thrown glowstick, alarm beacon and fault glow, written only through commands and published mutators, plus a pure **exposure query** that combines those records with the Layout's opaque footprints into a per-subject `lux_pm` (0..1000) and a `lit` bit. It is the data layer under three things the player feels directly: the edge of their own lamp (what the lamp reaches is exactly what they can read and touch), the dimming of a ship lamp when the scrubber draws, and the way the web answers to light (Attention, Horror Entities and the act-only-on-lit rule all read the same answers). It never reads a `Light2D`, never touches a physics space, never obtains a `PERCEPTION_OUT` view, and runs identically headless, on the host and under test.

The player never operates an emitter table. They meet it as a **pool of light with an edge**. The chest lamp (hands-free, Pandorum-style, origin at the per-facing chest point, `|x| <= 2` px) lays a wedge across the deck; a valve wheel half inside it prompts exactly when the wheel's anchor crosses the drawn edge and not a pixel sooner; the corridor lamps sag when the tier browns out and the junction box at the rim of their pool loses its prompt; a glowstick thrown into the next room blooms Chemlight green, holds steady, and then visibly shrinks; a lamp switched on takes a handful of ticks to warm because the ship has to serve it first. Nothing is ever shown as an exposure readout of any shape, or a "visibility" number (P3: shown in the world, never as a hidden meter): the only gameplay surface of the whole system is a prompt that is present or absent, drawn by the HUD, and the world itself.

The block exists because three decisions already made elsewhere need one honest place to live. The index (TD-SYSTEM-BOUNDARY) split *emitter state* from *light rendering* so that light is a simulation input no hallucination can touch (kernel CR-12) and so that Attention can run headless and on the host for remote players. The Controller made the beam an emitter "like any other for Attention" and made every verb act only on a lit object (PC-5, PC-6), which demands one query that the prompt, the `validate` step and the channel re-check all call. The Graph made lamps ordinary LOADs whose brightness is `base * served_pm / 1000` (registry formula `power_allocation`), which demands a place that turns that served power into a light a subject can be inside of. The result is a bottleneck with six dependents (2D Lighting & Occlusion, Interaction, Suit & Equipment, Derelict Authoring, Attention, Horror Entities).

**What the game loses without it.** There is no honest "go dark" strategy (nothing for Attention to read), no lit-only rule (PC-6 has no query to call), no way for a lamp to be dimmer because the ship is starved (P1: "a lamp never renders brighter than the power it is served"), no stable edge for the Tell's filaments to resolve at (P2), and no sim-side truth for Lighting to conform to, so the first hallucination that bends a cone would be indistinguishable from a bug. The sim/render agreement that this block guarantees by construction (the drawn hard edge *is* the queried edge for a full-output emitter) is the load-bearing P1 test of the whole lighting stack.

**Index wording, reconciled.** The index (rationale bullet 5) describes a geometric light-exposure query made of a cone test plus occluder tests on physics shapes. This GDD honours the substance, the Layout's collision footprint rectangles as the occluding shapes, and changes the means: occlusion is an integer segment-versus-rectangle test against those same rectangles behind an `OccluderSource` seam, with no physics backend in the MVP (EM-20). The reason is the index's own intent — the block "runs headless" and lets Attention run headless and on the host (kernel H10; CR-13 G3: the 2D physics engine makes no determinism claim, so a physics-derived `lit` flowing into a persisted block would have to be recorded for replay). The index and kernel wording refresh this implies is routed in Cross-References. Index numbering, once and for all: this system is **enumeration row 5**, rationale bullet 5, **Recommended Design Order 7**.

*Implementation questions raised while designing and deferred to ADRs (→ `/architecture-decision`): the `OccluderSource` seam and the integer segment test (ADR candidate 1); the subject-registration model with static and dynamic layers and counted work units (ADR candidate 2); the fixed-point direction table and Q28 cone test with zero floats on the sim path (ADR candidate 3); the broadphase (uniform grid versus room-gated) and whether the spatial index lives in Layout or here (ADR candidate 4); the occlusion-origin rule, contingent on the Lighting spike (ADR candidate 5). ADR-0017 (candidates 1 to 4 and 6) and ADR-0018 (candidate 5) now exist as Proposed *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The review adds to candidate 1 the **cell convention** of D.6 (cells as unit squares, doubled coordinates), to candidate 4 the **static-rebuild policy** (D.14 scenarios; synchronous, with the per-pair cache and the narrowing of door-leaf invalidation) and the (b) alternative it rejects, and a new candidate 6, the **read-view storage layout** (EM-25: typed or packed columns, sorted subject columns, formerly a per-tick snapshot, retired by ADR-0017 D4.2). Under workflow tier `full` these six ADRs are written with `/architecture-decision` before story breakdown, and the inline rulings and "alternatives considered" text then move into them (Open Question 24).*

## Player Fantasy

**Framing: indirect, with a cost layer.** The player experiences this system as the edge of their own light and the prompts that live inside it; they never see "exposure", an "emitter" or a "query". *(creative-director consulted; Framing A "The Honest Edge" adopted as the headline, with Framing C "Light as a Spend" as its price. Framing B "Lamp Management" — brightness dials, shape switching, lamp banks as a resource panel — was rejected: it breeds an exposure readout, which P3 forbids, and a shape-change input, which PC-5 forbids. Logged.)*

> The lamp is on your chest. It is the only light that follows you, and it is the only way to touch anything: what is not in the light cannot be worked (the four exceptions of the registry `act_gate` aside: `drop`, `toggle_beam`, the inspect card of an already-lit instrument and `throw_glowstick`, and the ACTOR_SELF self-patch, an actor verb on the player's own suit (Interaction IA-6)) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Every fixture on the home-ship, every running machine's lantern, every glowstick you leave behind is the same bargain. Light lets you read the ship, and light lets the web read you.
>
> Competence is choosing light, not having more of it. A veteran crew does not carry a brighter lamp; they know which corridor lamp to kill before a sortie, where a glowstick buys the most room for the least time, and when a second of beam on a dark corner is worth what it shows.
>
> And the edge never lies. Eyes can be wrong about what stands in the light, but not about where the light ends. When the sanity goes, the dark may fill with shapes and the cone may seem to reach further than it does; walk to the edge and it is exactly where it always was, and nothing past it will answer your hand.

**The emotion named: the bargain of sight** — agency purchased with exposure, under a guarantee that the light itself is never the liar. The power is not dominance over the dark; it is the small, honest sovereignty of your own light: you choose where the edge of the known world is, and the edge is where you left it.

**Tone and language.** Use: *lamp, chest lamp, beam, in the light, the edge, go dark, the lamp warms, the pool shrinks, spend light.* Avoid meter words (*visibility, detection, stealth, hidden, light level, lux*; `lux_pm` is an internal name and is never player-facing) and avoid *flashlight* (it implies a hand-held tool; the lamp is chest-mounted) and *torch* for the beam (the anti-pillars use "torches" for the VS-A deployables; concept Pillar 1's "carries their torch" means the beam, a wording refresh owed to the concept, listed in Cross-References). The ludonarrative anchor is the user's decision of 2026-10-26: the lamp is on the player's chest, hands-free, like Pandorum. The player *is* the lantern, so going dark is not putting a tool down; it is hiding your own body.

**Anchor moment (solo; the MVP is single-player).** Derelict A, power dead. The hatch opens on black. You thumb the chest lamp on: the click comes first, the light a breath later (PC-5: the sound pre-plays, the light follows committed state). A wedge of your wide beam — 30° half-angle, 288 px / 6 tiles, placeholder rows of the art bible 8.3 table — lays a hard edge across the deck plates. At the far end of it, half in, half out, a valve wheel. You turn a hair to the left and the wheel's prompt appears at the same moment the wheel does; a hair back and both are gone. The edge is a fact. The next compartment is past a bulkhead your beam cannot bend around. You crack a glowstick and throw it (Interaction's throw, `THROW_RANGE_TILES` 3). Chemlight green (#86B896) blooms in a room you have not entered yet, a 2-tile pool (96 px, an Item DB placeholder) that will not flicker but will shrink. Now there are two lights in this derelict and only one of them follows you. Something creaks overhead; filaments are resolving at the rim of your cone (Attention's presentation). You switch the lamp off. The dark is honest: the valve still exists, you just cannot touch it now. Behind you the glowstick you left keeps burning. You chose to leave it. It is still lit, and it is still yours to answer for.

**Second beat (home-ship, Graph-coupled).** Back aboard, the scrubber spins up and the corridor lamps sag as the power tier browns out (`lamp_output_pm` = `base * served_pm / 1000`). The pool under the galley lamp pulls in; the junction box at the edge of it, which you could reach a minute ago, is now outside the light and has no prompt. Nobody told you the power is short. The light told you. *(The crewmate beats — a downed crewmate's beam as a locator in the dark, a saner crewmate's beam as the truth check — are Vertical Slice B (VS-B); the MVP fantasy rests on the honesty of your own edge.)*

**Pillar alignment.** *Pillar 1 (primary), verbatim:* "Three channels are inviolable ... The human channel. Voice/proximity chat and a crewmate's own light beam (colour and shape) are never faked." And the art bible's Principle 1 anchor: "The physical radius your light actually reveals is a fixed anchor and never lies. Even at low sanity, the hallucination layer may add things but may not move that boundary." This block is that anchor in the simulation: the edge the player sees is the edge the query answers. *Pillar 3 (primary), verbatim:* "The players' light, noise, power use and restraint raise or lower its attention ... shown in the world, never as a hidden meter ... Restraint and stealth are real strategies." Every emitter is exposure; off-screen emitters still expose; going dark is a real, measured choice. *Pillar 4:* "No lie is unfalsifiable: the truth is always available through the physical world, a second instrument, a saner crewmate, the human channel, or time — and checking costs time, risk or trust." The true edge is a falsifier that costs a step and the exposure of standing in your own beam. *Pillar 2 (supporting):* the Tell's signature image, filaments resolving at the cone edge, needs a stable edge to resolve at. *Pillar 5 is not served* (glowstick scarcity is Suit's and the Item DB's). The "spend light" cost layer leans on glowstick scarcity, which is P5-adjacent (scarcity pushes inward), so the spend has a named economic owner and is not an unanchored promise: **the scarcity-to-depth link is routed to the Item & Module Database and Suit & Equipment** (the Item DB's `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` and its salvage-only faucet, Suit's per-seat carry and start counts, and Derelict Authoring's loose-stick supply by depth); this GDD prices the light (a stick is a real, shrinking reveal radius and a real exposure) and owns none of the supply.

**Design tests.**
1. **The honest edge (system test).** *When the place you can act differs from the place you can see light vs. they coincide → they coincide.* At rest (aim committed, not sweeping), an object whose anchor is one base pixel inside the drawn hard edge of a single full-output hard-edged emitter is in the light and prompts; one pixel outside is not and does not. This is the sim/render agreement test (a `(spike)` until Lighting exists). It holds for a *full-output* emitter; a dimmed emitter's actionable area honestly shrinks (see Formulas, `EDGE_FLOOR_PM`).
2. **Light is the price of agency.** *When a choice lets the player act without light vs. requires light to act → require light.* (the registry `act_gate` exemptions, four: `drop`, `toggle_beam`, the inspect card of an already-lit instrument and `throw_glowstick`; plus the ACTOR_SELF actor-verb rows, i.e. the self-patch (Interaction IA-6, which is not an object exception); the first draft listed three) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* Otherwise working in the dark becomes a free Attention dodge and "go dark" degenerates into the dominant strategy.
3. **Light is never free.** *When a choice makes a light safe (unseen by the web, no cost) vs. keeps every emitter an exposure → exposure.* Corollary: off-screen emitters still expose; a glowstick you walked away from is still yours to answer for.
4. **The lie draws, it does not reach.** *When a hallucination could change what is actionable vs. only what is drawn → only what is drawn.* No Filter output changes `emitter.state` or any query answer.

**Under hallucination the fantasy deepens rather than breaks.** Light gets the same split the Graph drew for the physical world. The left column is what this GDD guarantees by construction (a `SIM`-only block, kernel CR-12, EM-29); the right is what the Perception Filter and Lighting may do, as presentation only.

| Cannot lie (truth channel — this GDD guarantees) | Can lie (presentation — a decoy overlay drawn by Lighting on mask bit 3 from the Filter's per-viewer decoy list, LO-21) |
|---|---|
| The true reveal edge of every emitter: reach, cone, occlusion | Decoy lights that are not emitters (not in `emitter.state`, expose nothing) |
| Beam colour and shape, including a crewmate's (VS-B) | Shadows with no occluder (stage-2 drift, art bible 4.4) |
| Lamp and fixture brightness as served (`lamp_output_pm`) | A decoy glow appears past the true edge (an overlay, leaking violet) |
| Whether an object is in the light (`lit(subject)`), and therefore whether it prompts and accepts verbs | A decoy overlay that may itself flicker, on the decoy mask bit (Lighting; flash-clamped) |
| Glowstick waning: a real, monotone shrinking of the reveal radius | What a lit object looks like (the module-body channel: sprite, gauge) |
| Exposure to the web | — |

**The falsifier this hands the player.** A phantom extension of the cone carries no prompts and accepts no verbs, because the query never answered "in the light" there. So the cheapest truth check in the game needs no instrument: *reach for it.* If it is real light, the prompt is there. Cost: a step forward, a second, and the exposure of standing in your own beam. The ladder, cheapest first: (1) reach for it; (2) walk to the edge; (3) drop a glowstick (a second light, a second witness); (4) a saner crewmate's beam (VS-B, the human channel); (5) time. **Required leak:** any drawn extension past the true edge must carry the sane-palette leak (violet or flesh fringe at the false region — art bible 4.4, "subtle is allowed; absent is not"). That is a requirement this GDD states on Lighting and the Perception Filter, not something it implements. **Dependency of the falsifier (flag, routed):** "reach for it" holds only if no system ever draws a prompt where the query says not in the light — phantom salvage targets (kernel Open Question 14, Interaction's) and HUD prompt drawing must route through `lit(subject)` and nothing else. If a phantom target could prompt inside a decoy region the cheapest falsifier dies. An unregistered ref therefore answers exactly like an unlit one (EM-21); Interaction owns the ruling on phantom targets. *Art-bible wording tension (routed, not a value conflict):* art bible 4.4 lists "the cone seems to reach further than it does" as stage-2 drift, while Principle 1 names the true light radius an anchor that never shifts. They reconcile exactly as above — the *drawn* cone may be extended by a decoy; the *revealing* radius (what is actionable, what the query answers) does not move — and the art bible should say "a decoy glow appears past the true edge" so nobody reads it as licence to move the boundary.

**Reference techniques, taken precisely.** *Pandorum* — a chest-mounted, hands-free lamp that swings with the body (*adopt*, user decision); the hand-held torch traded against tools (*reject*: the hand point is a lightless grip, PC-5). *Project Zomboid* — lit houses seen from outside draw the horde (*adopt*: fixtures expose even when nobody is near; off-screen emitters count); the flashlight as a battery item (*adopt*, but the charge is Suit's). *Alien: Isolation* — your own light gives you away and turning it off is a tactic (*adopt*); the motion tracker as a threat readout (*reject*: no readout of any shape). *Darkwood* — the vision cone as the boundary of the known (*adopt*); the cone as a mask that hides drawn geometry (*reject*: art bible Principle 1, unlit not fogged, real occlusion). *Monaco* — line of sight as the honest rule of what you know (*adopt*: real occluders make "a shadow with no occluder" a readable lie). *Lethal Company* — a crewmate's flashlight as their locator (*adopt for VS-B*). *Amnesia / Don't Starve* — darkness drains sanity or kills (*reject*: darkness here is honest and is the *safe* choice; light is the cost; routed to Sanity so go-dark stays a valid strategy). *Doom 3* — flashlight or weapon (*reject*: no weapon exists to trade against). *Thief / Splinter Cell* — a light gem or any visibility readout (*reject*).

**What each emitter kind means to the player** (the tone table Lighting and Audio read; the numbers stay with their owners).

| Kind | Player meaning | Feel rule (presentation owner) | Numbers owned by |
|---|---|---|---|
| BEAM (chest lamp) | "My light; me." | Snaps on: the click pre-plays, the light lands on commit. Never dims for drama. A dim pool at the feet (the spill, EM-6) shows what is underfoot and nothing more. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Art bible 8.3 rows, placeholders (confirmed by the Lighting spike) |
| FIXTURE (ship lamp) | "The ship's light; it costs the ship." | Warms on across the switch latency, sags under brownout, never flickers (the cryo-bay gutter is Lighting's). | Art bible 8.3 / Item DB `emits` |
| FIXTURE, subkind DOCK_STATUS (the pod's status glow) | "The way home is still on." | A small, dim, steady glow on the docked pod at the dock room, on while the pod is parked at that dock (switched by Web Map's `pod_at` write, E10), independent of the ship's power; it is a lamp like any other, so it also shows the web where you came in (EM-11). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Derelict Authoring placement, art-director luminance (kept apart from the visible-unknown lamp), `DOCK_STATUS_*` knobs *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| NODE_LANTERN | "That machine is alive, and it is shining." | No switch: it shines because the machine runs. Killing the machine is the only way to dark it. | Item DB `emits` |
| GLOWSTICK | "A light I left behind." | Steady, never flickers, shrinks honestly; its waning is the only clock, read in the world with no timer. | Item DB `GLOWSTICK_*` placeholders |
| BEACON | "The one light nobody chose." | A rotating sweep, an alarm owned by Attention's breach machine. | Art bible 8.3, `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` |
| FAULT_GLOW | "The failure lights its own repair, and the web sees it too." | Ember, arc and cyan mist as point sources. | Failure & Trail's descriptor-to-glow table |
| (VS-A) flare / torch / decoy | "A light I spend to buy time." | Flicker is Lighting's; the query uses the steady mean. They repel or distract; they never harm. | Suit / Item DB |

**A focused searchlight is louder (deliberate).** The light stimulus Attention reads is output times reach (D.12) and is blind to the beam's shape, so the narrow beam (reach 384, the one that reveals the least of a room) reads about 2.3 times louder than the fan (reach 168). That is the intended trade, not an accident of the formula: a tight, far-reaching searchlight is a bright, far-carrying thing to be, and the loadout choice is *reach and focus against being heard by the web*, not a free pick of the quietest cone. No per-shape weight column exists in the MVP. *Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20) (D25).* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**What this GDD does not promise (anti-pillar guard rails).**
1. **No exposure readout of any shape.** No gauge, gem, icon, overlay, light-level number or "visibility" text in gameplay. The only gameplay surface is a prompt that is present or absent. The Debug Overlay (`DIAGNOSTIC`) may draw cones, segments and subject exposure and is absent from release.
2. **The beam reveals; it never repels or harms.** No beam stuns, burns, repels or damages a horror or a crewmate (rejected: the Alan Wake light-as-weapon pattern; anti-pillar "not a combat game"). Horrors may read exposure (`lux`, `emitters_near`) to decide behaviour (`lit_by` is deferred to VS-A, EM-24) — that is Horror Entities' — and being revealed is the beam's whole effect. VS-A flares, torches and decoys may repel or distract (anti-pillar "tools may repel"); that behaviour is Horror's and is never a damage channel. *(Horror Entities is now designed and re-runs this GDD's no-occluder, no-emission criterion as AC-HE-67; status refreshed 2026-10-27 — design review.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
3. **No light progression.** No beam upgrades, brightness levels or unlocks. Shape is a Suit loadout fact; better light comes from salvage, if at all.
4. **No mid-run shape switching and no brightness dial.** On and off is the only beam choice.
5. **Shining a beam on a crewmate has no mechanical effect** (anti-PvP): it does not blind, mark or harm; in VS-B it is only a truth check.
6. **Fixtures are placed by welding sections, never freeform** (anti-builder). No player-placed lamps in the MVP; glowsticks are the only player-placed light, and they are consumed.
7. **No stealth score or "hidden" status.** Going dark lowers a stimulus Attention reads; nothing tells the player they are hidden.
8. **Darkness is never punished by this system.** It neither drains, damages nor penalises; the dark only withholds agency (you cannot act on what is unlit). Any sanity cost of darkness is a **hard constraint on the Sanity & Hallucination Rules GDD, not a warning** (creative-director, CD-GDD-ALIGN: darkness never costs sanity, because go-dark collapses as a strategy, and P3's restraint with it, if darkness costs more than the exposure it saves; Open Question 4).
9. **No light for free.** No ambient reveal radius around the player; the avatar is a light source only through its beam. The one near-field exception is the beam's own foot pool (`BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` 24, EM-6): it exists only while the beam is on, is drawn, and reaches 24 px from the feet, so an object underfoot is not a broken prompt. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Every rule below is a sim rule of the Emitter State owner module (`src/sim/emitter/`), stated so that an implementer can write it and a tester can falsify it. Registry constants are cited by their registered name (`TICK_RATE` 60, `TILE_PX` 48, `AIM_UNITS` 4096, `REACH_PX` 72, `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` 24, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4, `rng_derive`, `state_hash`, `hook_runs_this_tick`, `chest_point`, `power_allocation`, `served_pm`, and the Graph's `INRUSH_STEPS` 5 / `INRUSH_PM` 1500) and are never restated with another value; owner-GDD tuning rows that are not registry entries (the Controller's `AIM_SEND_THRESHOLD`, `AIM_SETTLE_TICKS`, `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN`) are cited by name the same way. Every number this GDD introduces has its own name and the tag "placeholder, owner X, confirmed by Y" (Tuning Knobs); a number another GDD owns is cited by that GDD's name for it. Where a rule records an alternative that was considered and rejected, the rejection is part of the rule: a later change must answer it. Markers: "(orchestrator ruling: ...)" records a point where the specialists disagreed and the rulings, or a pick, decided; "(provisional — X …)" marks an assumption about a counterpart's text that is not yet confirmed; as of 2026-10-27 every counterpart GDD is Status Designed, so a provisional tag now waits on a named counterpart sentence, not on a missing GDD. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)**

**EM-1 — One block, one hook, one truth.** Emitter State is exactly one persisted `SIM` state block, `emitter.state` (owner Emitter State; `class` SIM; `persist` true; `persist_scope` WORLD; `scope` SHARED; `repl_hint` SHARED_SUMMARY; `schema_version` 1; **`supported_versions` [1]; `added_in_format` 1** (Save/Load SL-15 items 2 and 7: the boot lints `SL_BOOT_NO_SUPPORTED_VERSIONS` and `SL_BOOT_NO_ADDED_IN_FORMAT` refuse a persisted block that declares no `supported_versions` containing its own current `schema_version`, or has no `added_in_format` entry in the format data file; because MVP death means restart from the last autosave, restore is the main recovery path and a block that cannot boot or pre-check would strand it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; `restore_after: [layout.state, graph.state, body.state, controller.state]` (`layout.state` is Layout's registered block and `body.state` is Ship Body's BDY-5 block, both designed; the Graph's `restore_after: [layout.state]` and the Controller's PC-14 are the precedents) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* — fixture anchors and occluding rectangles need the Layout, lamp nodes need the Graph, the beam's foot origin and frame need Body, and the chest point and aim need the Controller; the three-block list in the context brief omitted `body.state` and is superseded), registered by the module's composition-root call during BOOTING (kernel CR-6, CR-8). Exactly one step hook, `emitter.place` (divisor 1, offset 0, `after: [body.integrate, graph.propagate.failure]`; the two `after` edges carry `tolerates_stale` false (`body.integrate`) and true (`graph.propagate.failure`), ADR-0017 D3.1) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*, does all per-tick work: beam pose, output, lifecycle, the subject table. Its position after `body.integrate` is fixed by PC-15 and the registry comment on `hook_runs_this_tick` ("Controller/Body/Emitter 1"); the second entry, `graph.propagate.failure`, is this GDD's (the wording refresh owed to PC-15 is in Cross-References); no second Emitter hook exists in the MVP, because placement, lamp reads, fault glow and the subject table all need the same ordered reads. Hook ids are written exactly: `graph.propagate.power`, `graph.propagate.atmo`, `suit.vitals`, `controller.step`, `body.integrate`, `emitter.place`; the kernel has no alias concept and an unknown id or a cycle fails boot (CR-3), so the three Graph hook ids are always written with their phase suffix. The direct entry `after: graph.propagate.failure` puts `emitter.place`, on every Graph tick, after all three Graph hooks (`graph.propagate.power` → `graph.propagate.atmo` → `graph.propagate.failure`, Graph LS-8), so the hook reads the state the Graph run just produced — served power, node state after the failure phase and the descriptor list — and a snapshot taken at that tick and restored gives `validate` at the next tick the same subject table (kernel G4). **Terminology: "Graph tick"** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: under the Graph's baseline stagger (LS-8: power offset 0, atmo 2, failure 4) "Graph tick" in this GDD means two different ticks. (a) For input timing (`arm_tick` rounding, M14 consumed by phase 0, assumption A1, the D.2 examples `T_a` 1200, 126 and 132) it is the power-hook tick, `T mod 6 = 0`. (b) For reads (the descriptor list, node `state`, the fault-glow reconciliation, AC-43b / AC-61 / AC-62 "on the Graph tick of its descriptor") it is the failure-hook tick, `T mod 6 = 4`, on which `emitter.place` runs after `graph.propagate.failure` and sees that step's final state; `served_pm` (phase 2) changes on `T mod 6 = 0`. A glow therefore appears on the failure tick of the step in which its descriptor appears, never later. No numeric value or hook registration changes (`emitter.place` stays (1, 0) after `[body.integrate, graph.propagate.failure]`). The second chain, `graph.propagate.atmo` → `suit.vitals` (divisor 6) → `controller.step` → `body.integrate` → `emitter.place`, fixes where Suit's `force_off` lands (EM-10): `suit.vitals` is `after: graph.propagate.atmo` (Graph LS-8) and `controller.step` is `after: suit.vitals` (PC-15). That chain is **assumption A2, locked by PC-15 and LS-8; Suit must keep the id** `suit.vitals` (Suit & Equipment, designed, keeps it with `after: graph.propagate.atmo`: SE-8); the lamp latency of D.2 no longer depends on it. In `MIRROR` mode the hook never runs; the replica reads the replicated `SHARED_SUMMARY`, which in VS-B must carry the beam's origin, aim, shape and switch (derived values the host publishes) so a remote client can draw a crewmate's beam (an ask to Save/Load and Co-op). The module is scene-tree free, headless-testable and deterministic (same seed and command stream → identical `state_hash`).

**EM-2 — Vocabulary: the two `lit`s, kept verbatim.** The Controller locks `emitter.state.lit` as a *record field* (PC acceptance criteria test it on the seat's beam) and `lit(o)` as the *exposure query* (PC-6, PC D.9). Both names stay exactly as the Controller wrote them; renaming either breaks the Controller's tests. (a) **`emitter.state.lit`** — the record field: true iff the emitter's `output_pm > 0` (any kind). It means "this emitter is emitting". Suit's `suit.vitals` reads it on the seat's beam to drain charge; the Presenter draws a beam only when it is true. (b) **`lit(subject)`** — the query: the hysteresis-filtered comparison of a subject's action-gating light, `act_lux_pm`, against the threshold `LIT_ON_PM` / `LIT_OFF_PM` (EM-22). It means "this thing is lit enough to read and touch". Related terms: **`output_pm`** — an emitter's own steady brightness, integer 0..1000 permille; **`reach_px`** — an emitter's current outer radius in base px; **`lux_pm`** — a subject's total light, the saturating sum of every contribution at its anchor, integer 0..1000, read by Horror Entities (Attention reads the `emitters()` list, never `lux_pm`; Attention AT-4); **`act_lux_pm`** — the same sum restricted to emitters that gate actions (the BEACON does not); **`top_emitter_id`** — the emitter with the largest contribution to a subject (ties by ascending id). These two `lit`s are also defined in the Summary glossary line.

**EM-3 — Six emitter kinds, a closed list.** An emitter is one of BEAM, FIXTURE (two subkinds mark the **self-powered fixture class**, `node_ref` 0: EMERGENCY, the declared cryo-bay lamp, authored by Derelict Authoring DA-23 as `fixture_subkind_emergency`; and DOCK_STATUS, the small status glow on the docked pod's dock-room prop, one per dock room, authored by Derelict Authoring as `fixture_subkind_dock_status` *(name proposed here, for Derelict Authoring and Web Map to confirm)* — an ordinary lit fixture, not a further `act_gate` exception *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, which makes the pod console's lit gate always satisfied; every other fixture carries no subkind and is a Graph LOAD) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X2)*, NODE_LANTERN, DEPLOYABLE (subkind GLOWSTICK in the MVP; FLARE, TORCH, DECOY in VS-A), BEACON or FAULT_GLOW. A seventh kind is a schema bump (registry: `emitter_kind`). No record carries a `phantom`, `perceived`, `decoy` or `hallucinated` field of any kind (EM-29).

| Kind | Created by / when | Removed by | Switch | Power | Layer | `gates_actions` | Id |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BEAM | `create_seat_beam(player_id)` at boot, one per bound seat (MVP: 1; the table, replication hint and Lighting's shadow slots are sized for 4 in VS-B) | never — DEAD forces it off, the record stays | `toggle_beam` | suit charge (Suit's), not a Graph LOAD | dynamic | yes | counter |
| FIXTURE (ship lamp) | `instantiate_section_emitters(section_id)` inside Assembly / Web Map's atomic apply | `discard_section_emitters(section_id)` | `set_switch` | Graph LOAD via M14 `set_load_request` | static | yes | counter |
| FIXTURE, subkind DOCK_STATUS *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X2)* | same section mutator (a dock room's placement); one per dock room | same | **`set_dock_status` (E10) only**, called by Web Map & Transit in the apply that writes `pod_at` (on where the pod parks, off where it leaves); `set_switch` of either origin is rejected `NOT_SWITCHABLE` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | none: self-powered, `node_ref` 0, no M14 call, no ARMING, fixed `base_pm` (EM-11) | static | yes | counter |
| NODE_LANTERN (machinery glow) | same section mutator | same | none | derived from the node's running state; no M14 call | static | yes | counter |
| DEPLOYABLE / GLOWSTICK | `spawn_deployable` at the landing tick | `life_remaining_ticks` reaches 0; the end of a cap eviction's visible wane (EM-15, never an instant removal); section discard | none | none | static (its reach steps) | yes | counter |
| BEACON (alarm) | with its section (same mutator) | with its section | `set_beacon` (`SYSTEM`) | Graph LOAD via M14 when `node_ref` is set (MVP: set) | dynamic (it rotates) | **no** | counter |
| FAULT_GLOW | `emitter.place`, from the Graph's failure descriptors (EM-17) | descriptor gone; cap eviction of the lowest-`sev`, unprotected record only (EM-17: never one younger than `W_MIN_WARNING_S`) | none | none | static | yes | counter |

**EM-4 — Records, ids, and what is canonical.** Every emitter is a record with a `u32` id allocated from a counter stored inside `emitter.state` (kernel CR-8: the owning block allocates; the Controller does not), never reused, strictly ascending, so the newest light is the one with the higher id (2D Lighting E-G); every iteration in this module is in ascending id (or ascending subject ref), never in Dictionary order. Everything keyed to a player is keyed by `PlayerId`, never by `peer_id`. *Canonical* fields (snapshotted, hashed) are discrete facts with no other source; *derived* fields are rebuilt in `post_restore` and never hashed (EM-28).

| Field | Type | C / D | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | u32 | C | never reused; the counter `next_emitter_id` is canonical |
| `kind`, `subkind` | enum | C | EM-3 |
| `owner_ref` | PlayerId / Graph node id / section id | C | beam: PlayerId; lamps: the Graph node; deployable: the thrower's PlayerId (informational) |
| `section_id` | u32 | C (static kinds, deployables, FAULT_GLOW); **D for the BEAM** | the Graph's `section_of` (EM-17 for FAULT_GLOW); a BEAM's is rewritten every tick in W0 from Body's `actor_section(actor_id)` (EM-21) and never hashed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `body_id` | u32 | **D for EVERY record** (BEAM, FIXTURE, NODE_LANTERN, DEPLOYABLE, BEACON, FAULT_GLOW) | the frame the emitter lives in (EM-30). Never stored, never hashed. Static kinds, deployables, beacon and FAULT_GLOW: Layout `body_of(section_id)`, derived at creation, in `post_restore`, and re-read in W0 (EM-23) whenever `static_version(section_id)` of the record's section changed since the last tick; BEAM: Body `body_of_actor(actor_id)` rewritten every tick in W0 (unchanged), so it follows its actor across a weld, a cut or a pod hop. A weld (Layout LY-16) or cut (LY-17) therefore moves every record to its new body in the tick the Layout change applies, with no Emitter mutator call. `section_id` is the canonical fact; `body_id` is a function of it and of Layout state, so by this table's own definition it is derived. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `anchor_px` | int2, **section-local** base px | C | static kinds and FAULT_GLOW in Layout's frame (LY-3 c); a landed deployable's position is converted once at spawn by E3 (which resolves the section from the landing position) to the same frame; the derived `origin_px` composes `section_offset(section_id)` into the body frame (EM-30); unused for the beam *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `shape_id` | enum | C | a row of the shape table (EM-8) |
| `base_pm` | int 0..1000 | C | from the module def's `emits`, `bind_beam`, or `BEAM_BASE_PM` |
| `switch_on` | bool | C | beam, fixture, beacon |
| `last_toggle_tick` | int | C | BEAM and FIXTURE (not set for other kinds): the tick of the last **realised** switch transition, −1 until the first one. For the BEAM: the tick at which the last accepted `toggle_beam` applied. For a FIXTURE: the commit tick of the last realised change of the switch gate `switch_on ∧ armed`, i.e. the apply tick of an OFF that darkened a lit or armed lamp's gate, or the `arm_tick` at which an ON was realised (written by `emitter.place` on that tick); an OFF applied while the lamp is still ARMING cancels an ON that never happened and writes nothing (EM-12); an ON that arms into STARVED (a FAILED, DESTROYED or dead-bus node) is still a realised change of the gate and writes `arm_tick`, and so does the E1 initial ON of an authored-on lamp, whose `arm_tick` is, for an INIT-scope creation, the first RUNNING tick on which the power hook runs (D.2; ADR-0017 D5.2) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)* (EM-5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* Read by `toggle_beam.validate` (`TOO_SOON`) and `set_switch.validate` (`COOLDOWN`) for the commit-spacing guard (EM-7, D.2b); never written by `force_off` or the DEAD tick *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; extended to fixtures 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D32)* |
| `arm_tick` | int | C | lamp kinds: the tick the lamp becomes lit after `set_switch` (EM-12) |
| `node_ref` | u32 or 0 | C | fixture, beacon: the Graph LOAD node; lantern: its source node |
| `life_remaining_ticks`, `life_total_ticks` | int | C | deployables, else -1 |
| `evict_tick` | int | C | deployables only: the tick a cap eviction began this stick's visible wane (EM-15, D.9b), else -1; `reach_at_evict` (int px) is stored beside it |
| `glow_key`, `sev`, `placed_tick` | int | C | FAULT_GLOW only: the descriptor id and severity the record stands for, and the tick the record was created (the age that protects it from eviction, EM-17; ids give order, not age) |
| `beacon_phase`, `beacon_dir` | int, ±1 | C | authored data |
| `priority` | u8 | C | deterministic cut order for Lighting (EM-26); default by kind in Tuning Knobs |
| `hue_class` | token | C (from def) | BEAM, FIXTURE, CHEMLIGHT, BEACON, LANTERN_O2, LANTERN_POWER, LANTERN_COOLANT, LANTERN_FUEL, LANTERN_INTEGRITY, FAULT_* (one per Failure & Trail glow-table row: FAULT_EMBER, FAULT_ARC, FAULT_O2 in the MVP); a lantern def carries its loop's token (the shape key stays `LANTERN`, shape row 9; only the hue token is per-loop); the token-to-hex map is registry `hue_class_token_map` (Lighting-owned; an unknown token is Lighting's to handle under that entry's rule); (VS-A) FLARE, DECOY |
| `origin_px`, `aim_units`, `half_angle_units`, `reach_px`, `output_pm` | ints | D | rebuilt every tick by `emitter.place` |
| `lit` | bool | D | `output_pm > 0` (EM-2) |

**EM-5 — Nobody writes emitters directly.** Records are created, changed and destroyed only through the published mutators below, each callable inside a kernel `WriteScope` (CR-9) by its declared caller, and by `emitter.place` itself for the two derived kinds. Lighting, Attention, Horror, the Perception Filter and every presentation subscriber never create, move, switch or delete an emitter (kernel C.3 row 7: "Lighting never writes emitters"). *Class A* mutators are infallible applies inside a caller's validated command; *Class B* mutators clamp and never reject, return `void` and report `OK`, `REFUSED_CAP`, `NO_SECTION`, `DUPLICATE`, `OVER_CAP` or `OVER_MOVING_CAP` in the caller-owned `EmitterOutcome` (`out.code`; an unknown id is a no-op that sets `out.code`, the Graph's precedent LS-18 adapted; ADR-0017 D3.4) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*. A creation mutator fills every derived field of the new record (origin = anchor, `output_pm` and `reach_px` by EM-11 / EM-14 / EM-15 / EM-16, `lit` from `output_pm`) so a record created in tick T is complete in the read view from P4 of T even when the creating hook runs after `emitter.place`; only the *subject table* trails (a new emitter enters exposure evaluation at the first `emitter.place` run at or after its creation, at most one tick later, inside PC-5's committed-versus-drawn tolerance).

| # | Mutator | Signature | Class | Caller (declared) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | `instantiate_section_emitters` | `(section_id)` | A | Web Map & Transit, **at `init_world` only** (WM-7 step 2, once per section, after Layout LM1 and Graph M1 have built the section's nodes). Web Map builds nothing after `init_world` (WM-24); a hop, weld and cut call no Emitter mutator *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | creates the section's fixtures, lanterns and beacon from Derelict Authoring placements and Item DB `emits` defs; **each placement carries an authored `switch_on`** (default false): a Graph-powered lamp or beacon authored on is created ARMING with M14 queued and `arm_tick` set (D.2 with `T_a` = the creation tick, or the first RUNNING tick for an INIT-scope creation), a self-powered EMERGENCY authored on is created ON, a DOCK_STATUS glow is created OFF whatever the authoring says; E1 reads no other system's state (`pod_at` included); unknown section → no-op *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| E2 | `discard_section_emitters` | `(section_id)` | A | **No MVP caller** (Web Map discards nothing at the MVP: a derelict goes dormant and is never discarded, WM-24); reserved for VS-A section discard and for the tests *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | also removes that section's deployables and unregisters that section's OBJECT subjects |
| E3 | `spawn_deployable` | `(scope, kind, body_id, pos_x, pos_y, owner, life_ticks, out)` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)* | B | Interaction's landing-tick hook (PC D.16) | `life_ticks` is the life *remaining at landing* (EM-15); over `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` puts the lowest-id non-evicting deployable of the same kind into its visible evict-wane (EM-15, D.9b), never an instant removal; a position in a dormant, unloaded or unknown section is a no-op that reports `NO_SECTION`; at `MAX_EMITTERS` the spawn is refused and reports `REFUSED_CAP` (EM-24); the mutator returns `void` and the position is in **body-frame** px, `body_id` 0 deriving the body from `owner` with `BodyView.body_of_actor` (ADR-0017 D3.4, CT-46) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)* |
| E4 | `bind_beam` | `(player_id, shape_id)` | A | once, by the host session owner at new game through the CR-7 boot step (Suit & Equipment's loadout, SE-8) | the only way a beam's shape changes |
| E5 | `force_off` | `(beam_id)` | B | Suit's `suit.vitals` at charge zero; Emitter itself at DEAD | sets `switch_on` false |
| E6 | `register_subject` | `(ref, kind, anchor_px, section_id)` | B | Interaction, Horror, loot spawners, the actor boot | returns `void`; `out.code` is `OK`, `DUPLICATE` (a repeat registration is idempotent, not a defect), `OVER_CAP` (over `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS`), `OVER_MOVING_CAP` (over `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS`) or `NO_SECTION` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)* |
| E7 | `unregister_subject` | `(ref)` | B | the registrant | |
| E8 | `set_subject_anchor` | `(ref, anchor_px)` | B | the owner of a non-actor moving subject (Horror's hook) | lands at the next `emitter.place`; Horror's hook is `after: emitter.place`, so its move shows with a one-tick lag (accepted) |
| E9 | `create_seat_beam` | `(player_id) -> beam_id` | A | the new-game LOADING step (the Emitter's `init_world` step, or the session owner's new-game step under the same `WriteScope`, before the kernel's LOADING rebuild) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | allocates the beam id from the block counter, initialises `last_toggle_tick` to −1 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* and returns it; the kernel then binds it from `beam_bindings()` (EM-7); the mutator itself writes no kernel data |
| E10 | `set_dock_status` | `(section_id, on)` | A (infallible apply inside Web Map's own apply, as E1; a defect is not a rejection) | Web Map & Transit, inside the apply that writes `pod_at`: **on** for the dock room the pod parks at (`init_world` step 7 for the home dock, `pod_settle`, the T5 reversal), **off** for the dock room the pod leaves (the departure apply) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | sets `switch_on` of the one DOCK_STATUS glow in `section_id` and recomputes that record's `output_pm` and `lit` at once, so the glow is complete in the read view from P4 of the same tick; self-powered, so no M14 call and no ARMING; **no commit-spacing guard and no `last_toggle_tick` write**: the pod's own timing (a hop leg is `HOP_LEG_TICKS` plus `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS`, Web Map's, many seconds) spaces the transitions far beyond `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS`, a rejection could not be observed by an atomic apply, and a status glow is not the strobe path; a same-state call changes nothing; an unknown section or one with no DOCK_STATUS glow is a lint-prevented defect: the call changes nothing, returns OK with `out.code` `NO_DOCK_GLOW` (a defect must not fault the caller's whole apply; ADR-0017 D3.4) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*, `emitter_dock_status_missing` increments (release telemetry) and `FakeDiagnostics.assert_reports` holds the code in a debug build (the authoring lint makes it unreachable; E1 has the same shape for an unknown section) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

**EM-6 — The beam is a chest emitter.** For the seat's BEAM the *apex* and the *reach* are measured from the chest point read through `ControllerView.get_chest_x` / `get_chest_y`, computed at read time from the same tick's `pos_curr` plus the frozen `CHEST_POINT` table (`pos_curr + CHEST_POINT[sheet][anim] (.) mirror`, `|x| <= 2` px, registry `chest_point`; PC D.12), with `get_aim_angle` and `get_loco_state`; there is no stored `chest_sim` (ADR-0017 D5.3, CT-28) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*, and the *axis* is the committed `aim_angle` (1/4096 turn, `AIM_UNITS` 4096; 0 = E, counter-clockwise, spawn aim 3072 = S). Both are **reads** of `controller.state` inside `emitter.place`, never commands: aim is not an Emitter command (the kernel row's "set/toggle/aim commands" wording is refreshed in Cross-References). The tool-grip anchor `HAND_POINT` is lightless and is never read by this block, and no rule anywhere names it an origin. The beam follows *aim*, not facing: facing diverges from aim while running (PC-4 rule 3). The drawn cone and the simulated cone may diverge by up to the PC-5 accepted tolerance (≤ 2.8°, ≤ 1 tick; the Controller's `AIM_SEND_THRESHOLD` / `AIM_SETTLE_TICKS` knobs widen it to 3 ticks), which is why the agreement test (design test 1) is stated at rest. **Occlusion starts at the foot, not the chest** *(orchestrator ruling: the foot-origin rule of the technical-director over the systems-designer's sanitised walk-back, because `pos_curr` is outside every footprint by construction — `LocomotionStep` keeps the avatar's box out of them — and is free of the 2 px mirror jitter; the walk-back remains the documented alternative, ADR candidate 5)*: `CHEST_POINT.y` is -45..-25 px (`h` = −y, 25..45), so with a 24 px wall footprint the chest point stands *inside* the footprint whenever the feet are `s` px south of the first legal row in front of a north wall (`s` = 0 on that row, LY-8 b) with `h − 24 ≤ s ≤ h − 1`: the band is **1–44 px** over all animation rows (10–33 px for the walk row at `h` 34 — the first draft's "10-34" was one row's band, not the range). Walls east or west of the avatar cost at most the 4 px of chest-x offset. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The cone's apex, reach and distance are therefore measured from the chest, while the occluder segment to a subject starts at the foot origin (`pos_curr`, `beam_pose`). Other emitter kinds use their floor-plane anchor for both. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **What ships for each Lighting-spike outcome** (2D Lighting LO-10; its spike step 3b is a **pre-implementation gate for ADR candidate 5**, not a later check): outcome **B** (the light's `offset` moves the apex without moving the shadow origin) — the foot-origin rule ships as written and Lighting draws the shadow from the foot; outcome **A** (the shadow originates at the chest) with AC-57 passing — the foot-origin rule ships and the residual discrepancy (≤ 2 base px) is recorded; outcome A with AC-57 failing (a beam that renders black, or leaks through the wall, while the sim reports the opposite) — outcome **D** ships: ADR 5 adopts the walk-back (the sim occludes from the chest, walked back to the first clear point on the segment to the foot), D.4, EM-20 g and AC-10 are re-derived, and nothing else in this GDD changes. Until the spike reports, the foot-origin rule is the working rule (ADR-0018 records outcome A as a control predicted to fail and B as the design of record) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0018 sync)* and the worst case — a beam that renders black while the sim reports it lit, which would happen every time a player walks up to a wall — is an open risk (Open Question 5). **Near-field is aim-dependent, and the ruled answer is a drawn foot pool, not a control tax** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; ruled 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D24)*: an object between the lamp and the feet in screen-y is behind the apex when the player faces up-screen; the cone is honest about it (nothing in the drawn cone reaches it either) and the sim adds no hidden compensation. The first draft called this "a skill expression"; the systems-designer, endorsed by the creative-director, reads it as a **control tax caused by the chest-lamp decision**: an object at the player's feet, or one tile to the side, is dark in every shape in any unlit room even though it is inside Interaction's `REACH_PX` 72, and it will read as a broken prompt, not as horror. P1 is preserved if the spill is *drawn*; the price is a small loss of P3 darkness right at the body. **Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions):** `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` ships at **24 px** (range 0–48; 0 switches the spill off) with `BEAM_SPILL_PM` 600 registered, as a dim, drawn, honest pool that gates actions only within its own actionable radius `r_lit_px` of the feet (18 px at the defaults, D.7 chain) and contributes minimally to exposure; Interaction's `REACH_PX` 72 is a different, larger distance (the hand's reach) and the spill does not light everything inside it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; it is validated by the *dark-at-feet* metric of the first playtest (the playtest stub's placeholder decision line is 5 % of lit-beam ticks; Open Question 17). The user's chest-lamp decision stands unchanged: the lamp is still on the chest and still the only light that follows the player; the spill is a small, honest, drawn pool at the feet. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The mechanics were fixed ahead of the ruling so that the ruling is one data value: the spill is one extra SOFT, omnidirectional, non-shadow contribution of the BEAM record, centred on the foot origin (`occ_origin`), of reach `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` and output `BEAM_SPILL_PM` (600, range 150–1000, owner game-designer; registered), present only while the beam is on (the beam record's `switch_on`, so the spill flips with the beam and adds no transition of its own to the commit-spacing guard), tested with the ordinary radius, occlusion and clearance rules, added to the `act_lux_pm` and `lux_pm` of objects (never to the owner's ACTOR self-lux, EM-21) and **excluded from `section_emission`** (D.12 counts the beam's cone term alone, so the spill adds no Attention load). At 24 px and 600 permille its actionable radius `r_lit_px` is **18 px, read from the D.7 integer chain** (the retired closed form ⌊24 × 450 / 600⌋ agrees at this one point and is not used; at d = 18, f = ⌊1000 × 6 / 24⌋ = 250 and c = ⌊600 × 250 / 1000⌋ = 150 = `LIT_ON_PM`; at d = 19, f = 208 and c = 124 < 150): it covers an object at the feet and not one a full tile (48 px) to the side; **no legal setting reaches a full tile**: at the maximum radius 48 and the maximum output 1000 the chain gives `r_lit_px` 40 (at d = 40, f = 166 and c = 166; at d = 41, f = 145 and c = 145), so "48 px to the side" is dark by design at every value in range *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **Subject kinds it applies to:** every registered subject kind except the owner's own ACTOR (EM-21 keeps the owner's self-lux at the beam's apex value): OBJECT, ITEM, a second seat's ACTOR (VS-B) and HORROR, each through the ordinary radius, occlusion and clearance rules; a horror within the spill's radius of the player's feet therefore reads lit (`lux_pm` up to 600), which is honest exposure and is not a special case (AC-10c). **The spill is published to Lighting** through the read-view accessor `beam_spill(beam_id)` (EM-26), not through `emitters()`, so that the sim edge and the drawn edge can be proved equal and so that Attention's list never counts it. It is `light_shape` row 11 `BEAM_SPILL` (a registry entry this GDD owns; see EM-8): a **conditional** row, present in the shape table iff `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` > 0, and it needs a Lighting texture and AC-10b. A non-zero radius must also satisfy I-REACH (at least `REACH_MIN_PX` 24), so the effective range is 0 (off) or 24–48. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**EM-7 — The beam changes only on committed state.** `toggle_beam` is owned by Emitter State and fixed by the Controller (PC-5, PC-2): `actor` = the seat's beam emitter id, `actor_binding OWNED_BY_ISSUER`, `actor_source = emitter.state` (kernel CR-7: the authority check keys `(emitter.state, beam_id)`, so a beam id never matches a Body actor id or a deployable's emitter id held under another key) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, `coalesce NONE`, `max_age_ticks` 120, origin `PLAYER`. The payload carries no state (it is a toggle: the `beam_toggle` press flips `switch_on`), so a rejected or duplicated command can never desynchronise the intent. Its apply flips `switch_on` in P2 of tick T; `emitter.place` in P3 of the same tick computes `output_pm`; the committed light therefore changes in the tick the toggle applies, with zero internal latency. The click and the hand's switch animation pre-play immediately (Presenter); **the light changes only on committed state and the Presenter never predicts beam state** (PC-5), because the beam is the human-channel anchor and may never lie, not even for one flicker. The seat's beam id is allocated by E9 inside the new-game LOADING (the Emitter's `init_world` step, or the session owner's new-game step under the same `WriteScope`, before the kernel's rebuild); the Emitter registers a **kernel binding source** at BOOTING (kernel CR-7): `beam_bindings()`, a flat `PackedInt64Array` `[player_id, beam_id, ...]` in ascending `player_id` delivered by the descriptor's `binding_source` (ADR-0017 D3.5, ADR-0016), a pure read of `emitter.state`; the kernel's LOADING rebuild binds each pair after every `post_restore()` (new game included), which is what lets `toggle_beam` carry `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`. The binding table is not saved (CR-7) and is rebuilt from `beam_bindings()` after every restore (EM-28). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* *Alternative considered:* the Controller allocates the beam id — rejected (CR-8). **Strobe protection is a sim guard, owned here** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; this supersedes the first-draft orchestrator ruling that routed the whole concern to the Controller's Adapter)*: the Adapter's debounce (`BEAM_TOGGLE_MIN_INTERVAL_TICKS`, owner Player Controller & Input, registry, PC-5) covers only the local human submitter, and PC-5 and Controller Open Question 18 both state that the sim-side guard "is Emitter State's to add"; with no guard here a replayed, scripted or remote submitter bypasses it and Suit SE-35's drain-sampling protection (the six-tick window) and the WCAG 2.3.1 limit rest on one client. The beam record therefore carries a canonical `last_toggle_tick` (EM-4), and `toggle_beam.validate` rejects `TOO_SOON` when `last_toggle_tick ≥ 0` and `T − last_toggle_tick < SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` (12 commit ticks, owned by this GDD, derived in D.2b; it replaces the first draft's citation of the Adapter's `BEAM_TOGGLE_MIN_INTERVAL_TICKS` as the sim's threshold, and the Adapter constant now sits at or above it); the apply of an accepted toggle writes `last_toggle_tick = T`. *Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20) (D32):* the same **commit-spacing rule** applies to `set_switch` for every submitter and every origin (the DOCK_STATUS glow is switched by E10, not by `set_switch`, and is exempt *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), see below. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The first-draft rationale ("a sim-side `COOLDOWN` would be wall-clock-shaped") was wrong: the field is counted in ticks, is canonical, and is therefore deterministic and save-equivalent. The guard applies in both directions (a turn-OFF inside the window is also `TOO_SOON`; the rule that a turn-OFF is never rejected for charge, EM-10, is about charge, not rate); `force_off` (Suit, the DEAD tick) is not a `toggle_beam` and neither reads nor writes the field. The validate order is `NO_SUCH_EMITTER`, `NOT_ACTIONABLE`, `TOO_SOON`, `CHARGE_EMPTY`. An Adapter-compliant stream is never rejected **only if** `BEAM_TOGGLE_MIN_INTERVAL_TICKS` is at least `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS`: the Adapter's registry value is 10 today, so until the Player Controller raises it to 12 (its range 10–20 allows it; 2D Lighting Ask E-E proposes the same) a human stream pressing every 10 ticks has every second press refused `TOO_SOON` (no light change, no charge drain); this is listed under I-SPACING (D.15) and AC-90b, not hidden. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Commit spacing for fixtures (`set_switch`), every submitter and origin** *(user rulings: D32; default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20). 2D Lighting Ask E-E, LO-22 assigned the guard to this GDD; no exception to "the picture never disagrees with `lit`" is granted to Lighting, whose last-resort clamp may not retime a committed switch)*: `set_switch.validate` rejects with `COOLDOWN` when `last_toggle_tick ≥ 0` and the **projected commit tick** lies fewer than `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` after it. The projected commit tick is the apply tick `T_a` for an OFF that would darken a lit or armed lamp, and `arm_tick` of D.2 for an ON (a deterministic function of `T_a`, EM-12). Rules: (1) an OFF applied while the lamp is still ARMING cancels an ON that never became visible and is never refused (it is not a realised transition, EM-12, AC-80); (2) the guard measures *realised switch transitions only*: a brownout, a node failure or `served_pm` falling to 0 changes `output_pm` but is the world's doing, not the submitter's, and writes nothing; (3) the guard applies to `PLAYER` and `SYSTEM` origin alike; the MVP has **two `SYSTEM` submitters in this area and neither is a `set_switch` submitter**: Attention's breach machine submits `set_beacon` (which carries no per-emitter guard, below) and Web Map & Transit switches the DOCK_STATUS glow through the guard-free mutator E10, so a refused `COOLDOWN` is only ever seen by the `PLAYER` path at the MVP (the `SYSTEM` form of `set_switch` is kept for VS-A, where a submitter receives `COOLDOWN` like anyone and may resubmit on a later tick) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; (4) a same-state `SYSTEM` `set_switch` is accepted and changes nothing (below) and is not a transition; (5) `toggle_beam` keeps its domain code `TOO_SOON` (the Controller's tests and AC-90 name it), `set_switch` uses `COOLDOWN`; both are this GDD's codes under `VALIDATION_FAILED(domain_code)`. **Consequence for the player's tap path** (stated so that no one assumes Interaction's `TAP_REARM_TICKS` 12 covers it): the tap guard spaces *submissions* by 12 ticks, while this guard spaces *commits*, and an ON commits at `arm_tick`, up to 5 ticks after its apply; so an ON tapped at tick 1201 (commit 1206) followed by an OFF tapped at 1213 (the first tap `TAP_REARM_TICKS` allows) commits at 1213, only 7 ticks after 1206, and is refused `COOLDOWN` until a tap that commits at 1218 or later (12 after 1206, subject to Interaction's own re-arm). The lamp never flickers; the refused tap changes nothing in the light, plays Interaction's failed-action cue (D.2b, delegated ruling G2) and the player taps again *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `COOLDOWN` is a domain code, not a gameplay message (no HUD text). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **Lighting's flash governor is never asked to delay a committed beam or fixture flip:** at 12-tick spacing any rolling half-open 60-tick window holds at most ⌈60 / 12⌉ = 5 realised transitions, which the registry `flash_count` definition counts as ⌈5 / 2⌉ = 3 flashes (a flash is a pair of opposing transitions, counted ⌈n / 2⌉, the unit ruled 2026-10-27, X9), equal to `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3, so the governor's defect counter (2D Lighting LO-22, Ask E-E) stays at 0 with a transition of margin (the old 10-tick floor admitted six, three flashes with none, and seven edges, four flashes, in a window closed at both ends). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* `TOO_SOON`, `NOT_ACTIONABLE` and `NO_SUCH_EMITTER` are this GDD's own domain codes under the kernel's `VALIDATION_FAILED(domain_code)` namespace. Fixture flips are now bounded here by the commit-spacing guard above (every origin), in addition to Interaction's `TAP_REARM_TICKS` (IA-21, registry, which keeps the *submission* rate) and the Graph pulse that delays every switch-ON (EM-12); `set_beacon` carries no per-emitter guard here and Attention's breach machine, its only submitter, rate-limits itself (the residual of Open Question 25). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

| Command | Payload | Origin | `actor` / `actor_binding` | `coalesce` | `max_age_ticks` | Rejection reasons | Submitter |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `toggle_beam` | none (toggle) | `PLAYER` | the seat's beam id / `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`, `actor_source` `emitter.state` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | `NONE` | 120 (locked, PC-5) | `CHARGE_EMPTY` (turn-ON only, from Suit's charge view), `NOT_ACTIONABLE` (`loco_state` in STAGGERED, DOWNED, DEAD — a defensive double of the Controller's input-ignore, PC-9), `NO_SUCH_EMITTER`, `TOO_SOON` (both directions: `T − last_toggle_tick < SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS`, the sim-side strobe guard) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | the Controller's Input Adapter |
| `set_switch` | `PLAYER`: `{emitter_id}`, a toggle against the true `switch_on` (as `toggle_beam`, EM-7; no believed state is carried); `SYSTEM`: `{emitter_id, on}` | `PLAYER` (an `interact`-declared verb, PC-6) or `SYSTEM` | the acting avatar / `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`, `actor_source` `body.state`, for `PLAYER`; none for `SYSTEM` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | `NONE` (a switch is a discrete tap; the kernel's vocabulary is `NONE` / `LATEST_PER_ACTOR_PER_TICK`) | 120 (PC-2's value for action commands) | `NO_SUCH_EMITTER`, `NOT_SWITCHABLE` (kind is not FIXTURE; or **any** `set_switch` on a DOCK_STATUS fixture, whose switch belongs to Web Map's E10 `set_dock_status`, called where `pod_at` is written) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X2)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `COOLDOWN` (the projected commit tick lies fewer than `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` after `last_toggle_tick`; every origin; an OFF during ARMING is exempt; EM-7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D32)*, `NOT_LIVE` (**`SYSTEM` origin only**; the code keeps its name because Interaction and Attention cite it, but it means *the node is not structurally present* (defined under the table), never EM-13's output predicate 'live' *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; a `node_ref` 0 self-powered fixture has no node and is never `NOT_LIVE`; for every other fixture — Interaction IA-11(a) forbids a PLAYER-origin `validate` from reading `node().state`, `served_pm` or any health field, so a `PLAYER` `set_switch` never rejects on node state: it is accepted and flips `switch_on`, and on a structurally gone node (missing, tombstoned, dormant, `4 SALVAGED` or `5 ABSENT`) the M14 call is a no-op and the lamp stays dark by EM-13 with the switch retained, which gives the player no oracle; for `SYSTEM` origin the structural check reads `node(id).state`, never `served_pm`; a `2 FAILED` or `3 DESTROYED` node, or a dead bus with a live node, **accepts** the switch, so a player can switch a failed lamp off before it is repaired and it relights on repair only if left on, EM-13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Interaction's declared verb; the PLAYER `set_switch` validate calls Interaction's pure predicate `InteractionView.verb_precondition(actor, subject_ref, verb)` after `NOT_SWITCHABLE` and before `COOLDOWN`, which returns `NOT_LIT`, `OUT_OF_REACH` or `NOT_ACTIONABLE` (Interaction System IA-36/IA-37; ADR-0017 D3.2a, CT-47) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)* |
| `set_beacon` | `{emitter_id, on}` | `SYSTEM` only | none | `NONE` | 120 (placeholder, owner technical-director, confirmed by Attention) | `NO_SUCH_EMITTER`, `NOT_BEACON`, `NOT_LIVE` (node not structurally present only, as for `set_switch`; a live node on a dead bus — `served_pm` 0 — **accepts** the command and the beacon goes STARVED and dark, EM-13; Attention E-30 expects `NOT_LIVE` for a dead bus and must be corrected) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Attention's breach machine (designed, `attention-system.md` AT-18) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

Same-state switches are accepted and change nothing (no dirty key); every command reaches exactly one terminal result (CR-5).

**Two predicates, two names** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the review found one word, 'live', naming two sets. (a) **Structurally present** is the *command-validation* predicate, the only one `set_switch` and `set_beacon` `validate` may use (and, for a `PLAYER` origin, it is *not even evaluated*: Interaction IA-11(a)): the node exists in the Graph's read view, is not dormant or tombstoned, and its state is neither `4 SALVAGED` nor `5 ABSENT`. A `SYSTEM`-origin command whose node fails it is rejected `NOT_LIVE`. (b) **Live** is EM-13's *output* predicate only: structurally present **and** state not `2 FAILED` or `3 DESTROYED`; a not-live lamp has `output_pm` 0 and its record and switch are untouched. The commands are decided by (a), the light by (b). One row per node state (the AC-33 table):

| Node state at apply | `SYSTEM` `set_switch` / `set_beacon` | `PLAYER` `set_switch` | M14 `set_load_request` | `output_pm` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| missing (unknown `node_ref`), tombstoned, dormant | `NOT_LIVE` | accepted, flips `switch_on` | no-op | 0, switch retained |
| `4 SALVAGED`, `5 ABSENT` | `NOT_LIVE` | accepted, flips `switch_on` | no-op | 0, switch retained |
| `2 FAILED`, `3 DESTROYED` | accepted, flips `switch_on` | accepted | queued, as for a healthy node | 0 (STARVED) until repair, then lit if left on |
| `0 OK` / `1 DEGRADED` on a dead bus (`served_pm` 0) | accepted | accepted | queued | 0 (STARVED) until power returns |
| `0 OK` / `1 DEGRADED`, served | accepted | accepted | queued | by D.1 |


**EM-8 — Beam shape is loadout, not input; the shape table is data this GDD owns.** The four shapes (wide, narrow, split, fan) are a per-player Suit & Equipment fact set once, by the host session owner at new game through the CR-7 boot step, by `bind_beam(player_id, shape_id)` (E4). There is no "change shape" action and no Emitter command for it (PC-5), no mid-run switching and no brightness dial. The shape table below is **data owned by this GDD as the carrier of the art bible 8.3 rows** (registry: `light_shape`) so that the queried edge and the drawn edge cannot drift: Lighting's textures and cone geometry derive from it, with a build lint that the rendered reach equals the table reach ±1 px for hard-edged rows (EM-26). The art-bible columns are carried **verbatim** in degrees and tiles; the integer encodings are this GDD's derivations, tagged. Every row: *placeholder, owner art bible 8.3 / game-designer, confirmed by the Lighting spike (art bible 8.12 step 2)*. The split beam's blind spot is its identity: two cones with a dark wedge that never closes — the one shape that can never see straight ahead. That is a loadout personality, not a bug, and no one should "fix" it.

| `shape_id` | Light (art bible 8.3) | Half-angle (verbatim) | `half_angle_units` | `COS2_Q28` | Reach, tiles / px (verbatim) | Curve class | Geometry | Shadow-casting (Lighting's, informational) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 `BEAM_WIDE` | Wide cone | 30° | 341 (29.97°) | 201,441,249 | 6 / 288 | HARD | one cone | yes |
| 2 `BEAM_NARROW` | Narrow cone | 12° | 137 (12.04°) | 256,768,576 | 8 / 384 | HARD | one cone | yes |
| 3 `BEAM_SPLIT` | Split (twin) | 10° each | `SPLIT_HALF_UNITS` 114 (10.02°) each (the split row's `half_angle_units`) | 260,305,956 | 7 / 336 | HARD | two cones, axes `aim ± 206` units (18.11°; centre-to-centre 36.2°); the dark wedge is 184 units = 16.17° ≥ the art bible's 16°, and never closes | yes |
| 4 `BEAM_FAN` | Broad fan | 60° | 683 (60.03°) | 66,994,225 | 3.5 / 168 | HARD | one short wide cone ("width at the rim about 2× reach" is consistent with 60° — the chord is 1.73 R — and is carried as a placeholder) | yes |
| 5 `POOL_DEPLOYABLE` | Deployable pool (flare, torch, decoy), VS-A | 360° | — | — | 2.5 / 120 | SOFT | omnidirectional; flickers (presentation) | nearest in view only |
| 6 `POOL_GLOWSTICK` | Glowstick pool | 360° | — | — | 2 / 96 at full life (Item DB `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES`) | SOFT | omnidirectional; steady, then waning (EM-15) | none by default |
| 7 `FIXTURE` | Fixture | 360° | — | — | 3 / 144 | SOFT | static housing; never flickers (the cryo-bay gutter is Lighting's) | none (the cryo-bay lamp may take the sixth slot — Lighting's) |
| 8 `BEACON` | Alarm beacon | 20° | 228 (20.04°) | 236,913,664 | 8 / 384 | HARD | rotating sweep, 2–3 s period (EM-16) | yes |
| 9 `LANTERN` (new row) | Node lantern | 360° | — | — | 2 / 96 (`LANTERN_REACH_PX`) | SOFT | machinery-node point light | none |
| 10 `FAULT_GLOW` (new row) | Fault glow | 360° | — | — | the radius in base px of the Failure & Trail glow-table row; default 1.5 / 72 (`FAULT_GLOW_REACH_PX`) when a row carries none | SOFT | point source | none |
| 11 `BEAM_SPILL` (new row, **conditional**) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D24)* | Foot pool of the seat's beam (the beam record's own contribution, not a separate emitter) | 360° | — | — | `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` 24 px (0 removes the row; any non-zero value is 24–48, I-REACH) | SOFT | omnidirectional pool centred on the foot origin, present only while the beam is on, output `BEAM_SPILL_PM` 600, gates actions, non-shadow, excluded from `section_emission` (D.12) | none |

Rows 9 and 10 are new numbers (not in the art bible 8.3 table): *placeholder, owner technical-artist / Failure & Trail, confirmed by the Lighting spike*. Row 11 is new and **conditional**: it exists in the shape table iff `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` > 0 (default 24, user ruling D24, 2026-10-27), is owned by the game-designer and art-director, and is validated by the dark-at-feet playtest metric (EM-6, Open Question 17). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* `COS2_Q28` is derived by the `ShapeCatalog` freeze from `DirTable` (the square of the Q14 cosine of `half_angle_units`, in Q28; EM-19); the shape table is stage-1 content (`assets/data/emitter/shape_table.json`), not a code constant (ADR-0017 D1.2) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*. The split's axis is 206 units, not the 205 that 18° rounds to, because with the cones' half-angle rounded to 114 units a 205-unit axis leaves a 182-unit (15.996°) wedge and fails the art bible's "dark wedge ≥ 16°" (invariant I-WEDGE, Formulas D.15). The split's axes are ±206 units off the committed **aim**, not off facing (an art-bible wording refresh, Cross-References). The beam's `output_pm` is the constant `BEAM_BASE_PM` while on (placeholder 1000, owner game-designer; no low-charge dimming in the MVP). Reach and output of every shape row are independent of depth in the web and of Attention level: the art bible fixes the revealing radius as the one physical anchor, so "the dark presses in" is presentation (Lighting's ambient) and never a reach change (Open Question 3, resolved 2026-10-27: user ruling, kept). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**EM-9 — The beam in every actionable state; DOWNED keeps it, DEAD ends it.** The beam is available while the actor is IDLE, WALK, RUN, CARRY_WALK or CHANNELLING (PC-8; the user's chest-lamp decision: nothing the hands do touches the lamp). While `loco_state == DOWNED` the beam keeps its committed state — the aim is frozen at the last committed value and the origin is the chest point of the down animation row; a downed crewmate is a locator (P1) — which is the handoff PC left to this GDD ("Emitter State decides what a downed emitter does"). While STAGGERED the state is likewise unchanged. At the tick `emitter.place` reads `loco_state == DEAD`, it forces `switch_on` false (the Controller's `DEAD` row: "beam off (Emitter State on actor death)") as an `emitter.place`-internal `force_off`, a read of `controller.state` with no command. Because Save/Load's death stub restarts from the last autosave through `request_restore` at that tick's P6, the DEAD-tick off-state is unobservable in the MVP (say so in tests). A beam that stays on over a corpse until Suit's charge runs out — a corpse as a lantern the web can see — is *not* a rule here; it is a VS-B question for Attention and Sanity (Open Questions). In IN_POD the beam follows its actor into the pod's body frame (EM-30; Ship Body's `body_of_actor` carries the frame and Web Map & Transit is designed). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**EM-10 — Beam charge is Suit's.** Suit & Equipment owns charge, drain, `CHARGE_EMPTY` and the HUD "beam and tool charge" reading (art bible HUD set). `toggle_beam.validate` reads Suit's charge view and rejects a turn-ON at zero charge with `CHARGE_EMPTY`; a turn-OFF is never rejected for charge (the strobe floor `TOO_SOON` of EM-7 applies to both directions). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Suit's divisor-6 `suit.vitals` reads `emitter.state.lit` on the seat's beam to drain charge and calls `force_off(beam_id)` (E5) at zero; because `suit.vitals` runs earlier in the tick than `emitter.place`, a `force_off` takes effect in the same tick's `emitter.place`, and the owner's six-tick re-check (EM-25) then finds a channelled target unlit and aborts, so at most five ticks of channel run on a dark target. The beam is a *suit load, not a ship LOAD*: Emitter State never calls `set_load_request` for it and the Graph is not involved. (Suit & Equipment, designed: SE-8; Suit keeps the hook name `suit.vitals` and its `after: graph.propagate.atmo`.)

**EM-11 — Fixtures are switchable Graph LOADs; the lamp formula gates on the switch.** A FIXTURE carries `node_ref` (a Graph `13 LOAD` node of the LOAD subtype `LAMP` — the Item DB assigns the subtype id, currently `2 LAMP`; the Graph names only `HEATER`; provisional), `base_pm` (from the def's `emits`) and `switch_on`. Its brightness is `lamp_output_pm` (Formulas D.1): `output_pm = switch_on and armed and live ? ⌊base_pm * served_pm / 1000⌋ : 0`. The lit branch is the registry formula `power_allocation`'s output "lamp brightness = base * served_pm / 1000", reused verbatim. **`served_pm` is the tier's satisfaction `sat_k`, independent of the node's own request** (Graph D.6), so a switched-off lamp can still read `served_pm` 1000: the Emitter's own `switch_on` is the gate, not the Graph, or an off lamp would shine.
