Power draw is a Graph LOAD: inside the `set_switch` apply the Emitter calls M14 `set_load_request(node_ref, req_w)` with `req_w` the def's rated draw (Graph MVP: 20 W) when on and 0 when off (Class B: it clamps to `0..req_max`, queues to the next Graph phase 0, and is a no-op on an unknown, tombstoned, dormant or SALVAGED node). A lamp never renders brighter than the power it is served (Graph P1); under re-energise inrush the bank draws `req_w * INRUSH_PM / 1000` for `INRUSH_STEPS` runs, which can dim the tier-3 bank for half a second — the Graph's business, visible as an honest dip, changing no Emitter rule. The Emitter never recomputes watts and never calls M14 for node lanterns or the beam. A FIXTURE with `node_ref` 0 is *self-powered*: permitted only for the two declared subkinds, EMERGENCY (the cryo-bay emergency lamp, exactly one in the world, declared by Derelict Authoring DA-23 as `fixture_subkind_emergency`) and DOCK_STATUS (below); it has no M14 call, no ARMING and a fixed `base_pm`, and any other `node_ref` 0 fixture is refused by the authoring lint and at boot (`SELF_POWERED_LAMP_COUNT`: exactly one EMERGENCY, exactly one DOCK_STATUS per dock room, none elsewhere). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; second subkind added 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X2)* **The DOCK_STATUS fixture (user ruling X2, 2026-10-27)** keeps the pod console's lit gate satisfied at every dock without a further `act_gate` exception *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* (Interaction's IA-6 refusal of a `pod_hop` exemption stands): it is an *ordinary lit fixture* (`gates_actions` true, SOFT curve, counted in `lux_pm`, `act_lux_pm` and `section_emission`, so the web can read it like any lamp, P3 and "light is never free"), authored by Derelict Authoring on the dock room's docked-podcraft prop, in the **dock room's section** and not on the pod body (the pod section is dormant always, Web Map WM-5, and the lamp must not depend on it). It is created and discarded with its section (E1, E2), has no `node_ref` and therefore no liveness test (EM-13 concerns Graph nodes) and cannot be failed, starved or drained by any ship state, which is the point: the stranded player always finds the console lit. Its `switch_on` is driven only by the mutator **E10 `set_dock_status(section_id, on)`**, which Web Map & Transit calls in the apply that writes `pod_at` (on for the dock room the pod parks at, off for the one it leaves; the berth and unberth verbs are the derelict weld verbs and have nothing to do with it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the first draft named the berth and unberth applies, and read `pod_at` at E1)*, so the glow emits only while the pod is there. **E1 creates every DOCK_STATUS glow switched off and reads nothing of Web Map's state**: E1 runs at `init_world` step 2, before Web Map writes `pod_at` at step 7, so the home dock's glow is switched on by Web Map's step-7 call to E10 (`pod_at` HOME) in the same `init_world` apply, and the derelict docks' glows stay off until the pod parks there. `set_switch` of either origin is refused `NOT_SWITCHABLE` for this subkind and Interaction registers no switch interactable for it; it takes no M14 call and no ARMING, and E10 has no commit-spacing guard and writes no `last_toggle_tick` (E10's row in EM-5 gives the reason: the pod's own timing spaces the transitions, and an atomic apply could not observe a refusal) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **Values** (placeholders, owner game-designer and art-director, confirmed by the Lighting spike; derivation in D.1b): `DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM` 500, `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` 48, shape row 7 (`FIXTURE`, SOFT) at that reach, and the placement constraint `DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX` 24 (the console subject's anchor lies within 24 px of the glow origin, clear of every occluder). Its luminance and hue sit outside the visible-unknown lamp's band (the art-director's, not this GDD's; a Lighting `hue_class` token owed). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**EM-12 — One latency story for switching.** A `set_switch` applies at P2 of tick `T_a`; its M14 call is queued to the next Graph phase 0; the lamp is **ARMING with `output_pm` 0** until `arm_tick`, the first `graph.propagate.power` run that consumed the request, and is lit on the first `emitter.place` at or after it (Formulas D.2 `switch_latency`: `arm_tick = T_a + posmod(power_offset - T_a, GRAPH_STEP_TICKS)` with `power_offset` read from `GraphTuning`; identical to the earlier `T_a mod GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` form at offset 0; ADR-0017 D5.2) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*. Under the stated assumptions the lamp is lit between 0 and `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS - 1` ticks after the apply — at most 83 ms — and no single number from this paragraph is a design fact: D.2 derives the range with a worked example at both alignments (switch on a Graph tick; switch on the tick after one). **Assumption A1 (derived, not assumed; confirmed by the Graph 2026-10-27 in its Interactions row for this system):** kernel CR-2 ("P3 sees all of P2") and Graph LS-8 phase 0 ("apply queued unconditional inputs: `set_load_request` changes") together mean that an M14 call made in P2 of a Graph tick is consumed by that same tick's phase 0, so the range is 0..`GRAPH_STEP_TICKS - 1` ticks; the negative case, a Graph that applied only strictly later requests, would give 1..`GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` and is kept in D.2 as the labelled alternative. The direct `after: graph.propagate.failure` entry (EM-1) guarantees that the hook reads that run's output on the same tick. Switching **off** is immediate (`output_pm` 0 from the apply tick; M14 `(node_ref, 0)`); an OFF applied while the lamp is still ARMING cancels the pending ON and is not a realised transition (it writes no `last_toggle_tick`, EM-7, D.2b). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D32)* The same "light changes only on committed state" rule PC-5 states for the beam holds for fixtures; the asymmetry (ON waits for the Graph pulse, OFF does not) is deliberate and physical: the ship has to serve the lamp, and the lamp cannot go on ahead of the ship. Fixtures never pre-play light; the wall-switch click and animation pre-play. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**EM-13 — Liveness: a lamp is dark when its node is dead.** A lamp or lantern is *live* iff its `node_ref` exists and `node(id).state` is neither `3 DESTROYED`, `2 FAILED`, `4 SALVAGED` nor `5 ABSENT` (Graph LS-10) and the node is not dormant or tombstoned (read each tick from the Graph read view). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: 'live' is the output rule only. Command validation uses 'structurally present' (EM-7), which excludes SALVAGED, ABSENT, dormant and tombstoned nodes but not FAILED or DESTROYED ones, so a failed lamp can still be switched.)* A not-live lamp has `output_pm` 0 while its `switch_on` stays as committed — when the node is repaired the lamp lights again without a new command. `served_pm` and node `state` are read live each tick; both change only on Graph ticks, and because `emitter.place` is `after: graph.propagate.failure` (EM-1) a Graph-tick read sees the state the run just produced, never a pre-failure-phase state. Which Graph states count as "running" for a *lantern* is the Item DB's `lantern_on_states` (default `{0 OK, 1 DEGRADED}`, resolved 2026-10-26). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(orchestrator ruling: R22 as amended at the checkpoint (CP-3) — `emitter.place` declares the direct `after:` entry, so no read is ever lagged; the technical-director's proposal to sample slow inputs once per Graph run into canonical state is not adopted, because the direct ordering needs no sampled state and a restore at a Graph tick rebuilds the identical table.)*

**EM-14 — Node lanterns are derived and switchless.** A NODE_LANTERN stands for the glow of a running machine (every machine node whose def carries an `emits` with shape `LANTERN`; the kinds are Item DB data, IDB-31, not a list in this rule: in the MVP a reactor cell, a coolant pump and a scrubber). E1 creates one NODE_LANTERN per such node, and nothing in this rule restricts the kind; the shape key stays `LANTERN` and the hue is per loop (`LANTERN_POWER`, `LANTERN_COOLANT`, `LANTERN_O2`). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* It has no switch and makes no `set_load_request` call: `output_pm` is `⌊base_pm * term / 1000⌋` when the node is live and *running* (Formulas D.3 `node_lantern_output`), where `term` is the node's `run_pm` reading. A lantern gated on `served_pm` alone would glow from a switched-off machine (the same trap as EM-11), so the lantern reads a *running* quantity `run_pm` from the Graph read view — **delivered by the Graph** (LS-19 additions, 2026-10-27: `node(id).run_pm`, 0..1000 — 0 when the requested load is 0 (LOAD `req_w` 0, PUMP `throttle_pm` 0, REACTOR `out_w` 0), otherwise `served_pm` for a consumer and `⌊1000 × out_w / out_w_rated⌋` for a REACTOR; it is not the Graph's D.9 `duty_pm`, which reads 1000 for a machine switched off on a met tier and carries the same trap as EM-11). The Emitter **consumes `run_pm` as published and clamps it** to 0..1000 (D.3): it never recomputes it from `out_w`, so a REACTOR running above rated output cannot make a lantern outshine its `base_pm`, and a rated output of 0 is the Graph's guard, not an Emitter division. There is no fallback form: a browned-out or starved machine glows at its served fraction and a lantern that cannot read `run_pm` is a boot defect, not a weaker rule (a lantern at full `base_pm` while its machine starves would lie about power state, P1). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Lanterns are created and destroyed with their section (EM-5), counted against Lighting's lantern budget by authoring lint (EM-26), and killing the machine is the only way to dark one — "every loop you run is a lantern the web can see" (art bible Principle 3).

**EM-15 — Deployables and glowsticks: spawn on landing, steady, then waning, then gone.** `spawn_deployable(scope, kind, body_id, pos_x, pos_y, owner, life_ticks, out)` (E3; body-frame px, `body_id` 0 derives the body from the owner with `BodyView.body_of_actor`, ADR-0017 D3.4, CT-46) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)* is called by Interaction's landing-tick hook (PC D.16: "the light exists only on landing"; the thrown light is never pre-played). The glowstick numbers are the Item DB's placeholders and are cited by name: `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` 150 (60–300), `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES` 2 (= 96 base px, 1.5–3), `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` 4, `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM` 400, `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM` 250 (Item DB IDB-12). No tick-denominated life constant is registered by this GDD: the record's `life_total_ticks` is `ticks_for_seconds(GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S)` = 9000 taken from the def at spawn, and the Item DB's rule that **the life is counted from the throw tick, not from landing** (a stick that never lands still burns out) is honoured by Interaction passing `life_ticks` = the life remaining at landing (9000 minus `THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30 for a thrown stick). This GDD owns the curve's *semantics*, id `WANE_LINEAR_25`: while the remaining life fraction is at or above `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM / 1000` the emitter holds its full reach (steady); below it, `reach_px` falls linearly (in the permille factor `f`, then floored to whole px) to `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM / 1000` of full reach at the last tick (24 px at `life_remaining_ticks` 1); this is the Item DB's F-7 `reach_px(rem)` verbatim, so the two GDDs agree tick for tick; at `life_remaining_ticks` 0 the record is removed and its id retired (Formulas D.9). *(orchestrator ruling: the Item DB's steady-then-waning parameterisation, `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM` 400, is adopted over the routed ruling's linear-from-spawn curve — they are the same curve at `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM` 1000 — because the art bible specifies "steady at full value, then dimming monotonically", and the Item DB owns these numbers. A different number there is a conflict to surface, not a silent change.)* The output stays constant (`GLOWSTICK_BASE_PM`), the reveal radius shrinks, and that waning is **sim state**, not presentation, because it changes the real reveal radius (P1). It never flickers (art bible 3.2); the flicker of the VS-A flare, torch and decoy family is Lighting's and invisible to the query, which uses the steady mean. The stick lights the floor plane in ship-local px, counts as exposure like any pool (Attention may weight by area rather than radius — a pool at the floor radius has one sixteenth of the full area; the read view carries `reach_px` and the shape so either is derivable), and a stick landing in a closed room lights only that room (occluders). At most `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` non-evicting deployables exist. **Cap eviction is a visible wane, never an instant removal** *(creative-director CD-GDD-ALIGN concern 2: a light dying with no physical cause would break the promise that a stick's waning is its only clock, and would be a P1 lie)*: a spawn over the cap (Class B never rejects; the item was already spent, and Item DB rule (c): a landed stick is spent without refund) is applied in full, and the lowest-id deployable of the same kind that is not already evicting enters an **evict-wane** — `evict_tick` and `reach_at_evict` are written, its output stays `GLOWSTICK_BASE_PM`, and its reach falls linearly from its current reach to the 24 px floor over `ticks_for_seconds(GLOWSTICK_EVICT_WANE_S)` ticks (placeholder 10 s = 600 ticks, owner game-designer, Tuning Knobs), then the record is removed and its id retired (Formulas D.9b). It reads in the world as a stick guttering fast, the same visible monotone shrink as the ordinary wane, only quicker; the 24 px floor is the ordinary wane's own last value, so the final removal is the same event the ordinary end of life already is. An evicting stick still exposes, still gates actions and still sums at its current reach until it is gone; it does not count against `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE`, but the evicting tail is itself bounded by `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_EVICTING` (placeholder 4); an eviction that would exceed it completes the oldest evicting stick at once, increments `emitter_deployable_evict_overflow` and fires a debug assertion — a budget defect, never a gameplay state. **Eviction is meant to be unreachable by supply in the MVP, and that supply bound belongs to other owners** *(cross-GDD invariant I-SUPPLY, routed, Cross-References; the total glowsticks one seat carries across all stacks is at most `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE`, so no seat can have more sticks in flight than the cap)*: Suit & Equipment owns the per-seat carry count and start count, the Item DB owns `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX`, and Derelict Authoring owns the loose-stick supply by depth, which must not allow a throw-pickup-throw cycle to exceed the cap inside one `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` (a landed stick is never picked up again, Item DB rule (c), so a cycle needs a loose stick on the floor). The evict-wane exists because the bound can be violated by content and by VS-B (four seats × a stack of 4 is 16 against a cap of 8); every eviction increments the release telemetry counter `emitter_deployable_evictions`.

**EM-16 — The beacon is a pure function of time.** A BEACON is a 20° cone (shape row 8) whose axis is `beacon_aim = (beacon_phase + beacon_dir * ⌊T * AIM_UNITS / BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS⌋) mod 4096` with `T` the kernel clock tick (non-negative modulus), `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` 128 (2.13 s, inside the art bible's 2–3 s; placeholder, owner art-director / audio, confirmed by the Lighting spike), so the step is exactly 32 units per tick. Direction and phase are authored data; **no per-tick angle state exists**, which is what makes save-equivalence automatic (the kernel clock is persisted by the kernel). *(orchestrator ruling: the rational form of the systems-designer over the routed ruling's `T * BEACON_UNITS_PER_TICK`, because it is identical at 128 and lets any integer sweep length be valid — the ruled range 120–180 contained exactly one divisor of 4096.)* The beacon's brightness is the lamp formula when it carries a `node_ref` (MVP: it does — a dead ship's beacon is dark, P1), switched by `set_beacon`; turned on, it starts from the angle the pure function gives at that tick (no restart at phase 0). It is a dynamic-layer emitter (it rotates) and `gates_actions` is **false** *(orchestrator ruling: the systems-designer's F9 adopted, then **ruled kept by the user 2026-10-27 (Open Question 1, D20; no longer provisional)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* — a cone sweeping over a point lights it for 14 or 15 of every 128 ticks by phase (the cone is 456 units wide and the sweep advances 32 units per tick, so ⌊456/32⌋ = 14 or ⌈456/32⌉ = 15; about 11 %), and a prompt that strobes at the sweep rate, or a channel that aborts every 128 ticks, would be the system lying about what can be worked; the beacon exposes and is seen, it does not enable verbs. This narrows the Controller's PC-6 for one kind; routed to the technical-director and game-designer)*.

**EM-17 — Fault glow is a derived emitter kind.** FAULT_GLOW emitters stand for a venting ember, a power arc and cyan O₂ mist as point sources (art bible 2.8 / Failure & Trail). They are created and removed by `emitter.place` from the Graph's failure descriptors (`failures(filter)`), each keyed by `FailureDescriptor.mode` (a failure mode 1..10, or a descriptor-only id 20..26) plus `medium`, matching the Failure & Trail glow-table keys; the MVP rows with a glow are `1/FUEL`, `1/GAS`, `2`, `3`, `7`, `9`. They use Failure & Trail's descriptor-to-glow data table (rows owned by F&T; a descriptor with no glow row creates no emitter), by comparing the Graph's list, read on Graph ticks, against the FAULT_GLOW records that already exist — no subscription (kernel CR-10) and no stored previous list. They have no switch, no Graph LOAD and no command; `output_pm` comes from the descriptor's `sev` 0..4 and the row's `sev_min` gate (D.11); they are non-shadow and count as exposure. **Position, section and frame — the anchor rule** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the first draft read mode, medium and `sev` from the descriptor but no position, and the Graph has no pixel geometry (LS-19), so the cone and radius test, the occlusion start `S`, the `section_emission` attribution and sim/render agreement had nothing to stand on)*: a FAULT_GLOW record's position is read from Layout, never guessed. For a descriptor that has a glow row: (1) `section_id` = the Graph's `section_of(descriptor.anchor)` — `section_of(node_id)` for a `(node_id, port_local_index)` anchor, `section_of(edge_id)` for an `edge_id` anchor (a compartment descriptor has no anchor and, by the F&T table, no glow row); (2) `body_id` = Layout `body_of(section_id)` (a Class 1 static query, valid straight after `restore()`, Layout LY-26 b); (3) `anchor_px` = **the first point, `base_px_polyline[0]`, of Layout's `anchor_geometry(descriptor.anchor)`** — the head of the combined wall-then-floor run, which is the source stamp the trail's glow comes from (FT-13, FT-27): a section-local base-px floor-plane point; for a `WALL_FACE` anchor it is the base of the host face, the first legal row in front of the wall (Layout LY-8 b), which already satisfies the clearance of EM-20 (b); and `origin_px` (derived) = `anchor_px + section_offset(section_id)` in the body frame, used for reach, distance and the occlusion start alike (EM-6: other kinds use their floor-plane anchor for both). `anchor_geometry` is a pure static read made once at creation and again in `post_restore`, never per tick. **Fallback when the Layout read is empty** (Layout returns nothing, without an error, only for a DISCARDED section, LY-23): no record is created on that Graph tick, `emitter_fault_glow_no_anchor` increments (release telemetry) and a debug assertion fires; reconciliation retries on the next Graph tick, and the case cannot persist for a loaded section because Layout's lint (`WALL_FITTING_NO_RUN`, AC-87) refuses a glow-capable fitting with no run. **Clearance lint, extended to every emitter anchor** (EM-20 b): a data-load lint checks the anchor of every authored FIXTURE, NODE_LANTERN and BEACON placement (the E1 input) and the head of every glow-capable `anchor_geometry` polyline against `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` for every `OCCLUDES_LIGHT` rectangle, and refuses the build (`EMITTER_ANCHOR_CLEARANCE`, naming the placement or anchor); it covers template data, so no run-time nudge exists. If a defect gets past it, E1 or the FAULT_GLOW creation still makes the record (the exposure stays honest: `section_emission` counts it, P3) but increments `emitter_anchor_clearance_fail` and asserts in debug, and the emitter lights nothing in its own room (its segment starts inside a rectangle, so every pair is blocked) — a diagnosable defect, where the first draft's was silent. They are capped at `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` (placeholder 10); **a fault's light never goes dark with no physical cause** *(creative-director CD-GDD-ALIGN concern 1: a real failure whose light vanishes weakens P1, the physical consequence becomes invisible, and P2, a fault glow is one of the tells a careful player reads)*, so the cap is a content bound that is held by a boot invariant (I-CAPS (c), D.15: `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` is at least the most simultaneous glowing authored faults on one body plus a 25 % margin; in the MVP slice a welded body carries at most 6 simultaneous glow rows, so the floor is ⌈1.25 × 6⌉ = 8 ≤ 10, the 8 live faults being spread world-wide across bodies; registry: `mvp_fault_allocation`), and eviction is a last resort with three rules. (1) **Lowest severity first**: when reconciliation would hold more than `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` records, the records evicted are those with the lowest `sev`, ties broken by the higher descriptor id first (the kept set is the same as "highest `sev` wins, ties by ascending descriptor id"). (2) **A protected record is never evicted**: a record whose age `T − placed_tick` is below `ticks_for_seconds(W_MIN_WARNING_S)` (the Graph's registered fairness constant, 10 s = 600 ticks; cited, not restated) is protected, so the tell of a new fault is always shown for at least the Graph's warning window. (3) **Protection beats the cap**: if every candidate for eviction is protected, no record is evicted and the table stays over `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` (bounded only by `MAX_EMITTERS`, at which the creation rule of EM-24 refuses and counts), increments the release telemetry counter `emitter_fault_glow_overcap` and fires a debug assertion; it is trimmed back by rules 1 and 2 on the first Graph tick on which a candidate is no longer protected. Overcap is a content or budget defect and never a gameplay state. Every eviction increments `emitter_fault_glow_evictions`. An evicted glow removes only the descriptor's light and its exposure: the Failure & Trail emissive overlay on the sprite (an unshaded overlay, not an emitter) still draws the fault, so the fault stays visible in the world. **Timing (R22 as amended, CP-3):** `emitter.place` is `after: graph.propagate.failure`, so on a Graph tick it reads the descriptor list that run just produced and a glow appears on the Graph tick of its descriptor, never later; between Graph ticks the list cannot change. A FAULT_GLOW record is a normal record (it has an id from the canonical counter), so it persists as the set of descriptors it stood for. The emissive `_msk` trail glow on a sprite is an *unshaded overlay, not an emitter*, and exposes nothing: a lit-but-unregistered source would light a room Attention cannot see (Failure & Trail's brief). Whether a glow gates actions: yes (an arc lights the scrubber beside it). (Failure & Trail's designed FT-27 agrees: it supplies "a descriptor-to-glow data table (palette hex, radius in base px, flicker class bounded by FT-29)" and "Emitter State registers the emitter from the same descriptor". **Failure & Trail owns the glow-table rows including the ledger values `GLOW_BASE_PM[key]` and `SEV_GLOW_PM[0..4]` (placeholders it carries in V/A.6); this GDD owns D.11, the FAULT_GLOW record, `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` and `FAULT_GLOW_REACH_PX`, and multiplies the ledger values.** This GDD takes each row's `reach_px` from the F&T `radius_px` at the sev 4 column (the sev 3 column where sev 4 is n/a), defaulting to `FAULT_GLOW_REACH_PX` when a row carries none; a row radius outside [`REACH_MIN_PX`, `REACH_MAX_PX`] (invariant I-REACH, D.15) is a data defect — the data-load lint refuses it, and if one gets past the lint the record uses `FAULT_GLOW_REACH_PX`, `emitter_reach_invalid` increments and a debug assertion fires, so the glow is never dark and no division by a zero reach can occur. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* F&T's emissive layer "casts no light", which is the `_msk` sentence above.)

**EM-18 — The illumination model: contribution, curve classes, saturating sum.** For one emitter and one subject, the contribution `c` is **0** unless all of: the subject's `body_id` equals the emitter's (EM-30), the subject is inside the emitter's radius (`d2 <= reach_px²`, a closed edge), inside its cone for cone shapes (EM-19), not blocked (EM-20). The one exception is the owner's own beam on the owner's ACTOR subject, which contributes its apex value, facing- and occlusion-independent (EM-21). For every other pair `c = ⌊output_pm * f(d, reach_px) / 1000⌋` (Formulas D.7), `d` the exact integer floor of the distance from the emitter's origin (the chest, for the beam). Two **curve classes** are carried as a data table so the Lighting spike can replace a curve without changing the API: **SOFT** (pools, fixtures, glowsticks, lanterns, fault glow) `f = 1000 * (R - d) / R`, linear to zero at the fade-out radius; **HARD** (the cones and the beacon) `f = EDGE_FLOOR_PM + (1000 - EDGE_FLOOR_PM) * (R - d) / R`, so the contribution at the rim of a full-output hard-edged emitter is `EDGE_FLOOR_PM` (placeholder 250 permille, range 160–400, owner technical-artist, confirmed by the Lighting spike) and the lit boundary **is** the drawn hard edge. *(orchestrator ruling: this is a deliberate deviation from the routed ruling's single default curve `c = output_pm * (R - d) / R`, which contradicts its own "hard outer edge exactly at `reach_px`" clause: with `LIT_ON_PM` 150 a full-output wide beam of reach 288 px would be actionable only to `d <= 244` px — 44 px, almost a full tile, inside the hard edge the player sees, the light visibly falling on a valve the hand cannot reach, which is the game lying (P1) and contradicts art bible hero #1. Three specialists converged on the edge-floor remedy; rejected: lowering `LIT_ON_PM` toward 0 (a near-black pixel becomes actionable — a different lie, and hysteresis dies) and redefining the "drawn edge" as an iso-line at 150 permille (contradicts the hard edge). Surfaced as a deviation from the ruling's default in the return value, not a registry conflict.)* The consequence is intended: a *dimmed* hard-edged emitter (output below `⌈LIT_ON_PM * 1000 / EDGE_FLOOR_PM⌉` = 600) has an honestly smaller actionable area; a full-output emitter is actionable to its last pixel (invariant I-EDGE: `BEAM_BASE_PM * EDGE_FLOOR_PM >= 1000 * LIT_ON_PM`). For SOFT classes the actionable radius `r_lit` is **the largest `d` whose integer chain (D.7) gives `c ≥ LIT_ON_PM`**, published by the sim per emitter as `r_lit_px` (the closed form ⌊R × (output_pm − LIT_ON_PM) / output_pm⌋ of the first draft ran 1 px high for some dimmed outputs and is not used) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, inside the fade-out radius `reach_px` by design (a full fixture of reach 144 px is actionable to 122 px on approach). The **latch-release radius** `r_off_px` (the largest `d` whose chain gives `c ≥ LIT_OFF_PM`, D.7; 126 px for the same fixture) is where an already-lit subject stops being lit: Lighting draws a soft pool to the published `r_off_px` (a prompt never appears outside the visible pool), and `r_lit_px` is where an approaching subject first becomes actionable, `r_off_px − r_lit_px` px inside the drawn edge; the Lighting spike confirms the drawn edge (the art bible and Item DB "radius" numbers — 3 tiles, 2 tiles — are provisional, and the spike must decide whether they mean fade-out or readable radius). **Resolved 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix (G6): the pool is drawn to the latch-release radius (Lighting's `soft_pool_scale`, the registry source, wins); Emitter State's `lit` latch is unchanged.** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* **Aggregation is a saturating sum**, `lux_pm = min(1000, Σ c)`: integer addition is commutative, so no iteration-order dependence; several lamps in one room read brighter and expose more until fully lit. There is **no ambient term**: `AMBIENT_PM` is fixed at 0 in the sim, and Lighting's ambient is cosmetic and can never make an object actionable (P1; art bible Principle 1: unlit, not fogged). `lux_pm` sums every emitter (Horror reads it; Attention reads `emitters()` and never `lux_pm`, AT-4); `act_lux_pm` sums only the emitters whose `gates_actions` is true (EM-3) and feeds `lit` (EM-22).

**EM-19 — Cone membership is fixed-point and mirror-symmetric.** A cone test uses no trigonometry, square root or float at run time (Graph LS-1(b) precedent). Directions come from a table `DIR` of Q14 unit vectors — **the table is generated at build time by a data tool outside `src/sim/emitter/` and checked in as `dir_table.gd`, a generated `const Array[int]` pair (`const PackedInt32Array(...)` is not a constant expression on 4.7.2) and the source of truth, never computed in the sim module** (the boot step only reads it and asserts I-MIRROR and I-OVERFLOW, so the AC-2 lint holds; ADR-0017 D1.3) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)* — indexed by an `AIM_UNITS` angle (0 = E, counter-clockwise, screen y down, so aim 3072 = S is `(0, +16384)`), generated from one quadrant by reflection so that `DIR[2048 - a] = (-ux, uy)` **exactly**. A subject at offset `r = anchor - origin` is in the cone iff `d2 <= R²`, `D > 0` and `D² >= d2 * COS2_Q28`, with `D = rx*ux + ry*uy` (Formulas D.5); the `D > 0` guard is required (the squared test alone accepts the opposite half-plane) and `d2 = 0` is inside every cone of that emitter, the apex included. The split beam is two tests at axes `aim ± 206` units; the wedge between them never closes (I-WEDGE), so a point is in at most one cone and its contribution is never doubled. Every product has a documented bound under 2^62 and the boot assertion refuses a build that violates it (Formulas D.15). The half-angles and the 18° offset are not integral in `AIM_UNITS`, so the shape table (EM-8) carries the encoded integers; the quantisation misplaces a rim by under 0.1° (under 0.7 px at 384 px), far inside the accepted committed-versus-drawn divergence. **The symmetry property is a test, not a hope**: the exposure of a mirrored scene (x → -x, aim `a` → `2048 - a`, `flip_h` toggled) equals the mirror image of the original exactly, for the five drawn and three mirrored facings, every `CHEST_POINT` animation row and all four shapes (Acceptance Criteria).

**EM-20 — Occlusion is integer geometry over the Layout's opaque footprints.** *(orchestrator ruling R3 — the one deliberate deviation from a routed thread's wording.)* The routed thread and the index describe occlusion as tests on "physics shapes". This GDD honours that in substance and changes the test method: the occluding shapes are the Layout's collision-footprint rectangles — walls (`WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` 24 base px: face 72, cap 24, tile 48), closed door leaves, and props flagged `occludes_light` — which are the very rectangles the Layout `PROJECTOR` hands to physics and `LocomotionStep` already sweeps the avatar's box against (PC-14, PC-18). The query tests a segment against those rectangles in exact integer arithmetic (Formulas D.6) behind an **`OccluderSource` seam** (`rects_in(body_id, ax0, ay0, ax1, ay1, out) -> int`, ascending rect key into a caller-owned `QueryBuffer` of occupied spans; `version(section_id)`, a monotone counter bumped when any opaque rectangle in the section changes; `static_version`, `version_epoch()`, `changed_region_*`; ADR-0014 section 5) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*; there is **no physics backend** in the MVP, **not even a test-only one**: the cross-check of the integer segment test is an independent integer separating-axis test (ADR-0017 Alternative 1) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*. The reasons that decide it: kernel H10 (systems stay scene-tree free, headless) and CR-13 G3 (the 2D physics engine makes no determinism claim, so a physics-derived `lit` flowing into a persisted block would be a physics observation that must be recorded for replay); a direct-space-state query is valid only during the physics step, while the kernel allows this query from presentation prompts at render rate, exactly the unsafe site; and the 2D ray API is not sourceable in the pinned engine reference (its `physics.md` carries only a 3D example). *Rules.* (a) **Cells are unit squares, boxes are closed, and the rule is conservative** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the first draft tested the closed span of cell *indices* `[x0, x1−1]`, which treats a cell as a point, so two abutting rectangles `[0,24)` and `[24,48)` left an open strip between x 23 and x 24 and an oblique ray crossed a wall seam untouched while Lighting drew a seamless shadow — a prompt lit behind a wall, a P1 lie; every worked example used an axis-aligned ray, which hid it)*: Layout's integer coordinates name pixel cells (LY-12 b), and a cell is a unit square centred on its integer coordinate. The box D.6 tests for an occupied span `(sx0, sy0, sx1, sy1)` returned by `rects_in` (= the stored `[x0, x1) × [y0, y1)` as `(x0, y0, x1−1, y1−1)`) is the closed continuous box `[sx0 − ½, sx1 + ½] × [sy0 − ½, sy1 + ½]`, computed in **doubled coordinates** (every segment endpoint × 2) as the integer box `[2·sx0 − 1, 2·sx1 + 1] × [2·sy0 − 1, 2·sy1 + 1]` — in the stored rectangle's own terms `[2·x0 − 1, 2·x1 − 1] × [2·y0 − 1, 2·y1 − 1]`. Abutting rectangles share a box face (`[0,24)` and `[24,48)` give doubled x `[−1, 47]` and `[47, 95]`, touching at 47), so no wall seam, door-leaf/jamb joint or corner join leaves a strip; a segment that touches a box (a corner graze, an edge-on pass) is blocked; light does not leak around a corner. A segment endpoint is an integer cell, doubled to an even number against odd box bounds, so it is never exactly on a box face and is inside a box if and only if its cell is occupied. This convention is stated once, here and in D.6; Layout's occupied-span algebra (registry `rect_algebra_closed_span`, LY-12 b, D.2) remains the definition of *which cells are occupied* and of the anchor lint and is unchanged (routed to Layout: its rule text "the conservative closed test is applied to the occupied span" must read "the cell-unit box of the occupied span", Cross-References). (b) **Anchors must be at least `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` (1 base px) outside every occluding rectangle** (Chebyshev distance in cells from the occupied span, Layout D.2: a wall stored [408,432), whose occupied span is x 408..431, admits anchors at x = 407 and x = 432 and rejects 408 through 431; registry: `rect_algebra_closed_span`, the Layout's half-open convention), checked by authoring lint at registration. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **What the clearance means under the unit-square convention:** at 1 the anchor's centre is half a pixel (one doubled unit) outside the occluding box face, with its own cell directly adjacent to the wall, which guarantees that the segment's start is outside every box; a clearance of 0 would put the anchor inside the box. The check covers **every emitter anchor, not only subject anchors**: fixture, lantern and beacon placements at instantiate (a data-load lint) and FAULT_GLOW anchors at creation (EM-17); an emitter anchored on its own wall footprint would light nothing in its room and, in the first draft, raised no diagnostic. An anchor found strictly inside a footprint at run time is treated as blocked from every emitter (fails safe to dark) and increments `emitter_anchor_clearance_fail`. (c) **Actors and horrors never occlude.** A shadow with no occluder is a stage-2 sanity symptom (art bible 3.2 open item: it should not cast an occluding shadow) and must stay an unambiguous tell; so a horror standing in a beam leaves the beam unchanged and the subject beyond it lit. (d) **Door state is read from the Layout projection of the Graph's `door_state`** (Graph LS-6: the Graph owns door state and opening `area`) — the same door-leaf rectangle `collide` uses (PC-3, PC-18 test 7: same-tick door response) — so there is one source for collision and light, derived from the Graph in the same tick, and Emitter State holds **no door flag**. The door-leaf rectangle is the Layout's `door_leaf_gap` projection (Layout D.5, LY-9): `leaf_w` is continuous in the Graph's opening area and `leaf_w` 0 yields no rectangle, so no open-fraction threshold exists; `DOOR_LIGHT_OPEN_PM` is deprecated and unused. (e) A door interactable registers **two** face subjects, one per face, each `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` outside the leaf, so a closed leaf blocks the other side's emitters from each face subject (Interaction's registration; EM-21). (f) No glass or translucent occluders in the MVP. (g) The occlusion segment starts at the occlusion origin of EM-6 (the foot, for the beam). The wording this implies for the index and the kernel is routed in Cross-References. *Alternatives considered:* a physics-space query against a dedicated occluder layer — rejected (above); reusing a rendering occluder object as the sim's occluder — rejected outright, it is Lighting's rendering object and the sim never reads it.

**EM-21 — Subjects register; each has one anchor; an unregistered ref answers like an unlit one.** Anything that asks "am I lit" registers a **subject** with `register_subject(ref, kind, anchor_px, section_id)` (E6) and withdraws it with `unregister_subject(ref)` (E7). Subject kinds: OBJECT (an interaction anchor), ACTOR (the foot origin, *read* from `body.state` each tick, not supplied; its `section_id` and `body_id` are read the same way from Body's `actor_section(actor_id)` and `body_of_actor(actor_id)`, BDY-29, so the owner's exposure is attributed to the section the foot stands in and moves with it across a door), HORROR, ITEM (loose items). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The subject's single `anchor_px` is the only sampled point: a large object half in a beam is lit iff its anchor is (the same one-point model as `REACH_PX` and focus scoring, PC-6, and the cheapest symmetric one). Sections are never point-sampled; they aggregate by emitter list (`section_emission`, EM-27). `moving` is a per-registration flag: ACTOR and HORROR subjects are always moving; an ITEM registers static (a carried item is unregistered at pickup and re-registered at drop); only moving registrations count against `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS`. **The owner stands in their own light** *(orchestrator ruling: the systems-designer's SD-7 adopted with the checkpoint fix CP-9 — as first proposed it excluded the owner's beam from the owner's lux, so a player alone with the lamp on would have read `lux` 0 to Horror, the opposite of P3)*: the owner's own beam contributes its **full `output_pm`** — the d = 0 apex value, which EM-19 already places inside every cone — to the owner's ACTOR `lux_pm`, independent of facing and of occlusion, so the owner's self-lux never flips with the way they face; it is not added to the owner's `act_lux_pm` (the player is never the target of their own verb for the `act_gate` lit test; the one self-targeted verb, the ACTOR_SELF self-patch, is `act_gate`-exempt, Interaction IA-6, so `lit(ACTOR_SELF)` is never consulted for it and no self-lit or anchor-coverage rule is owed here; crediting the owner's beam to `act_lux_pm` is deliberately not done) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Attention reads the beam as *emission* (`emitters()`, `section_emission`) and does not add the same beam again to the owner's lux (EM-27). Every other beam lights the owner, and the owner's beam lights everyone else, by the ordinary cone test. **An unregistered ref answers exactly like an unlit registered one** *(orchestrator ruling: SD-16 adopted — a P4 requirement)*: `lux(ref)` is 0 and `lit(ref)` is false in release builds, so the Perception Filter's phantom targets (kernel Open Question 14) cost Interaction the same check as real unlit ones and the query cannot become an existence oracle that undermines the phantom-loot hallucination. A debug-only `registered(ref)` exists for tests and the Debug Overlay. Registration over `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` reports `OVER_CAP` in `out.code`, increments `emitter_subjects_overflow` and the subject reads dark (fails safe); a duplicate registration is a no-op reporting `DUPLICATE` (idempotent, not a defect) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*. **Runtime overflow is a content or budget defect, never a gameplay state** *(creative-director CD-GDD-ALIGN concern 3)*: failing safe to dark is the right behaviour for a subject the table cannot hold, but a real object sitting visibly inside a beam that refuses to prompt breaks design test 1 (the honest edge) and the P4 "reach for it" falsifier, because the player reads a real lit object as a hallucination. So the caps are held upstream by budgets, not discovered at run time (EM-24, D.15 I-CAPS (d)), and the two counters `emitter_subjects_overflow` and `emitter_moving_overflow` ship in release builds as telemetry (the probe counter went with the probes, EM-24) (a counter in the diagnostics block, not only a debug assertion), so a budget breach in a playtest or live build is visible and reportable. The subject table fields are below; every subject's `body_id` is derived (Layout `body_of(section_id)`; Body `body_of_actor` for an ACTOR) and never stored or hashed *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Registrations are canonical (EM-28, R16): registrants (Interaction, Horror, Suit) own the thing registered; registrations restore verbatim, a registrant's `post_restore` only compares them with its own window without writing, and its first ordinary hook run repairs drift through E6 / E7 / E8 with at most a one-run lag (ADR-0017 D10.2, CT-38) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*.

| Field | Type | C / D | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ref` | kind-tagged integer | C | opaque to this block |
| `kind` | enum | C | OBJECT, ACTOR, HORROR, ITEM |
| `anchor_px` | int2 | C | OBJECT and ITEM: **section-local** (Layout's frame, as Layout's `subject_anchors` returns it), composed with `section_offset(section_id)` at evaluation like an emitter's, so a weld or detach moves nothing; ACTOR: read from Body in the body frame; HORROR: the body-frame position Horror passes through E6 / E8 (Horror HE-34), in the record's current body frame, converted to section-local px at apply time (W3) with the offsets of that moment (ADR-0017 D10.2) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*; a weld or cut that changes the `section_offset` of the horror's section shifts that frame, and Horror re-bases its stored body-frame points and re-sends E8 in its next run (Horror HE-22 as amended 2026-10-28); until E8 lands the subject reads from its last anchor, the same one-run lag the E8 row accepts *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. The frame each registrant passes is to be confirmed by Interaction and Horror (Open Question 29) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `section_id` | u32 | C (OBJECT, HORROR, ITEM); **D for ACTOR** | the section; an ACTOR's is rewritten every tick in W0 from Body's `actor_section` (derived, never hashed); when `actor_section` answers NONE (a defect-only state, BDY-29) the previous value is kept and `emitter_actor_section_none` increments *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `body_id` | u32 | **D for EVERY subject kind** | OBJECT, ITEM and HORROR: Layout `body_of(section_id)`, re-read as for emitters (EM-4, EM-23 W0); ACTOR: Body `body_of_actor` rewritten every tick in W0 (the NONE rule above is unchanged). Never hashed. HORROR: the registered `section_id` is Horror's at E6; a HORROR whose section leaves its body (a cut) is Horror's to re-register or send to TRANSIT (Horror HE-22 / AC-HE-48 already re-derive `body_id`); the Emitter never guesses a moved horror's section *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `moving` | bool | C | |
| `static_lux_pm`, `static_version` | int | D | the cached static-layer partial sum and the version it was built at |
| `lux_pm`, `act_lux_pm` | int 0..1000 | D | |
| `lit` | bool | C | the hysteresis bit (EM-22): canonical, one bit per subject, snapshotted and hashed |
| `top_emitter_id` | u32 | D | |
| `last_eval_tick` | int | D | |

**EM-22 — `lit(subject)`: threshold, hysteresis and the beacon exception.** `lit(ref)` is a hysteresis filter over `act_lux_pm` (Formulas D.8): it becomes true when `act_lux_pm >= LIT_ON_PM`, becomes false when `act_lux_pm < LIT_OFF_PM`, and holds between. Placeholders `LIT_ON_PM` 150 (range 100–300) and `LIT_OFF_PM` 120 (60–140), the `LIT_THRESHOLD_PM` family **owned by the technical-artist** (art bible 7.0: "the illumination threshold that enables prompt and inspect-card drawing"), confirmed against the rendered edge in the Lighting spike (8.12 step 2). A newly registered subject starts unlit and is evaluated at the next `emitter.place`. A hard-edged rim is a *step* (a full-output rim contributes `EDGE_FLOOR_PM` then 0 across one pixel), which the 150/120 hysteresis never sees; rim chatter while a beam sweeps is held by the Controller's committed-aim quantisation (`AIM_SEND_THRESHOLD` 32 units) and `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN` (PC-6), and the hysteresis bites only for dimmed or soft light (brownout, waning glowsticks, fixture pools). There is **no dwell** (`LIT_OFF_DWELL_TICKS` fixed at 0): keeping `lit` true after the light went would be a window where the sim says lit and the world is dark, a P1 lie. `lit(ref)` and `lux(ref)` are separate answers because the beacon's sweep must not strobe prompts (EM-16). **The hysteresis bit is canonical** (checkpoint ruling CP-2): `lit(ref)` depends on last tick's answer, and a subject whose `act_lux_pm` sits between `LIT_OFF_PM` and `LIT_ON_PM` has no other source for its bit, so by EM-4's own definition the bit is a discrete fact — one bit per registered subject, at most `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` bits, snapshotted and hashed. `post_restore` copies each `lit` bit verbatim and never calls the hysteresis; `lux`, `top_emitter`, `output_of`, `r_lit_of`, `r_off_of` and `section_emission` may differ from the live run's for one run after a restore, while `lit`, `beam_of`, `subject_headroom` and the registration set are exact (ADR-0017 D9.5, D14.4) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*; the next `emitter.place` applies hysteresis normally. A subject saved at 130 permille and lit is therefore lit after the restore, and Interaction's `validate` at T+1 reads the same answer in the live run and the restored run (kernel G4). *(orchestrator ruling: the systems-designer's canonical bitset is the rule. Rejected alternative: rebuild every bit against `LIT_ON_PM` in `post_restore` (the earlier ruling R6) — a subject between the thresholds would flip from lit to unlit across a restore, `validate` at T+1 would accept in one run and reject `NOT_LIT` in the other, and canonical state downstream would diverge.)* Interaction's `validate` and the HUD prompt call this same `lit(ref)`, so the prompt never lies by omission (PC-6).

**EM-23 — The order of work inside `emitter.place`; static and dynamic layers; no subscriptions.** The hook does, in this order and nothing else: **W0** read upstream (`controller.state` and `body.state` for bound seats; the Layout `OccluderSource.version(section_id)`; the Graph's `served_pm` and `node(id)`; on Graph ticks the Graph's `failures(filter)`), and rewrite the derived `section_id` and `body_id` of each BEAM record and ACTOR subject from Body's `actor_section(actor_id)` and `body_of_actor(actor_id)` (a tick in which the foot crosses a door moves the beam's section in that tick) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, AND, for every loaded section whose Layout `static_version(section_id)` differs from the value cached at the last W0 (a derived per-section cache, never hashed, seeded in `post_restore` after the cold build with the current version triple, offset and `body_id` so the first W0 sees no change; an epoch change rebuilds that section's body; ADR-0017 D11.4, CT-45) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*, re-read `body_of(section_id)` and `section_offset(section_id)` and rewrite the derived `body_id` of every record and non-ACTOR subject whose `section_id` is that section (cost `W_FRAME` each, D.14); a change of `body_id` or `section_offset` is a FRAME CHANGE of that section *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; **W1** lifecycle (DEAD force-off, deployable aging, evict-wane (D.9b) and removal, `arm_tick` arming, fault-glow reconciliation); **W2** derive every emitter in ascending id (origin, aim, reach, `output_pm`, record `lit`); **W3** find the static-layer rows whose inputs changed; **W4** update them by deltas, in ascending (emitter id, subject ref), **synchronously — nothing is left pending past W4** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; **W5** the dynamic layer for every subject (with the owner-beam special case of EM-21: the owner's own beam adds its apex value to the owner's ACTOR `lux_pm` with no cone or occlusion test); **W6** saturating sums, hysteresis, write the subject table, record the tick's counted work; **W7** write dirty keys (EM-26). The **static layer** (FIXTURE, NODE_LANTERN, FAULT_GLOW, resting DEPLOYABLE) is cached as **per-pair contribution rows** `(emitter id, subject ref) → {d, f_pm, c}` (a row exists only while the emitter is in radius and unblocked) plus, per subject, an **unsaturated** raw sum `Σ c`, clamped to 1000 only when the answer is read. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The first draft cached a saturated partial sum, which is exact for *adding* an emitter (`min(1000, min(1000, a) + b) = min(1000, a + b)`) but cannot *remove* one (a saturated 1000 has forgotten what it summed), so every output change would have forced a full re-evaluation of the subject; with the pair rows every change is a delta: (i) an emitter's **output** changes (to or from 0, a brownout step, inrush): `c = ⌊output_pm × f_pm / 1000⌋` is recomputed from the stored `f_pm` with no geometry, and an `output_pm` of 0 stores `c` = 0 with no multiply while the rows (the geometry) are kept, so a blackout is a zeroing of rows and not a re-evaluation of the ship, and switching back on is a delta too; (ii) an emitter's integer **reach** changes (a waning stick): `d` and `f_pm` are recomputed for its rows and rows beyond the new reach are dropped, with no occlusion test (reach only shrinks, so no pair is added); (iii) an emitter is **created** (its candidate subjects are tested radius first, then occlusion, and rows are added) or **removed** (its rows are dropped); (iv) a subject is **registered or moved** (it is tested against the static emitters in range); (v) an **occluder** changes (only pairs whose segment bounding box meets the changed region are re-tested, below); (vi) the layer is **cold** (`post_restore`, section instantiate: everything is built); (vii) a **FRAME CHANGE** (a weld or cut, Layout LY-16 / LY-17) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Rows store `d`, `f_pm` and `c` and no coordinates, and a weld or cut moves a whole body by one translation (`rot_q` is 0 in the MVP, Layout D.6 / D.12), so: (a) rows whose emitter and subject lie in the same moved component are KEPT unchanged; (b) rows whose emitter `body_id` differs from the subject `body_id` after re-derivation are DROPPED (a cut: the rows that crossed the severed seam; cost `W_DELTA` each); (c) every subject with a changed `body_id` or on the host side of the seam is tested radius-first against the emitters of the OTHER side that are now on its body, and rows are ADDED (a weld; cost `U_sub` with the cross-seam counts of S8); (d) for each socket-host section the hatch slot set changed (PLATE <-> JAMB + LEAF), so pairs whose segment bounding box meets the changed region are re-tested exactly as for a leaf change (narrowing rule L / D). Layout reports the kind of change per section as `changed_region(section_id)` = ALL, REGION(box) or FRAME: FRAME = translation only, no re-test; REGION = the socket slot union box (a socket-host section that also moves reports REGION, and the Emitter detects its translation by comparing `section_offset(section_id)` with its cached value); ALL (build, discard, restore, or attach / detach if Layout declines REGION / FRAME) = cold rebuild of the affected bodies, priced as S8b. The rebuild stays synchronous (W3 / W4); nothing is pending past W4. Rows are derived, never hashed, rebuilt in ascending (emitter id, subject ref), and the pair-row stride and layout are ADR-0017 D11.1 *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*; a row-table overflow changes cost, never an answer, and increments `emitter_pair_rows_overflow`; the **dynamic layer** (BEAM, BEACON, which rotate or translate every tick) is recomputed per tick with the cheapest rejects first — radius, then cone, then the occlusion test. A subject's static rows are updated when `emitter.place` *observes* a change in what it read last tick: the section's `OccluderSource.version(...)` changed, an emitter's `output_pm` or integer `reach_px` changed, an emitter was created or removed, or the subject moved. **Never by subscription**: kernel CR-10 ("simulation systems never subscribe; sim-to-sim is ordered step reads or commands"); dirty keys are the notification channel for PRESENTATION and friends, not an input to this block. A pose-unchanged skip (a dynamic emitter whose origin, aim, output, shape and occlusion origin equal last tick's skips re-evaluation) is a pure optimisation and changes no answer. A static emitter's cost is paid when something changes, not every tick; a glowstick's reach steps by one px every 50 ticks while it wanes (3600 ticks over 72 px, D.9), so the rows of the subjects in its range are recomputed that often (D.14, S5). **Narrowing rule for door leaves** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `OccluderSource.version(section_id)` says only *that* something in the section changed, and a leaf bump arrives at the Graph's step rate while a door travels (roughly ten per second), so a response that re-tests every pair of the section would cost 11,522 units per bump (D.14, S1: 14 × `T_sub` 823) against `B` 10,000 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The Emitter therefore re-tests only pairs whose segment bounding box meets the changed region: **(L)** Layout publishes the changed region with the bump (ask E-N, a union box of the old and new leaf rectangles; a structural bump — build, discard, restore — reports `ALL`; attach and detach report REGION / FRAME (trigger vii) when Layout supplies them, else `ALL`), or, if Layout declines, **(D)** every pair whose segment bounding box meets a door-leaf pre-filter box of its section is tagged *dynamic* and evaluated every tick in the dynamic layer, which costs the same amount every tick instead of once per bump (priced in S1). Option L is adopted (ADR-0017 D11.5) on ADR-0014's `changed_region`; option D is the named fallback and is not built *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*. After a restore `changed_region` is advisory and `version_epoch` is authoritative (ADR-0017 D11.5, CT-43): Layout's `post_restore` runs first, so the Emitter's cold build already sees the bumped leaves, and the version cache is seeded after it *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*. **The contract is "never stale", and the rebuild is synchronous** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; resolves the question that D.13's age bound left open)*: W3 and W4 finish every update inside the tick, before W6 writes the table, so there is no pending state and the D.13 bound (age 0 or 1 tick) holds for every reader, including Interaction's `validate` at T−1 and its six-tick re-check, Attention and Horror. A tick whose updates exceed the ceiling of D.14 still completes them, increments `emitter_rebuild_over_ceiling` and is a hitch to be removed by content or by the ordered fallbacks, never deferred. **Alternative (b), rejected and recorded as ADR-0017 Alternative 9:** a deterministic counted-unit cap per tick, a widened staleness bound and a fail-safe lit-or-dark rule for pending subjects. It was rejected because (1) `lit` would then depend on a rebuild queue that is derived, not canonical, so a snapshot restored mid-queue (which rebuilds cold and complete) would answer differently from the uninterrupted run — kernel G4 broken in exactly the way the old probe budget broke it (EM-24); (2) a fail-safe-dark rule would blink the prompts of a whole room on every door bump; and (3) it would rewrite the age bound that three designed GDDs already build on. The ceiling's named owner is the technical-director; the exposure spike reports it (AC-53).

**EM-24 — Counted work, not wall-clock; the caps.** The per-tick exposure cost is bounded by **counted work units**, never by timing — a time-based governor would be nondeterministic. The caps (all *placeholder, owner technical-director, confirmed by the exposure spike and the Interaction / Derelict Authoring content counts*; never inherited from a default of 64): `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` 192 (derived from the MVP slice's 14 compartments — home-ship 5, derelict A 4, derelict B 5 — their verb-bearing nodes, doors counted twice for their two face subjects, loose items, actors and horror subjects; Formulas D.14; *orchestrator pick: the systems-designer's 192 over the technical-director's 128, because doors register two subjects (EM-20 e) and the MVP Graph's node count supports a larger interactable set*), `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS` 16 (the 17th moving registration reports `OVER_MOVING_CAP`, increments `emitter_moving_overflow` and the subject reads dark, the same fail-safe as `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS`), `MAX_EMITTERS` 96, `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` 8, `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_EVICTING` 4, `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` 10. **The caps are content budgets, held at data load, not gameplay states** *(CD-GDD-ALIGN concerns 1 and 3)*: the MVP slice has exactly 8 live faults world-wide, of which a welded body carries at most 6 simultaneous glow rows (registry `mvp_fault_allocation`); I-CAPS therefore requires `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` to be at least the most simultaneous glowing authored faults on one body plus 25 % (⌈1.25 × 6⌉ = 8; the cap of 10 keeps 2 of headroom), and requires the **dynamic registrants** that arrive after boot to be held to per-category budgets owned by their authors: Derelict Authoring owns the budget for authored OBJECT and ITEM subjects and for dropped loot and spawned salvage per section, Horror Entities owns the horror-subject budget (`HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` 2 (Horror Entities, registry), all moving; Horror's `horror_subject_count` holds `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` + `A_max` 4 = 6 ≤ `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS`) and the share of `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS` it may use, Interaction owns the dropped-item registrations, and the deployable budget is `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` + `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_EVICTING` (D.14, D.15 I-CAPS (d)). A runtime overflow of any cap is a defect of one of those budgets, is counted by a release-build telemetry counter (`emitter_subjects_overflow`, `emitter_moving_overflow`, `emitter_fault_glow_overcap`, `emitter_fault_glow_evictions`, `emitter_deployable_evictions`, `emitter_deployable_evict_overflow`, and, added by the 2026-10-27 design review, `emitter_create_refused`, `emitter_fault_glow_no_anchor`, `emitter_anchor_clearance_fail`, `emitter_reach_invalid`, `emitter_actor_section_none`, `emitter_pair_rows_overflow`, `emitter_rebuild_over_ceiling`, and, added by the re-review, `emitter_dock_status_missing`, `emitter_invalid_transition` and the playtest counters of the metric stub) as well as asserted in debug, and is never something a design relies on. `EXPOSURE_BUDGET_MS` is **0.4 ms P99** for `emitter.place` plus all exposure evaluation (placeholder, spike on the release export on RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK, delegated ruling R12; the kernel's `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 is a logging threshold and P3's target is ≤ 2.0 ms P99 with the Graph at 0.5 ms and Controller + Body about 0.3 ms). It is the *steady-state* budget; the named rare-event scenarios of D.14 are held to `EXPOSURE_TICK_CEILING_MS` (hot class) and `COLD_REBUILD_CEILING_MS` (cold class), both owned by the technical-director. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Mandatory work (the dynamic layer, static rebuilds, the subject table) is never skipped. **Creation at `MAX_EMITTERS`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the first draft had no run-time rule at the global cap)*: when the record count equals `MAX_EMITTERS` a creation is **refused**, never made by removing a light that has a physical cause. E1 refuses the section's remaining records, `spawn_deployable` reports `REFUSED_CAP` (the item was already spent, Item DB rule (c)), and a FAULT_GLOW reconciliation creates nothing for the lowest-ranked descriptors; each refusal increments `emitter_create_refused` (release telemetry, per kind) and asserts in debug. Within one pass creations are admitted in `priority` order, highest first (BEAM 255, BEACON 200, FIXTURE 120, DEPLOYABLE 100, FAULT_GLOW 80, NODE_LANTERN 60; ties by ascending creation order), so the light the player most needs is the one kept; the seat beams are never refused, because their slots are reserved (I-CAPS (a) counts the bound seats). The cap is a content budget held by I-CAPS (a); the rule exists so that a breach is deterministic, counted and visible, never a gameplay state. **Probes and `lit_by(point)` are deferred to Vertical Slice A** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: no MVP consumer calls them (Horror HE-19 and HE-25 use Layout `room_at` / `room_adjacency` and make no `probe()` call, Attention AT-4 makes none, Sanity no longer uses `lit_static` (deferred to VS-A, EM-25), and Interaction's re-checks are `lit(ref)` lookups), so `probe`, `lit_by`, `MAX_PROBES_PER_TICK`, `W_PROBE`, `probes_allowed` and the probe counter are removed from the MVP read view, budget and acceptance criteria. The first draft derived `probes_allowed` from the previous tick's counted work `T_mand_prev`, which depends on cache hits and skips, is not canonical (absent from EM-28) and is rebuilt differently after `post_restore`, so the same probe could answer OK in a live run and `UNKNOWN` after a restore — a kernel G4 break that the old AC-54 could not detect. **Reserved for VS-A:** if probes return, their budget is a *constant* number per tick that is never derived from counted work, and their signatures take the frame, `probe(body_id, point)` and `lit_by(body_id, point)`, because geometry is compared only within one body (EM-30). The GDScript op cost is not sourceable from the pinned engine reference (no scripting module), so every cost number is a model parameter for the spike; the fallback if the dynamic layer is too slow is a baked per-room illumination grid for the static layer (the brief's option c), then reducing `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS`, then room-gated broadphase (the numeric decision table of D.14 governs the order). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**EM-25 — Query semantics, the read view and the staleness bound.** Public reads are table lookups against the subject table and the emitter records; they are pure, side-effect free and callable from `validate`, from P3 and from presentation prompts, **never from notification handlers that decide state** (kernel C.3 row 7). Because `emitter.place` runs every tick, a reader that does not declare `after: [emitter.place]` sees a value **at most one tick old**; P2 `validate` sees the table committed by tick T-1's `emitter.place`; a hook that declares `after: [emitter.place]` sees the current tick (the routed ruling's correction of the brief's "up to 5 ticks", which was borrowed from the Graph's divisor-6 caveat and does not apply to a divisor-1 hook). Source ages stack on top and are named, not hidden: the beam's committed aim is up to the Controller's tolerance behind the drawn aim; a lamp's brightness follows `served_pm`, which changes only on Graph ticks. Owners of channelled verbs re-check exposure every 6 ticks through the same `lit(ref)` and abort if unlit at a check (PC-7); a single-tick flicker between checks is tolerated. Attention declares `after: [graph.propagate.power, emitter.place]` (cross-divisor `after:` is already in use: `controller.step` is `after: suit.vitals`) and Horror Entities declares `after` both Attention and `emitter.place` (kernel row 9; HE-3), so the former asks are met and AC-60 lints them. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

| Read-view entry point | Returns | Staleness |
|---|---|---|
| `emitters(filter)` / `emitter(id)` | records, ascending id, with derived fields, stamped with the monotone `tick` of the committed table. **The read is served from the live typed columns, which only `emitter.place` writes (no snapshot copy; ADR-0017 D4.2)** *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)* (typed or packed columns; emitter ids and subject refs held in sorted columns, so iteration is ascending id by construction and never by Dictionary insertion order, which is not ascending after a restore; the storage layout is ADR-0017 D13.1: typed scalar getters over packed columns and caller-owned buffers for lists) and returns a read-only view or fills a caller-provided buffer (2D Lighting E-F). **No-retain rule:** a reader may not keep the view, a slice of it or a reference into it beyond the call that obtained it — the live columns are overwritten by `emitter.place` of the next tick (list reads fill a caller-owned buffer, ADR-0017 D13.1) — and the lint forbids storing it in a field. A GDScript read that cannot meet "copy-free, read-only, no allocation" as plain `Dictionary` code is why the layout is an ADR and not an implementation choice *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | current if `after: emitter.place`, else ≤ 1 tick |
| `beam_of(player_id)` | the seat's beam id | exact |
| `beam_bindings()` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | a flat `PackedInt64Array` `[player_id, beam_id, ...]` in ascending `player_id` delivered by the descriptor's `binding_source` (ADR-0017 D3.5, ADR-0016) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*: the kernel binding source registered at BOOTING (kernel CR-7); a pure read of `emitter.state`, so the kernel's LOADING rebuild can bind the seat beams after every `post_restore()` | exact |
| `beam_spill(beam_id)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | the foot spill's derived record while the beam is on and `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` > 0, else null: `{beam_id, kind BEAM, shape_id 11, origin_px (foot origin), reach_px, r_lit_px, r_off_px, curve_class SOFT, output_pm, hue_class, section_id, body_id, priority}` (EM-26) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Not a stored emitter, not in `emitters()`, not in `section_emission`; the no-retain rule applies as for `emitters()` | as `emitters()` (the committed table) |
| `lux(ref)` / `lit(ref)` / `top_emitter(ref)` | `lux_pm` 0..1000 / bool / emitter id (unregistered ref: 0 / false / none) | as above |
| `lit_by(body_id, point)` / `probe(body_id, point)` | **not in the MVP read view — deferred to Vertical Slice A** (EM-24). The names are reserved; a call in MVP source is a lint error. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | — |
| `section_emission(section_id)` | integer (D.12), read from a **precomputed per-section array accumulated in W2 and made current in W7** (O(1) per call; the first draft summed over all emitters per call, O(E); a late-created emitter is absent from it until the next run, the same one-tick lag as the subject table; ADR-0017 D4.2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)* | as above |
| `subject_headroom()` | `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` − registered subjects (int ≥ 0); Interaction's `drop` / `place_loose` need it above 0 | as above |
| `emitters_near(body_id, px, r)` (name provisional) | ids of emitters on `body_id` whose origin lies within `r` px of `px` (inclusive, `d²` ≤ `r²`), ascending id; a filter of the records, not a probe (Horror HE-35 L3 disturbance, C-HE-15) | as above *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `lit_static(ref)` **— deferred to VS-A; not in the MVP read view** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | bool: the subject's static-layer `lux_pm` alone (fixtures, node lanterns, fault glow, resting deployables; EM-23 cache) is at or above `LIT_ON_PM`; stateless, no hysteresis; unregistered ref: false. *The MVP has no consumer: Sanity's per-visit rule (SN-16, design review) dropped it. The definition is kept here so that a VS-A consumer inherits it; the MVP build neither defines nor calls it, and AC-30's lint adds `lit_static(` to its banned call forms.* | as above |
| `shapes()` | the shape table (EM-8) | static |
| `registered(ref)` | bool — debug builds only | exact |
| ~~`block_hash()`~~ **not exposed by the owner** (ADR-0005 section 1: `canonical_bytes()` and `block_hash()` on an owner become kernel-side reads; an owner that wants to expose "its hash" forwards to the kernel read and computes nothing, and this block exposes neither) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)* | the kernel's `block_hash(&"emitter.state", uncached = true)`, i.e. `H64(canonical_encode(snapshot payload))` (ADR-0005 section 4.3) | exact |

**EM-26 — Dirty keys, the Lighting contract and the budget agreement.** Dirty keys are emitted for **emitters**, never for individual subjects (publish rule): a key `E/<emitter id>` — an int `(KIND << 32) | id` as in Graph LS-20 — fires on create, remove, `switch_on` flip, `shape_id` change, an `output_pm` change that crosses a bucket (`OUTPUT_DIRTY_QUANTUM_PM`, placeholder 50) and an integer `reach_px` step; continuous pose (origin, aim) never dirties — Lighting polls the read view at render rate, and keys wake only discrete presentation (a light switching on or off, a record spawning). Subject `lux` and `lit` are poll reads: sim consumers read inside their hook, presentation prompts poll at render rate, so a sweeping beam over 192 subjects cannot collapse notifications to `ALL` against `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256 (worst case here is every emitter, at most `MAX_EMITTERS` 96, in one tick). **The Lighting contract** (2D Lighting & Occlusion reads and never writes): per emitter `{id, kind, subkind, shape_id, origin_px, aim_units, half_angle_units, reach_px, r_lit_px, r_off_px, curve_class, output_pm, hue_class, lit, section_id, body_id, priority}` plus the shape table (`r_lit_px` and `r_off_px` are the sixteenth and seventeenth fields, the former added by the 2026-10-27 design review and the latter by the 2026-10-28 cross-GDD fix; 2D Lighting's "15-field" wording is refreshed, Cross-References). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* **The foot spill is published by a separate accessor** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the 17-field record describes a stored emitter and cannot represent the spill (a sub-contribution of the BEAM record centred on the foot, EM-6). `beam_spill(beam_id)` returns, while the beam is on and `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` > 0, the derived record `{beam_id, kind BEAM, shape_id 11, origin_px (the foot origin `occ_origin`), reach_px (`BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX`), r_lit_px (the D.7 chain), r_off_px (the same chain at `LIT_OFF_PM`), curve_class SOFT, output_pm (`BEAM_SPILL_PM`), hue_class (the beam's), section_id, body_id, priority (the owning beam's, BEAM default 255)}` and null otherwise; it is not a record, has no id of its own and is **not in `emitters()`** (so `section_emission`, Attention's list and the 96-record cap never see it), and its changes dirty the beam's own key `E/<beam id>`. Its `kind` is the owning beam's BEAM and its `priority` is the beam's (255 by default), so it is never cut before occlusion and never demoted (Ask E-L accepted 2026-10-28); it is read by Lighting only through `beam_spill(beam_id)`, it is a row of `shapes()` (row 11, conditional) but not of `emitters()`. Lighting draws the pool from its `origin_px` and `r_off_px` (edge) and `r_lit_px` (approach prompt), and the build lint of AC-56 checks the drawn edge against max(`r_off_px`, `POOL_RESIDUAL_PX`) ± 1 px (`POOL_RESIDUAL_PX` is Lighting's, 8 px) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, which is what lets "what you see lit is what the sim calls lit" be tested for the spill (AC-46c). This block publishes facts; Lighting conforms to them. Lighting owns textures, ambient, the canvas modulate, shadow-slot assignment (the sixth slot), the flash clamp (`flash_count` ≤ 3 per rolling second per light), flicker, gutter and decoy visuals; flicker is invisible to the query, which uses the steady mean. **Budget agreement (R17):** the sim never culls by view (an off-screen emitter still exposes — correct for P3); instead authoring lint keeps in-view emitter counts under Lighting's hypotheses (shadow-casting ≤ 6, non-shadow ≤ 24, node lanterns ≤ 8; art bible 8.10 budget shape and 8.11 flags 2 and 8), a debug assertion reports overflow, and every record carries a deterministic `priority` (default by kind: BEAM 255, BEACON 200, FIXTURE 120, DEPLOYABLE 100, FAULT_GLOW 80, NODE_LANTERN 60 — placeholder, owner technical-artist, confirmed by the Lighting spike) so Lighting's "emitters are cut before occlusion" is reproducible; a sim-lit but unrendered light would be a P1 lie, and the residual risk stays in Open Questions. A build lint asserts the rendered reach of every hard-edged row equals the table reach ±1 px.

**EM-27 — The light stimulus for Attention.** The primary read is the per-emitter list (`emitters()`: id, kind, `section_id`, `body_id`, origin, `output_pm`, `reach_px`, shape); the convenience read is `section_emission(section_id)` (Formulas D.12). Attention owns units and weights. The stimulus is **shape-blind by design** (D.12: a narrow beam reads about 2.3 times louder than the fan; default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20), D25) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **No double counting:** the Graph's `power_use(section_id).lamps_lit_w` is the *power* stimulus; this block's `output_pm`-weighted reach is the *light* stimulus; Attention must not count one lamp as both. The owner's own beam counts once, at its apex value, in the owner's `lux_pm` (EM-21); Attention reads it as emission and does not add it again. Off-screen and unoccupied emitters count. The seat's beam is attributed to the section its foot origin stands in (EM-4, Body's `actor_section`), and the attribution changes in the tick the foot crosses a door, so Attention's per-section ledger and Horror's targeting see the player's exposure where the player is. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Attention may weight a pool by area rather than radius (the list carries `reach_px` and the shape). (Attention is designed: AT-4 reads `emitters()` and no `probe()`, AT-18 concurs on the beacon.) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**EM-28 — Persistence, restore and the binding table.** *Canonical* (snapshotted, hashed): the id counter; per record, the canonical fields of EM-4 (including the FAULT_GLOW records, which are the descriptors they stand for, with their `placed_tick`, the age that protects them from eviction; `arm_tick`; a beam's `last_toggle_tick` (the only source of the `TOO_SOON` strobe guard, EM-7); and a deployable's `evict_tick` and `reach_at_evict`, so an evict-wane in progress survives a save); and the subject registrations including each `lit` hysteresis bit (EM-21 table, canonical columns, EM-22), plus the pending-intent columns (emitter `pending`; subject `pending`, `has_pend_anchor`, `pend_x` / `pend_y`), `last_place_tick` and `fault_step_seen` (ADR-0017 D2.3, D2.5) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*; no record and no subject stores `body_id` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. *Derived, rebuilt in `post_restore`, never hashed:* beam origin and aim, `origin_px` / `aim_units` / `reach_px` / `output_pm` / record `lit`, every record's and every subject's `body_id` (Layout `body_of`; Body `body_of_actor` for the beam and ACTOR subjects), a beam's and an ACTOR subject's `section_id`, the per-section `static_version` cache of EM-23 W0 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, the static-layer pair rows and each subject's unsaturated raw sum, the per-section emission array (EM-25; there is no read-view snapshot copy, ADR-0017 D4.2), and the subject `lux_pm` / `act_lux_pm` / `top_emitter_id` table (the subject's `lit` bit is not rebuilt: it is copied verbatim and the hysteresis is never called in `post_restore`, ADR-0017 D9.5). A snapshot taken before a weld and restored into a Layout that is already welded (Layout restores first, `restore_after`) yields body ids from Layout, never from the snapshot *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Rejected: `persist false` (`switch_on`, `life_remaining_ticks`, `arm_tick` and the beacon's data have no other source, so save-equivalence would break). `post_restore` runs after all blocks restore (CR-8) and rebuilds the derived set in ascending id, including the whole static layer (the cold class of D.14, scenario S6) over the **applied** view derived at `last_place_tick` over end-of-tick upstream state; rows pending at the snapshot stay pending until W3 of the first run at T+1, as in the live run (ADR-0017 D2.5, D14.2) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*; the block does not run its hook while RESTORING. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The kernel states (ADR-0016 section 4.1) that `post_restore` calls follow `restore_after`, so Layout's `derive_doors` pass (its `post_restore`, Layout LY-26 c) has run before the Emitter's cold build and the cold build is correct for open doors; Open Question 26 is granted *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0016 sync)*, and no restore-time re-test of the leaf pairs is needed on tick 1 (the version cache is seeded after the cold build, EM-23 W0). After a restore the kernel's binding table is rebuilt: the kernel's LOADING rebuild re-binds the seat beams from `beam_bindings()` before the first RUNNING tick (KRN-BIND-01), so there is no window in which `toggle_beam` meets an empty table *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Save-equivalence (kernel G4) holds with no accepted exception: the `lit` bit is canonical (EM-22), and the hook is ordered after the Graph's last hook on a Graph tick (EM-1). It also depends on one **requirement on the Graph**, now met: `served_pm` is a persisted hook product (`n_served`) read as-is after restore, and its `post_restore` rebuilds only the descriptor list and derived caches (ADR-0011 §6.1; Graph LS-21; Cross-References) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0011 sync)*, without which lamps would read dark and fault glows would be absent for up to `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS - 1` ticks after a load. AC-41 fails if a regression removes the rebuild. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `schema_version` 1; the restore migration policy is the owner's (kernel CR-8: an unsupported version is a reported failure, never a silent default). The block has a round-trip test (kernel CR-8) and a `state_hash` that covers the canonical set only.

**EM-29 — Isolation: the truth channel.** `emitter.state` is a `SIM` block. The module **never obtains a `PERCEPTION_OUT` view** (kernel CR-12; a lint mirroring PC's acceptance criterion on the Adapter's requested views), never references `Light2D`, `LightOccluder2D`, `Node` or `PhysicsServer2D` (the H10 lint over `src/sim/emitter/`), and runs under a headless test with zero scene nodes.
