The beam, the real reveal radius and lamp / fixture brightness are on the closed **consequence channel** (the Graph's closed consequence-channel list names "lamp brightness"; the Controller's Perception Filter row: the Filter "may never move the chest point ... or touch the beam"). **A Perception Filter decoy light is a Lighting visual**: it is not an emitter, is absent from `emitter.state`, and exposes nothing; rejected: decoys as `phantom = true` emitters (a perception fact written into a `SIM` block breaks CR-12 and H5). No sentence in this GDD says the Filter may dim, move or add a lamp. The honest cross-check — walk to the real edge and it is where it always was — depends on this. Isolation is by construction, not by policy: no field named `phantom`, `perceived` or `decoy` exists, and the query never takes a Filter-wrapped value (the Graph's `true_value` is the only wrapped path and Emitter State never calls it).

**EM-30 — Frames, zoom, mirror and the determinism contract.** All geometry is integer base px; stored anchors are **section-local** (Layout's frame, LY-3 c) and are composed with `section_offset(section_id)` once per derive, and every comparison runs in the **body frame** (ship-local); an emitter and a subject are compared only if their `body_id` matches. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The `body_id` of every emitter and subject is derived from Layout (EM-4), so all sections welded into one body share one `body_id`: light crosses a weld, the beam lights the objects of a welded derelict and its lamps count toward the player, exactly as Lighting draws it; sections that are not welded (an unwelded derelict, a docked pod) are other bodies and stay dark to each other. A subject on another body (an unwelded or cut-off section, a pod) is not lit by the home frame's emitters (cross-body light between unwelded bodies is VS-A, Open Question 9) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Zoom, camera, render scale and the integer ladder change **nothing** in the sim; an off-screen lit object stays lit and exposes, and no prompt is drawn off-screen (the HUD's rule). Light positions are integer base px at 60 Hz (`TICK_RATE`); every duration is in ticks via `ticks_for_seconds`. The determinism contract mirrors the Graph's LS-1: integer-only SIM state; **zero floats** stored, hashed, replicated or used on the query path (a designer float in a data file is converted once by `int(round(x * scale))` in the build-time data tool outside `src/sim/emitter/`, which emits the immutable integer direction table `DIR` (`dir_table.gd`, ADR-0017 D1.3; the EM-8 `COS2_Q28` column is not emitted but derived by the `ShapeCatalog` freeze from it, D1.2) that the boot step reads and asserts; they are not block state, and no float or trig call exists in the sim module, AC-2); the Q14 direction table, the Q28 cone test, an exact integer floor square root and integer segment-versus-rectangle tests; no global RNG (the block draws none); iteration in ascending id; budgets in counted units; `state_hash` over the canonical set only. Physics-derived observations never enter this block, so the kernel's G3 recording obligation does not apply.

**MVP slice.** MVP (single-player): one seat beam; the home-ship's fixtures (count per room fixed by Derelict Authoring DA-22, registry `mvp_fixture_counts`: home 8, AC-58), derelict A (4 compartments) and B (5) fixtures and node lanterns; the self-powered cryo-bay emergency lamp and one self-powered DOCK_STATUS glow per dock room (X2); the beam's 24 px foot spill (D24); one alarm beacon; glowsticks (`MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` 8); FAULT_GLOW for the MVP faults with a glow row (`1/FUEL`, `1/GAS`, `2`, `3`, `7`, `9`). VS-A: flares, torches, decoys (`POOL_DEPLOYABLE`), more fixtures, cross-body light. VS-B: four beams, `SHARED_SUMMARY` replication, the corpse-lantern question. Nothing else in this GDD is gated.

### States and Transitions

Three small machines live in this block — the emitter record, the subject's `lit` bit and the block itself — plus one table that says what the beam does in each of the Controller's locomotion states. Every pair not listed is invalid and is reported to diagnostics (counter `emitter_invalid_transition`, labelled with the from and to states; AC-109), never silently applied. "Who may cause" is exhaustive: a transition no row names does not exist. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**Emitter record lifecycle.** `output_pm` is 0 in every state except ON and WANING.

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions (to) | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ABSENT | no record exists: never created, or removed | a creation | → OFF (BEAM, FIXTURE, BEACON created switched off; a DOCK_STATUS glow is always created OFF); → ARMING (a Graph-powered FIXTURE or BEACON whose placement is authored `switch_on` true: E1 queues M14 and sets `arm_tick` in the same call, see the transition table); → ON (a self-powered EMERGENCY fixture authored on, no ARMING; DEPLOYABLE on landing; a live NODE_LANTERN at instantiate; FAULT_GLOW on reconciliation) | E9 `create_seat_beam`; E1 `instantiate_section_emitters`; E3 `spawn_deployable`; `emitter.place` (FAULT_GLOW only) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| OFF | `switch_on` false | a switch-on, or removal | → ON (BEAM, same tick, if Suit's charge view is above zero; the self-powered EMERGENCY lamp, same tick, no ARMING, when a `set_switch` turns it on; a DOCK_STATUS glow, same tick, no ARMING); → ARMING (FIXTURE, BEACON, when the node is structurally present); → STARVED (a `PLAYER` ON on a FAILED, DESTROYED or dead-bus node, see the transition table); → ABSENT (section discard) | `toggle_beam` (the seat's PlayerId via `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`); `set_switch` (Interaction's declared verb, or `SYSTEM`); `set_beacon` (Attention, `SYSTEM`); E10 `set_dock_status` (Web Map, DOCK_STATUS only); E2 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| ARMING | lamp kinds only: `switch_on` true and `T < arm_tick` (EM-12) | `T >= arm_tick`, a switch-off, or removal | → ON (`served_pm > 0` and live); → STARVED (`served_pm` 0 or not live); → OFF; → ABSENT | the clock (`emitter.place` W1); `set_switch` / `set_beacon` off; E2. A lamp cannot be lit while ARMING |
| ON | `output_pm > 0`: the emitter is emitting | `served_pm` falls to 0; the node stops being live; a switch-off; `force_off`; the DEAD tick; a cap eviction (deployable, starts the evict-wane, not a removal); removal | → STARVED; → OFF (immediate; M14 `(node_ref, 0)` for lamps); → WANING (a deployable past its steady portion, or one a cap eviction selected); → ABSENT | the Graph's state through reads (no one writes it); `toggle_beam` / `set_switch` / `set_beacon` off; E5 `force_off` (Suit, or Emitter at DEAD); E2 |
| STARVED | the emitter's switch (or derivation) says on but `output_pm` is 0: `served_pm` 0 (brownout to zero, SCRAM), the node not live (EM-13), or a lantern's machine not running; `switch_on` is unchanged | power restored, node live again, machine running; a switch-off; removal | → ON (no new command needed); → OFF; → ABSENT | the Graph's state through reads; `set_switch` off; E2 |
| WANING | a deployable whose remaining life fraction has fallen below `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM / 1000`; `reach_px` shrinking (D.9) | `life_remaining_ticks` reaches 0; the evict-wane ends (D.9b); discard | → ABSENT (record removed, id retired, no fade — the waning already took the reach to 24 px). A cap eviction (E3) does not remove: it sets `evict_tick` and the stick stays WANING on the faster D.9b curve, entering it from ON as well as from steady | the clock; E3 (starts the evict-wane, never removes); E2 |

| From | To | Guard | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| ABSENT | OFF / ARMING / ON | creation mutator ran inside a `WriteScope` | record with a fresh id; every derived field populated (EM-5); dirty key `E/<id>`. **E1 only, for a Graph-powered FIXTURE or BEACON placement authored `switch_on` true** (the new-game home-ship lamp): `switch_on` true, state ARMING, M14 `set_load_request(node_ref, req_w)` queued (the same call the OFF → ARMING row makes), `arm_tick` set by D.2 with `T_a` = the creation tick, or for an INIT-scope creation at `init_world` the first RUNNING tick (so the new-game lamp has `arm_tick` equal to the first RUNNING tick on which the power hook runs, ADR-0017 D5.2) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*, and `last_toggle_tick` written by `emitter.place` at `arm_tick` like any realised ON; a self-powered EMERGENCY authored on goes straight to ON; a DOCK_STATUS glow is created OFF whatever the authoring says and is switched on only by E10; E1 reads no other system's state, `pod_at` included *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| OFF | ON | BEAM: `toggle_beam` applied, charge view above zero | `switch_on` true; `emitter.place` this tick computes `output_pm` |
| OFF | ON | a self-powered EMERGENCY fixture (`node_ref` 0) switched on by `set_switch` | `switch_on` true, no M14 call and no ARMING, `output_pm` = its fixed `base_pm` from the apply tick; `last_toggle_tick` = the apply tick (D.2b: `commit_tick` = `T_a` for a self-powered fixture) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| OFF | ARMING | `set_switch` / `set_beacon` applied, node structurally present (EM-7), **node_ref non-zero** | `switch_on` true; M14 `set_load_request(node_ref, req_w)` queued to the next Graph phase 0; `arm_tick` set (D.2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| ARMING | ON or STARVED | `T >= arm_tick` | ON if `served_pm > 0` and live, else STARVED |
| ON, ARMING, STARVED | OFF | an off command, `force_off`, or the DEAD tick | immediate: `switch_on` false, `output_pm` 0; M14 `(node_ref, 0)` for lamps |
| OFF | STARVED | a `PLAYER` `set_switch` ON accepted on a FAILED, DESTROYED or dead-bus node (accepted by EM-7: the player gets no oracle) | `switch_on` true, M14 queued as for any ON, `arm_tick` set (D.2); at `arm_tick` the state is STARVED (`output_pm` 0, `switch_on` retained) and the lamp lights on repair with no command (EM-13). The realised ON writes `last_toggle_tick` = `arm_tick`, because the switch gate `switch_on ∧ armed` changed whatever the node does afterwards (the guard measures the submitter's transitions, not the world's, EM-7 rule 2); on a structurally gone node the M14 call is a no-op and the state is STARVED from the apply tick *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| ON | STARVED and back | `served_pm` or liveness changes | `output_pm` 0 or restored; `switch_on` unchanged |
| ON (deployable) | WANING | `life_remaining_ticks * 1000 < GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM * life_total_ticks`, or a cap eviction selected the stick (E3 writes `evict_tick`) | `reach_px` follows D.9, or D.9b after an eviction *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| WANING, ON (deployable) | ABSENT | `life_remaining_ticks` = 0, the end of the evict-wane (D.9b — never the eviction itself, which only starts the wane), or `discard_section_emitters` | record removed; subjects it lit re-read next tick *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the first draft's guard listed "eviction" as a removal, contradicting the WANING row above)* |
| any (FAULT_GLOW) | ABSENT | the descriptor is absent at the next Graph-tick reconciliation, or cap eviction | removed |
| any | ABSENT | `discard_section_emitters(section_id)` | removed with its section |

**The beam in each Controller locomotion state** (`controller.state.loco_state`; the Controller's table owns the states, this block owns what the beam does).

| `loco_state` | Beam | Origin / aim | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| IDLE, WALK, RUN, CARRY_WALK | available; follows committed `switch_on` | chest point of that animation row; committed `aim_angle` | the chest lamp is hands-free (PC-8) |
| CHANNELLING | available | facing is locked, but the chest lamp still follows aim (PC-4 rule 2) | the owner re-checks `lit` every 6 ticks |
| STAGGERED | unchanged | frozen | `toggle_beam` rejected `NOT_ACTIONABLE` (defensive) |
| DOWNED | **unchanged** — a downed crewmate is a locator | chest point of the down row; aim frozen at the last committed value | `toggle_beam` rejected `NOT_ACTIONABLE` |
| DEAD | **forced off** at the tick the state is read (`force_off`, a read, no command) | — | unobservable in the MVP: the death stub restarts from the last autosave at that tick's P6 |
| IN_POD | follows its actor into the pod's frame | the pod body's frame (EM-30) | (Ship Body and Web Map & Transit are designed; `body_of_actor` carries the frame) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **`toggle_beam` is accepted in IN_POD**: PC-9 ignores only locomotion there (a `set_aim` is `Applied`, the lamp keeps following the hand) and lists no beam-input exemption, so `NOT_ACTIONABLE` is not returned; the commit-spacing guard and `CHARGE_EMPTY` apply as everywhere, and Suit's statuses run in the pod (D60), so the beam drains charge there like anywhere *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

**Subject `lit` bit.**

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions (to) | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UNREGISTERED | no table row; `lux` 0, `lit` false, indistinguishable from UNLIT in release | `register_subject` | → UNLIT | E6 by Interaction, Horror, loot spawners, the actor boot |
| UNLIT | registered (starts here), or `act_lux_pm < LIT_OFF_PM` while LIT | `act_lux_pm >= LIT_ON_PM`; unregistration | → LIT; → UNREGISTERED | `emitter.place` W6 (a read of the emitters); E7 / E2 |
| LIT | `act_lux_pm >= LIT_ON_PM` | `act_lux_pm < LIT_OFF_PM`; unregistration | → UNLIT; → UNREGISTERED. Between `LIT_OFF_PM` and `LIT_ON_PM` the bit holds, across a restore too (the bit is canonical, EM-22) | `emitter.place` W6; E7 / E2 |

**Block lifecycle.**

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions (to) | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EMPTY | `default_state()`: no records, counter at 1, no subjects | boot creation of the seat beam, or a restore | → LIVE | new-game LOADING (E9) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; the kernel on restore |
| LIVE | after boot creation or a successful restore; the hook runs, mutators accept | a restore request; a kernel fault | → RESTORING; → FAULTED | kernel control plane (`request_restore`); a structural-invariant failure in any build |
| RESTORING | two-phase restore in progress; the hook does not run | `post_restore` completes (derived tables rebuilt, EM-28) | → LIVE | the kernel (CR-8) |
| FAULTED | kernel state after a failed `apply` (CR-9) or a numeric assert in debug: hooks and mutators stop | a new world or a restore of the last good snapshot | → EMPTY / LIVE | the kernel owns the exit; this block never transitions itself out of FAULTED. Every mutator here is Class B or validated, so a fault is a defect, never a rejection |

Mutators called outside a `WriteScope` assert in debug and are rejected in release (CR-9).

### Interactions with Other Systems

*Status key. **designed** — the counterpart GDD exists and its own Interactions row is quoted below with a consistency verdict. **designed (sibling)** — the counterpart is a sibling GDD of this run with `Status: Designed` on disk (the Item & Module Database, Save/Load & Versioned Format and Failure & Trail); its own Emitter row is quoted below with a consistency verdict. **in flight** — reserved for a counterpart whose draft file exists on disk but is not yet Designed; no counterpart holds it as of 2026-10-26. **provisional** — the counterpart has no GDD file yet (undesigned; its sibling stage is running); the contract is what this GDD offers and asks, and each row says what the other GDD must repeat. A dependent's internal state is never guessed here: each row lists only what the dependent must supply or may read. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: Layout, Ship Body, Suit, Interaction, 2D Lighting, Attention, Horror and Derelict Authoring were undesigned at first draft and are all Status Designed now; their rows below say "designed" and name what the counterpart still owes.)*
