| Perception Filter | nothing | nothing it may change; a decoy overlay drawn by Lighting on mask bit 3 from the Filter's per-viewer decoy list (LO-21); not a modifier, absent from `emitter.state` | the closed consequence-channel list | presentation | designed (kernel CR-12) |
| Sanity & Hallucination Rules | nothing | `lit(ref)` and `lux` only at the MVP; `lit_static(ref)` is deferred to VS-A because SN-16's per-visit rule no longer uses it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (Sanity does not use `lit_by(point)`); hallucinated lights are presentation | the closed list | — | designed; darkness is not to be a sanity cost: ruled 2026-10-27 (Open Question 4, user ruling, kept) and a hard constraint on Sanity & Hallucination *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| Adaptive Audio & Tells | nothing | dirty keys `E/<id>` on switch and output changes (switch click, lamp hum, glowstick hiss are Audio's); `emitters()` `output_pm`, the contract Audio AA-20 reads to know whether the BEACON emitter is live (`output_pm` > 0) | Audio owns sound; it reads no exposure | presentation | designed (`adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`) |
| HUD & Suit Layer / In-World Instruments | nothing | `lit(ref)` gates the inspect prompt and the inspect card; the HUD anchors with `world_to_hud`; a running loop's housing pip is a non-readable find-aid on the emissive layer and does not make an instrument lit (In-World Instruments IW-4, K-6) | the HUD | presentation | provisional |
| Debug Overlay (`DIAGNOSTIC`) | nothing | may draw cones, occlusion segments, subject exposure, `unknown_subject_queries`; absent from release | read-only | — | designed (`debug-overlay-true-values.md`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Save / Load & Versioned Format | nothing | the block contract only (EM-28) | the kernel registry | P6 snapshot at a tick boundary | **designed (sibling)** — SL-25 lists `emitter.state` with the identical `restore_after` (consistent); it registers no blocks of its own |
| Web Map & Transit (MVP) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X2)* | `instantiate_section_emitters` / `discard_section_emitters` (E1, E2); E10 `set_dock_status(section_id, on)` on the dock room's DOCK_STATUS glow, in the apply that writes `pod_at` (on where the pod parks: `init_world` step 7, `pod_settle`, the T5 reversal; off where it leaves: the departure apply) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | nothing new; `lit(ref)` for the pod console subject, satisfied by the glow | Web Map owns berth and unberth timing and the `pod_hop` verb's reasons; Emitter State owns the glow record (EM-11) and the commit-spacing guard | in Web Map's atomic apply (P2) | designed (`web-map-and-transit.md`): its ASK-EM-1 / OQ-WM-39 is answered by EM-3 and EM-11; the matching Web Map text (ASK-BS-W2 answer reversed, ASK-BS-W3 accepted, AC-WM-127, AC-WM-128) is its own |
| Web Map & Transit / Co-op (VS-B) | — | the beam of a remote crewmate replicated from `emitter.state` (`SHARED_SUMMARY`), never coarsened below the Controller's facing hysteresis (PC-17) | Co-op Session | `MIRROR` kernels never tick `emitter.place` | VS-B |

**Counterpart rows, quoted, and consistency verdicts.** The three designed upstream counterparts, the three designed siblings (Failure & Trail, Item DB and Save/Load, items 4 to 6) name this system in their own words; the bidirectional check is below.

1. **Kernel, C.3 row 7** — "Emitter block (`SIM`, `persist`) and its set/toggle/aim commands (Interaction, Suit and deployables submit *those*; nobody writes emitters directly); exposure query as a pure read | Read views for Lighting; time base | Emitter State | The query touches physics space (…): callable from validate, P3 and presentation prompts, never from notification handlers that decide state. Per-tick query budget → Section D. Lighting never writes emitters" (the elided parenthetical is quoted in full in the Cross-References refresh row). **Consistent** on the block, the persistence, the pure read, the call sites, "nobody writes emitters directly" and "Lighting never writes emitters"; the per-tick budget the kernel deferred is EM-24. **Wording refresh owed to the kernel** (Cross-References): "set/toggle/aim commands" is now `toggle_beam`, `set_switch`, `set_beacon` — aim is a *read* of `controller.state` (PC-5); the query's physics-space wording is now integer footprint tests (EM-20). Kernel row 4 (Suit): "Deployables-as-emitters go through Emitter State's commands" — **consistent**: `spawn_deployable` is the published mutator the deployable path calls (E3). Kernel row 9 (Horror): "hook `after` Attention and Emitter exposure; ... light disturbance exposed as state Lighting reads" — **consistent** (EM-25, Horror row above). Kernel row 13 (Attention): "Ordered reads of Emitter exposure, Graph and Suit" — **consistent** (EM-27).
2. **Graph, Interactions table** — "Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query | M14 `set_load_request` per lamp (on/off, dim) | `served_pm(node_id)` for brightness | Graph publishes M14; Emitter State calls it | Emitter runs at divisor 1; requests are queued to the next Graph phase 0 | designed; routed note 'Emitter State calls `set_load_request`'", and the Graph's Dependencies downstream row — "`served_pm(node_id)` for lamp brightness (a browned-out lamp is dimmer — P1); `power_use(section_id)` | `set_load_request` (lamp on/off, machine on/off) | reads after `.power` | Yes". **Consistent**: EM-11 calls M14 per lamp and reads `served_pm`; the Graph's "(on/off, dim)" is read here as on/off only — a lamp is dimmed by the Graph's brownout through `served_pm`, never by a requested fraction (the Emitter requests the rated draw or 0). `power_use` is read by Attention, not here (EM-27). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The one ask and the one requirement this paragraph made on the Graph are **both answered** by its 2026-10-27 consistency pass: (i) `node(id).run_pm` is published (LS-19 additions) with the definition EM-14 asked for, and it is not the Graph's D.9 `duty_pm`; (ii) `served_pm` is a persisted hook product read as-is after restore and the Graph's `post_restore` rebuilds only the failure-descriptor list and derived caches (ADR-0011 §6.1; LS-21) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0011 sync)*, so lamps and fault glows are correct on the first presented frame and the `served_pm_last` fallback is not needed; and assumption A1 (an M14 call applied in P2 of a Graph tick is consumed by that tick's phase 0) is confirmed in the Graph's Interactions row for this system. The Graph's downstream row ("reads after `.power`") is a wording refresh owed to "after `.failure`" (Cross-References).
3. **Controller, Interactions row 4** — "Adapter → Emitter (`toggle_beam`); Controller → Emitter (committed aim, chest point — the beam origin) | The beam is an emitter; `emitter.place` `after: body.integrate`; the light changes only on committed state (PC-5); the exposure query gates focus, prompts and actions (PC-6) | provisional". **Consistent** (EM-6, EM-7, EM-22). The Controller's own Dependencies row — "The beam toggle/aim commands (the beam is an emitter owned there) | — | No — add edge" — is **stale in two places**: aim is a read, not a command, and the index already lists Player Controller & Input in this system's Depends On (a refresh owed to the Controller, listed in Cross-References).

4. **Failure & Trail, FT-27 and Interactions row 7** — "This system writes no light node and registers no emitter and no exposure. It supplies a descriptor-to-glow data table (palette hex, radius in base px, flicker class bounded by FT-29) ... Emitter State registers the emitter from the same descriptor and Lighting renders it (designed; EM-17). The emissive layer on scorch, arc and ember stamps is an unshaded additive layer that casts no light." **Consistent** with EM-17 (the derived FAULT_GLOW kind; the emissive overlay exposes nothing). FT-27 leaves open "whether such a visible emissive element counts toward the web's exposure" and "an exposure-ledger column for [Emitter State] to fill": this GDD answers that the *FAULT_GLOW emitter* counts as exposure (a lit-but-unregistered source would light a room Attention cannot see) and the *overlay* does not, and Failure & Trail's V/A.6 carries the ledger values, which D.11 multiplies (EM-17). Note F&T's table also carries a flicker class: flicker is Lighting's and invisible to the query.

5. **Item & Module Database (designed), its Emitter State row (`item-and-module-database.md` line 470, repeated at line 862)** — "`emits {shape_id, base_pm, hue_class}` on **any** glowing def (`mod_lamp`, `mod_reactor_cell`, `mod_coolant_pump`, `cons_glowstick`), `lantern_on_states` (default `{0 OK, 1 DEGRADED}`), the glowstick parameters (F-7) | the `emits` record (kernel row 7) | The Item DB owns the numbers and the field shape; Emitter State owns the instance, the semantics and the curve evaluation." **Consistent** on the field shape, the ownership split, the glowstick key `POOL_GLOWSTICK` (its line 259), `emits` on any glowing def (its lines 145 and 169), the `lantern_on_states` default (its line 170) and F-7, which this GDD's D.9 reproduces tick for tick. No shape key remains open; its rows 470 and 862 call this GDD designed.

6. **Save/Load & Versioned Format (designed), SL-25 (`save-load-and-versioned-format.md` line 1006; block table lines 276 and 471)** — "`emitter.state` fields and `restore_after` | SL-25 | `design/gdd/emitter-state-and-light-exposure-query.md` EM-1 (designed)"; its block table carries `WORLD`, `SHARED`, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema 1, `restore_after: [layout.state, graph.state, body.state, controller.state]`. **Consistent**: identical to EM-1, and Save/Load registers no blocks of its own (SL-17), so the kernel orders the restore.

**Item DB (designed; `item-and-module-database.md`, Status Designed).** The designed Item & Module Database has adopted what this GDD asked of its draft. *Adopted (kept):* the `emits {shape_id, base_pm, hue_class}` field shape (its field *shape* is the Item DB's, its *semantics* are here: `base_pm` is the lamp's rated brightness, `shape_id` a key into the shape table, `hue_class` a colour token); the LOAD subtype `2 LAMP` (`req_w` 20 W, `req_max` 40 W, `power_tier` 3); the glowstick parameters (EM-15, F-7) including life counted from the throw tick; the glowstick key `POOL_GLOWSTICK` (shape row 6); `emits` on any def that glows (module lantern or beacon, or consumable); and `lantern_on_states` with the default `{0 OK, 1 DEGRADED}`. *Remaining ask:* none; `GLOWSTICK_BASE_PM` is the def's `emits.base_pm` (confirmed, 1000 placeholder). *Resolved:* lantern defs key `emits.shape_id` to `LANTERN` (shape row 9; Item DB lines 169 and 397, Open Question 21). *Noted, not an ask:* the draft's remark that exposure scales with area is honoured by carrying `reach_px` and the shape in the read view (EM-15, EM-27); IDB-12 (d) attributes "the sixth-slot and lighting-budget rules" to Emitter State, but shadow-slot assignment is Lighting's (EM-26): this GDD only keeps the authoring lint and the `priority` field that make Lighting's cut reproducible.

**What each dependent must repeat in its own GDD** (so the dependency is bidirectional, per the design-docs rule). Lighting: it reads only the `emitter.state` read view, never writes, conforms to the shape table and curve classes, owns flicker / gutter / decoys / the flash clamp. Interaction: every prompt and every phantom target routes through `lit(ref)` only; it registers two face subjects per door; it calls `spawn_deployable` with the life remaining at landing; it owns `NOT_LIT`. Suit: it owns beam charge and `CHARGE_EMPTY`, calls `force_off`, reads `emitter.state.lit` as of T-1, keeps the hook name `suit.vitals` and its `after: graph.propagate.atmo`. Attention: it declares `after: [graph.propagate.power, emitter.place]`, reads the owner's beam as emission and does not add it again to the owner's lux (it counts once, at its apex value, in `lux_pm`), and does not double-count `lamps_lit_w`. Horror: it declares `after` Attention and `emitter.place`, registers its subjects, and treats being revealed as the beam's whole effect. Derelict Authoring: it stays inside the emitter caps by lint and declares the cryo-bay lamp. Layout: it publishes `OccluderSource` with `version(section_id)` and the `occludes_light` flag and projects the Graph's `door_state` into the door-leaf rectangle.

## Formulas

*Conventions. Every quantity is an integer (permille `_pm` 0..1000, base px, ticks, Q14 / Q28 fixed point). Every `⌊a / b⌋` divides non-negative operands (GDScript integer division truncates toward zero, so no formula divides a negative). Names are used verbatim — registry (locked): `TICK_RATE` 60, `TILE_PX` 48, `AIM_UNITS` 4096, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6, and the Graph's `INRUSH_STEPS` 5 and `INRUSH_PM` 1500. Foreign formula ids are qualified (PC D.12, Graph D.6, kernel D.12); a bare D.n is this section's. Every number this section introduces is a placeholder with an owner (Tuning Knobs).*

### D.1 Lamp output

The `lamp_output_pm` formula is defined as:

`output_pm = switch_on × armed × live × ⌊ base_pm × served_pm / 1000 ⌋` — the lit branch is the registry formula `power_allocation`'s output "lamp brightness = base * served_pm / 1000", reused verbatim

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `switch_on` | bool | 0/1 | `emitter.state` | the committed switch (fixture, beacon) |
| `armed` | bool | 0/1 | D.2 | `T >= arm_tick` (always 1 for a self-powered FIXTURE, `node_ref` 0) |
| `live` | bool | 0/1 | Graph `node(node_ref).state` | node exists, is not dormant or tombstoned, and its state is not `2 FAILED`, `3 DESTROYED`, `4 SALVAGED`, `5 ABSENT` (EM-13) |
| `base_pm` | int | 0..1000 | Item DB `emits.base_pm` (`mod_lamp` 1000; placeholder) | rated brightness |
| `served_pm` | int | 0..1000 | Graph `served_pm(node_ref)` | tier satisfaction `sat_k`, independent of this lamp's own request; a self-powered lamp uses 1000 |

**Output Range:** 0..1000, never above `base_pm` and never above the `served_pm`-scaled value (Graph P1: a lamp never renders brighter than the power it is served). **Example:** `base_pm` 1000, `served_pm` 1000, on, armed, live → 1000; `served_pm` 450 → ⌊1000 × 450 / 1000⌋ = 450 (the actionable radius of a 144 px fixture falls from 122 to 95 px: at `d` 95 the chain gives `F` 340 and `c` ⌊450 × 340 / 1000⌋ = 153, at `d` 96 `F` 333 and `c` 149, so 95 is the largest lit `d`; the closed form ⌊144 × 300 / 450⌋ = 96 runs 1 px high, D.7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; switched off with `served_pm` 1000 → 0; SALVAGED node → 0.

### D.1b Dock status glow sizing *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X2)*

The `dock_status_glow` sizing is defined as the set of constraints the self-powered DOCK_STATUS fixture (EM-11) must satisfy, with D.1 and D.7 as the formulas:

`output_pm = DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM` while `switch_on` (self-powered: `armed` 1, `live` 1, `served_pm` 1000, so D.1 reduces to `base_pm`); the console subject at distance `d_c ≤ DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX` from the glow origin is lit iff `c(d_c) = ⌊ DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM × F(d_c, R) / 1000 ⌋ ≥ LIT_ON_PM`, `R = DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX`, `F(d, R) = ⌊1000 × (R − d) / R⌋`

**Constraints.** (C1) `R ≥ REACH_MIN_PX` 24 (I-REACH). (C2) the console is lit with margin at the farthest allowed placement: `c(DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX) ≥ LIT_ON_PM` plus a margin that absorbs a placement tolerance. (C3) the glow is small and dim: its `section_emission` term `⌊ output_pm × R / 1000 ⌋` is at most one sixth of an ordinary fixture's 144 (1000 × 144 / 1000), that is **at most 24**, so it reads as a status light and not as a lamp that lights the room. **C3 is enforced by I-DOCK** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the ruled ranges (`DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM` 300–800, `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` up to 96) allow up to ⌊800 × 96 / 1000⌋ = 76, so a range check alone left C3 unenforced. (C4) the glow is a FIXTURE under the 28-fixture cap (I-CAPS (a)), so it needs no new cap: the largest loaded set is 21 fixtures (registry `mvp_fixture_counts`), leaving 7 for dock glows. (C5, cited, not owned) the arrival cell stays dark: Web Map's lint G2 (registry `pod_console_glow`, Web Map D.15) requires `c(d_arr)` < `LIT_OFF_PM` for the distance `d_arr` from the glow origin to the centre of the dock room's `arrive_*` cell. At the defaults `F`(37) = ⌊1000 × 11 / 48⌋ = 229 and `c`(37) = ⌊500 × 229 / 1000⌋ = 114 < 120 while `F`(36) = 250 and `c`(36) = 125 ≥ 120, so the least legal `d_arr` is `DOCK_STATUS_ARR_MIN_PX` = 37 (derived from this chain; it moves with `DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM`, `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` and `LIT_OFF_PM`). Derelict Authoring runs it as `DOCK_GLOW_ARRIVAL_DARK`; no Emitter value or range changes. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM` | int | 500 (300–800, jointly bound by I-DOCK C2 and C3) | placeholder, owner game-designer / art-director, confirmed by the Lighting spike | the glow's steady output *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` | int px | 48 (35–49 at the other defaults; I-DOCK, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*) | same | SOFT fade-out radius |
| `DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX` | int px | 24 (< `r_lit_px`, 33 at the defaults) | owner Derelict Authoring (placement lint) | the console subject's anchor lies within this distance of the glow origin, clear of every occluder (`ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX`) |

**Output Range:** `c` 0..500; `r_lit_px` by the D.7 chain. **Example (defaults):** at `d_c` 24, `F` = ⌊1000 × 24 / 48⌋ = 500 and `c` = ⌊500 × 500 / 1000⌋ = 250 ≥ 150 (C2: a margin of 100 permille over `LIT_ON_PM`, which is a ratio `c` / `LIT_ON_PM` of 1.67; margin and ratio are different quantities and the bound uses the first) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the chain gives `r_lit_px` 33 (at `d` 33, `F` 312 and `c` 156 lit; at `d` 34, `F` 291 and `c` 145 unlit), so a placement tolerance of 9 px beyond the 24 px bound still prompts (the bound is set inside `r_lit_px`, not at it, so the lint has room). C3: ⌊500 × 48 / 1000⌋ = 24, one sixth of a fixture's 144 (24 = 144 / 6, the boundary). C1: 48 ≥ 24. C4: 21 + (dock glows in the largest loaded set, 3 at most) ≤ 28; the count of dock rooms is Derelict Authoring's and is recounted into `mvp_fixture_counts` there (`[PLACEHOLDER — derive at DA-22 recount]`). If the Lighting spike moves `LIT_ON_PM` to 300, `c` at 24 px is 250 < 300 and C2 fails, so `DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM` must rise to at least ⌈300 × 1000 / 500⌉ = 600 (the same I-EDGE style joint bound; in general the least legal base is ⌈`LIT_ON_PM` × 1000 / `F`(`DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX`, `R`)⌉ with `F` the **integer** chain value of D.7, not the real quotient (`R` − `d`) / `R`: at `R` 49 and `d` 24 the chain gives `F` 510 where the real quotient is 510.2, and the bound is ⌈300 000 / 510⌉ = 589). A boot check `I-DOCK` (D.15) states C1, C2 and C3 together and is **false whenever `DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX` ≥ `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX`** (the domain hole: with the console bound at or beyond the reach, `F` is 0 or undefined and no base output can light it; `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` 24 therefore always fails at the default bound 24) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

### D.2 Switch latency

The `switch_latency` formula is defined as:

`arm_tick = T_a + posmod(power_offset − T_a, GRAPH_STEP_TICKS)` with `power_offset` read from `GraphTuning` (ADR-0012; identical to the earlier form `T_a + ((GRAPH_STEP_TICKS − (T_a mod GRAPH_STEP_TICKS)) mod GRAPH_STEP_TICKS)` at offset 0); for an INIT-scope creation `T_a` is the first RUNNING tick (ADR-0017 D5.2) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*, `L = arm_tick − T_a`; the negative case (A1 false, a Graph that consumes only strictly later requests) is, at offset 0, `arm_tick′ = T_a + GRAPH_STEP_TICKS − (T_a mod GRAPH_STEP_TICKS)` and is a `GraphTuning` fact the unit tests parameterise, not a second code path

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T_a` | int | ≥ 0 | kernel clock | the tick whose P2 applies `set_switch` / `set_beacon`; **or, for an E1 authored-on lamp or beacon created under the INIT scope at `init_world`, the first RUNNING tick** (one more than the clock value the INIT pass is stamped with, ADR-0016 section 1.1; ADR-0017 D5.2, CT-36) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)* |
| `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` | int | 6 | registry (locked) | Graph hooks run at divisor 6; under the baseline stagger (LS-8: power 0, atmo 2, failure 4) the power hook, which consumes M14, runs at offset 0 (`power_offset`, read from `GraphTuning`), so for input timing a Graph tick is `T mod 6 = 0` (EM-1, terminology) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `arm_tick` | int | `T_a`..`T_a + 5` | calculated | the first Graph tick whose phase 0 consumes the queued M14 request; the lamp is ARMING (`output_pm` 0) before it |
| `L` | int | 0..5 | calculated | ticks from apply to lit; switching off is `L = 0` |

**New-game example (E1, authored-on home-ship lamp)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `init_world` runs under the INIT scope at clock 0 and Web Map's E1 creates the home-ship's authored-on lamp (`switch_on` true, rated draw 20 W). `T_a` is the first RUNNING tick, 1 (ADR-0017 D5.2, CT-36), so `arm_tick` = 1 + posmod(0 − 1, 6) = 6 and `L` = 5: M14 `set_load_request(node_ref, 20)` is queued inside E1 and drained at phase 0 of the first `graph.propagate.power` run after LOADING to RUNNING (ADR-0012's INIT drain row), which is tick 6 (assumption A1); the lamp is ARMING (dark) until then and lit by tick 6's `emitter.place`, with the Graph carrying the 20 W request from tick 6, so the lamp and the drawn request agree on every tick (the first ticks of RUNNING are a 0.1 s dark that the new-game presentation covers). `last_toggle_tick` is written 6 at that tick. **Assumption A3** *(resolved 2026-10-29)*: the first RUNNING tick is one more than the clock value `init_world` ran at (ADR-0016 section 1.1), not the clock value itself; the former negative case of A3 (`T_a` 1, `arm_tick` 6) is therefore the rule, not an alternative *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*. **Output Range:** `L` in 0..5 ticks (0..83 ms); the negative case 1..6. The hook is `after: graph.propagate.failure` (EM-1), so the lamp is lit by the `emitter.place` of the same tick as `arm_tick`. **Example:** `T_a` 1200 (a Graph tick) → `arm_tick` 1200, `L` 0 (negative case 1206, `L` 6); `T_a` 1201 → 1206, `L` 5; `T_a` 1205 → 1206, `L` 1. Inrush (the Graph's business, visible as an honest dip): eight lamps switched together on a tier-3 island with 700 W free request 8 × ⌊20 × `INRUSH_PM` / 1000⌋ = 240 W plus a neighbouring 500 W heater, so `served_pm` = ⌊1000 × 700 / 740⌋ = 945 for `INRUSH_STEPS` 5 runs (0.5 s), then 1000.

### D.2b Switch commit spacing *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D32; default applied, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20))*

The `switch_commit_spacing` formula is defined as:

`accept ⇔ last_toggle_tick < 0 ∨ commit_tick − last_toggle_tick ≥ SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS`, with `commit_tick = T_a` for a beam toggle, for an OFF that darkens a lit or armed lamp, and for **every switch of a self-powered `node_ref` 0 fixture** (no M14 call, no ARMING, `armed` always 1: its ON commits at the apply tick, so charging it the `arm_tick` of D.2 would shift its spacing check by 0..5 ticks *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), `commit_tick = arm_tick` (D.2) for the ON of a Graph-powered lamp, and no test at all for an OFF applied while the lamp is ARMING (it cancels an ON that never became visible); a rejection is `TOO_SOON` for `toggle_beam` and `COOLDOWN` for `set_switch`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` | int | 12 (11–20) | this GDD, placeholder owner technical-director and technical-artist, confirmed by 2D Lighting LO-22 | the least number of commit ticks between two realised switch transitions of one emitter |
| `last_toggle_tick` | int | −1 or ≥ 1 | the record (EM-4) | commit tick of the previous realised transition |
| `commit_tick` | int | `T_a`..`T_a` + 5 | D.2 | the projected tick at which the requested transition becomes visible (`T_a` exactly for a self-powered fixture) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `N(s)` | int | — | derived | the most realised transitions of one emitter inside any rolling window of 60 ticks at spacing `s` |

**Derivation (the constraint `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` satisfies).** The flash budget is `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3 per second, and the registry `flash_count` unit is ⌈n / 2⌉ (X9), so one emitter may realise at most N = 6 transitions in any rolling second (⌈6 / 2⌉ = 3; seven would be four flashes). At spacing `s`, a half-open window of 60 ticks holds at most N(s) = ⌈60 / s⌉ transitions (2D Lighting's governor convention) and a window closed at both ends holds at most ⌊60 / s⌋ + 1. Requiring N ≤ 6: half-open gives 60 / s ≤ 6, `s` ≥ 10; closed gives ⌊60 / s⌋ ≤ 5, `s` ≥ 11. `s` = 12 ships because it satisfies both with a transition of margin under the governor's count (N(12) = 5, ⌈5 / 2⌉ = 3 flashes; the closed count is 6, three flashes) and because it equals Interaction's `TAP_REARM_TICKS` 12. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the first draft added "so a player's tap path is rarely the tighter guard". That is false for ON-then-OFF: the tap guard spaces submissions and this guard spaces commits, so the fastest legal OFF after an ON is refused in 5 of the 6 latency alignments, see the table below.)* Upper limit: above 20 ticks (0.33 s) a deliberate double tap reads as a dropped press; hence the range 11–20.

**Output Range:** accept or reject; at most 5 realised transitions of one emitter in any 60 ticks, hence at most 3 flashes. **Examples (fixture, previous realised OFF at tick 120):** an ON requested at `T_a` 121 has `arm_tick` 126 (D.2: 121 mod 6 = 1, +5) and 126 − 120 = 6 < 12 → `COOLDOWN`; at `T_a` 126 (a Graph tick) `arm_tick` 126 → 6 → `COOLDOWN`; at `T_a` 127 `arm_tick` 132 → 12 → accepted; at 132 `arm_tick` 132 → accepted. **ON then OFF:** ON at 1201 (`arm_tick` 1206, realised at 1206, so `last_toggle_tick` 1206 from then); OFF at 1203 → cancels, accepted, writes nothing; but OFF at 1213 → 1213 − 1206 = 7 → `COOLDOWN`, OFF at 1218 → 12 → accepted. **Beam:** toggles submitted on 60 consecutive ticks T..T + 59 are accepted at T, T + 12, T + 24, T + 36, T + 48, which is 5 accepted and 55 rejected `TOO_SOON` (AC-90). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**ON then the fastest legal OFF (the `12 − L` table)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: an ON applied at `T_a` commits at `arm_tick` = `T_a` + `L` (`L` = D.2, 0..5). The player's next tap may be applied `TAP_REARM_TICKS` 12 later, at `T_a` + 12, and commits at once. The gap between the two commits is 12 − `L`, so the OFF is refused `COOLDOWN` whenever 12 − `L` < `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` 12, that is whenever `L` > 0, and is accepted only when `L` = 0.

| `T_a mod 6` | `L` | ON commits | fastest OFF applies / commits | gap 12 − `L` | OFF result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0 | `T_a` | `T_a` + 12 | 12 | accepted |
| 1 | 5 | `T_a` + 5 | `T_a` + 12 | 7 | `COOLDOWN` |
| 2 | 4 | `T_a` + 4 | `T_a` + 12 | 8 | `COOLDOWN` |
| 3 | 3 | `T_a` + 3 | `T_a` + 12 | 9 | `COOLDOWN` |
| 4 | 2 | `T_a` + 2 | `T_a` + 12 | 10 | `COOLDOWN` |
| 5 | 1 | `T_a` + 1 | `T_a` + 12 | 11 | `COOLDOWN` |

At the sim a refusal is a domain code (`COOLDOWN`, no HUD text, no light change), so a deliberate off-tap right after an on-tap is refused in 5 of 6 alignments (a tap `L` ticks later, 1 to 5, gets through). The creative-director reads a silently ignored deliberate tap as a broken switch that erodes trust in the light model, which the horror depends on. **Options (resolved 2026-10-28, delegated ruling R20, Open Question 31):** (a) raise the ON-to-OFF tap re-arm to 17 (`SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` 12 + the worst `L` 5), so every tap that gets through commits at least 12 after the ON and none is silently dropped (a sibling change, Interaction's `TAP_REARM_TICKS`); (b) keep 12 and add a readable refusal tell (a short dull switch-resistance cue, presentation only, no HUD text, never a light change); (c) keep 12 and silent. **Resolved 2026-10-27 — delegated ruling G2 (re-review; user delegated to best judgment): the creative-director's stated lean is against a silent refusal, so (b) is the applied default:** the `COOLDOWN` result reaches the submitter as its terminal result and Interaction's existing failed-action cue path (the path that already carries `NOT_LIT`; the Adapter already plays `cue_input_ignored` for a `toggle_beam` inside the rate-limit window) plays one short dull cue for it, with no light change and no HUD text; the sim, the value 12 and every formula are untouched, so nothing reverses; (a) is not adopted (Open Question 31, resolved 2026-10-28, delegated ruling R20). The cue itself is Interaction's and the Presenter's to draw (a cross-GDD note). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

### D.3 Node lantern output

The `node_lantern_output` formula is defined as:

`output_pm = lantern_on × ⌊ base_pm × min(1000, max(0, run_pm)) / 1000 ⌋` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the clamp is the Emitter's; `run_pm` is the Graph's published reading)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `lantern_on` | bool | 0/1 | Item DB `lantern_on_states` (default `{0 OK, 1 DEGRADED}`) on `node(node_ref).state` | the machine's state counts as running; a dead module is dark (P1) |
| `base_pm` | int | 0..1000 | Item DB `emits.base_pm` (`mod_reactor_cell` 300, `mod_scrubber` 200, `mod_coolant_pump` 150; placeholder) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | rated glow |
| `run_pm` | int | 0..1000 as published; clamped here to 0..1000 | Graph `node(id).run_pm` (LS-19 additions, delivered 2026-10-27) | how hard the machine runs: 0 when the node's requested load is 0 (machine off: LOAD `req_w` 0, SCRUBBER `req_w` 0, PUMP `throttle_pm` 0, REACTOR `out_w` 0), else the served fraction (`served_pm` for a consumer, including a SCRUBBER, a PWR_SNK consumer, `⌊1000 × out_w / out_w_rated⌋` for a REACTOR, which can exceed 1000 above rated output); not `served_pm`, which is 1000 for a machine whose request is 0 and would glow from a switched-off machine, and not the Graph's D.9 `duty_pm`, which carries the same trap *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** 0..`base_pm`, never above it: `run_pm` is clamped to 0..1000 on the Emitter side because a REACTOR running above its rated output reports above 1000. There is **no weaker fallback**: the Graph publishes `run_pm`, so a browned-out or starved machine glows at its served fraction and a switched-off machine is dark; a lantern at full `base_pm` while its machine starves would lie about power state (P1). **Boundary values:** a REACTOR cell (`base_pm` 300) with `out_w` 0 → `run_pm` 0 → 0; at rated output `run_pm` 1000 → 300; at 1.2 × rated the Graph reports 1200, the clamp gives 1000 → 300 (not 360); a negative or missing reading is treated as 0. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Example:** reactor cell at `run_pm` 1000 → 300, lit radius `r_lit_px` 48 px by the D.7 chain (at d = 48, F = 500 and c = 150 lit; at d = 49, F = 489 and c = 146 unlit); pump at `run_pm` 1000 → 150, which lights nothing beyond its own anchor (`r_lit` 0); pump at `run_pm` 500 → 75; a pump switched off (`throttle_pm` 0) on a met tier → 0 although its `served_pm` is 1000; FAILED → 0. **Scrubber example** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: a scrubber lantern (`mod_scrubber`, `base_pm` 200, hue `LANTERN_O2`, reach 96) at `run_pm` 1000 → 200, lit radius `r_lit_px` 24 and `r_off_px` 38 by the D.7 chain (d 24: F 750, c ⌊200 × 750 / 1000⌋ = 150 lit; d 25: F 739, c 147 unlit; d 38: F 604, c 120; d 39: F 593, c 118), since 200 sits above `LIT_ON_PM` 150. The designed Item DB keys lantern defs (`mod_reactor_cell`, `mod_scrubber`, `mod_coolant_pump`) to `LANTERN` (its lines 169 and 397; its Open Question 21 marks the key resolved), so shape row 9 and the Item DB agree. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

### D.4 Beam pose

The `beam_pose` formula is defined as:

`origin = chest` (the chest point), `occ_origin = pos_curr`, `axis_k = DIR[(aim_angle + off_k) mod 4096]`, `off_k ∈ {0}` for single-cone shapes and `{−SPLIT_AXIS_UNITS, +SPLIT_AXIS_UNITS}` for BEAM_SPLIT

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `chest` | int2, ship-local px | x within ±2 of the foot, `y` −45..−25 | `ControllerView.get_chest_x` / `get_chest_y`, computed at read time from the same tick's `pos_curr` plus the frozen `CHEST_POINT` table (PC D.12; ADR-0017 D5.3, CT-28); no stored `chest_sim` | the apex, reach and distance origin; read, never recomputed |
| `pos_curr` | int2, px | ship-local | `body.state` | the foot origin: the start of every occluder segment (EM-6); `LocomotionStep` keeps it outside every footprint |
| `aim_angle` | int | 0..4095 | `controller.state` | committed aim, 0 = E, counter-clockwise, 3072 = S |
| `SPLIT_AXIS_UNITS` | int | 206 | shape table (derived from the verbatim 18°, D.15 I-WEDGE) | the split's axis offset |
| `SPLIT_HALF_UNITS` | int | 114 | shape table (10° encoded) | the split's per-cone half-angle, bound with `SPLIT_AXIS_UNITS` by I-WEDGE |
| `DIR[a]` | Q14 int pair | each component within ±16384 | generated data file (built by the data tool outside `src/sim/emitter/`, loaded and asserted at boot) | `(round(16384 cos θ), −round(16384 sin θ))`, `θ = 2π a / 4096`, built from one quadrant by reflection so `DIR[2048 − a] = (−ux, uy)` exactly (I-MIRROR) |

**Output Range:** integer origin and Q14 axis; no interpolation (the kernel's H9: the sim never reads a render alpha). **Example:** `pos_curr` (302, 200), facing W (`flip_h`, mirror (−1, 1)), `CHEST_POINT[E][walk]` (1, −34) → mirrored x −1 → `chest` (301, 166), `occ_origin` (302, 200); `aim_angle` 3072 → `DIR` (0, +16384); flipping to E moves `chest` to (303, 166), a 2 px change inside the Controller's 4 px bound; BEAM_SPLIT at `aim_angle` 1024 (N) tests axes 818 and 1230.

### D.5 Cone membership

The `cone_membership` formula is defined as:

`in_cone = (d2 = 0) ∨ ( d2 ≤ R² ∧ D > 0 ∧ D² ≥ d2 × COS2_Q28 )` with `r = anchor − origin`, `d2 = rx² + ry²`, `D = rx × ux + ry × uy`; a 360° shape is `d2 ≤ R²`; BEAM_SPLIT is the OR of two such tests, of which at most one is true (the dark wedge never closes)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `r` | int2 | each component within ±400 | calculated | subject anchor minus emitter origin (EM-8: the single `anchor_px`) |
| `d2` | int | 0..147,456 | calculated | squared distance; 0 is inside every cone (the apex) |
| `R` | int px | 24..384 (I-REACH) | `reach_px` | the hard reach; the edge is closed (`d2 = R²` is inside) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the first draft's 96 floor ignored the 24 px waning floor and the 72 px fault glow)* |
| `(ux, uy)` | Q14 int pair | ±16384 | D.4 | the axis |
| `D` | int | within ±6,291,456 | calculated | Q14 dot product; the `D > 0` guard is required (the squared test alone accepts the opposite half-plane) |
| `COS2_Q28` | int | per shape row | derived by the `ShapeCatalog` freeze from `DirTable` (square of the Q14 cosine of `half_angle_units`; ADR-0017 D1.2), not a stored column | 201,441,249 wide; 256,768,576 narrow; 260,305,956 split; 66,994,225 fan; 236,913,664 beacon |

**Output Range:** bool. Every product is below 2^62 (`D²` and `d2 × COS2_Q28` ≤ 3.96 × 10^13; I-OVERFLOW). The quantised edge misplaces a rim by under 0.1° (under 0.7 px at 384 px). **Example (wide cone, origin (300, 166), aim 0):** subject (540, 200) → `r` (240, 34), `d2` 58,756 ≤ 82,944, `D²` 15,461,882,265,600 ≥ 58,756 × 201,441,249 = 11,835,882,026,244 → inside; subject (540, 28) → `r` (240, −138), `d2` 76,644, product 15,439,263,088,356 ≤ `D²` → inside (29.90°); subject (540, 27) → `r` (240, −139), product 15,495,062,314,329 > `D²` → outside (30.08°; the encoded edge is 29.97°). One pixel flips the answer, which is the sim/render agreement test.

### D.6 Occluder segment test

The `occluder_segment_test` formula is defined as:

`blocked = ∃ K′ ∈ boxes(OccluderSource.rects_in(body_id, ax0, ay0, ax1, ay1, out)) : t_lo ≤ t_hi` with `(ax0, ay0, ax1, ay1) = aabb(S, P)`, evaluated on **doubled coordinates** `S′ = 2S`, `P′ = 2P` against the unit-square box of each rectangle, `K′ = [2·x0 − 1, 2·x1 − 1] × [2·y0 − 1, 2·y1 − 1]` for a stored `[x0, x1) × [y0, y1)` (EM-20 a); `t_lo = max(0, tx_lo, ty_lo)`, `t_hi = min(1, tx_hi, ty_hi)`; per axis `a` with `d_a = P′_a − S′_a`: if `d_a = 0` the interval is all of ℝ when `min_a ≤ S′_a ≤ max_a` and empty otherwise, else `t = (min_a − S′_a) / d_a` and `(max_a − S′_a) / d_a` ordered by the sign of `d_a`; fractions are compared by cross-multiplication with positive denominators (no division) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `S` | int2, px | ship-local | D.4 `occ_origin` (the foot for the beam, the anchor for other kinds) | segment start |
| `P` | int2, px | ship-local | subject `anchor_px` | segment end; at least `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` outside every rectangle (authoring lint) |
| `K′` | int4, doubled px | the closed unit-square box `[2·x0 − 1, 2·x1 − 1] × [2·y0 − 1, 2·y1 − 1]` of a stored half-open rectangle `[x0,x1) × [y0,y1)` — equivalently the occupied span `(x0, y0, x1−1, y1−1)` that Layout returns (registry `rect_algebra_closed_span`) widened by one doubled unit, half a pixel, on every side; `min_a`, `max_a` are its bounds *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Layout via `OccluderSource` | wall (`WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` 24), closed door leaf, `occludes_light` prop; actors and horrors never occlude |

**Output Range:** bool. Closed unit-square boxes and a conservative rule: a touch (corner graze, edge-on pass) is blocked (EM-20 a); an endpoint in an occupied cell is inside a box and is blocked; an endpoint outside every box is never on a box face (even doubled coordinates against odd box bounds). All cross-products are below 2^42 (doubled offsets within ±2^11), inside I-OVERFLOW. **Example (axis-aligned; the first draft's probes, re-derived):** wall stored [408,432) × [96,288) (occupied span x 408..431, y 96..287; doubled box x [815, 863], y [191, 575]); `S` (302, 200), `S′` (604, 400). `P` (540, 200), `P′` (1080, 400): `d_x` 476, x-slab [211/476, 259/476] = [0.443, 0.544], `d_y` 0 with 191 ≤ 400 ≤ 575 → `t_lo` 0.443 ≤ `t_hi` 0.544 → blocked. `P` (407, 200), `P′` 814: x-slab [211/210, 259/210] = [1.005, 1.233], `t_lo` 1.005 > `t_hi` 1 → not blocked. `P` (408, 200), `P′` 816: x-slab [211/212, 259/212], `t_lo` 0.995 ≤ `t_hi` 1 → blocked (the anchor is in the wall's own cell, which the clearance lint refuses). From the east, `S` (500, 200) to `P` (432, 200) (`S′` 1000, `P′` 864, `d_x` −136): x-slab [1.007, 1.360], `t_lo` > `t_hi` 1 → not blocked (anchor 432 has clearance 1). **Example (oblique, the leak the first draft hid):** a horizontal wall run stored as two rectangles [0,24) × [240,264) and [24,48) × [240,264) (doubled boxes x [−1, 47] and [47, 95], y [479, 527]); `S` (23, 300), `P` (24, 140). On the first draft's closed spans (x 0..23 and 24..47) the ray crossed the wall rows at x 23.23..23.38, inside the strip between the spans, and touched neither: a leak. On the boxes: `S′` (46, 600), `P′` (48, 280), `d_x` 2, `d_y` −320; box 1 has x-slab [(−1−46)/2, (47−46)/2] = [−23.5, 0.5] and y-slab [(527−600)/−320, (479−600)/−320] = [0.228, 0.378] → `t_lo` 0.228 ≤ `t_hi` 0.378 → blocked. **Example (diagonal corner join):** A [0,24) × [0,24) and B [24,48) × [24,48) meet only at a corner (doubled boxes [−1, 47]² and [47, 95]², sharing the point (47, 47)); `S` (24, 23) to `P` (23, 24), `S′` (48, 46), `P′` (46, 48), passes exactly through that point: box A has x-slab [0.5, 24.5] and y-slab [−23.5, 0.5] → `t_lo` = `t_hi` = 0.5 → blocked (on the first draft's spans the segment touched neither rectangle). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.7 Illumination

The `illumination` formula is defined as:

`c = ⌊ output_pm × f / 1000 ⌋`, SOFT: `f = F(d, R)`; HARD: `f = EDGE_FLOOR_PM + ⌊(1000 − EDGE_FLOOR_PM) × F(d, R) / 1000⌋`; `F(d, R) = ⌊1000 × (R − d) / R⌋` (curve id LINEAR, the MVP default); `d = max{k : SQ[k] ≤ d2}` (= `ConeMath.floor_root(sq, d2) = sq.bsearch(d2, false) − 1` on a squares table, ADR-0017 D1.4) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*; `lux_pm = min(1000, Σ c)`; `act_lux_pm = min(1000, Σ c over gates_actions emitters)`; actionable radius `r_lit = max{ d ∈ 0..R : c(d) ≥ LIT_ON_PM }` over the same integer chain (−1 when `c(0) < LIT_ON_PM`), found by binary search because `c` is monotone non-increasing in `d`, and **published from the sim per emitter as `r_lit_px`** (derived on an output or reach change; the closed form ⌊R × (output_pm − LIT_ON_PM) / output_pm⌋ is retired) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; latch-release radius `r_off = max{ d ∈ 0..R : c(d) ≥ LIT_OFF_PM }` over the same integer chain (−1 when `c(0) < LIT_OFF_PM`), found by a second binary search bounded to the window [max(0, `r_lit_px`), min(`R`, max(0, `r_lit_px`) + ⌊`R` × (`LIT_ON_PM` − `LIT_OFF_PM`) / `output_pm`⌋ + 2)] because `c` is monotone, and **published per emitter as `r_off_px`** (`r_off_px ≥ r_lit_px` always) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `output_pm` | int | 0..1000 | D.1, D.3, D.9 (constant), D.11, `BEAM_BASE_PM` | the emitter's steady output |
| `d` | int px | 0..R | `SQ` table: 385 squares, native `bsearch` (`floor_root`, ADR-0017 D1.4), 9 probes | exact integer floor of the distance from the origin (the chest for the beam); no square root, no float |
| `R` | int px | 24..384 | `reach_px` | current reach (D.9 for a waning stick) |
| `F` | int | 0..1000 | curve id (data) | 1000 at the apex, 0 at `R`; a replacement curve (a 17-knot table, linear interpolation on `u = ⌊d × 1024 / R⌋`) keeps the signature and must stay monotone, integer, with non-negative operands (R15) |
| `EDGE_FLOOR_PM` | int | 250 (160..400) | placeholder, owner technical-artist, confirmed by the Lighting spike | the HARD-class contribution kept at the rim; jointly bound with `LIT_ON_PM` by I-EDGE |
| `LIT_ON_PM` | int | 150 (100..300) | D.8 | the turn-on threshold |
| `r_lit_px` | int px | −1..R | calculated, published per emitter | the largest `d` whose integer chain gives `c ≥ LIT_ON_PM`; the approach radius: where a prompt for a not-yet-lit subject first appears (no longer what Lighting draws a soft pool's boundary at: that is `r_off_px`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `r_off_px` | int px | −1..R | calculated, published per emitter | the largest `d` whose integer chain gives `c ≥ LIT_OFF_PM`; where a soft pool is drawn (Lighting draws at max(`r_off_px`, `POOL_RESIDUAL_PX`)) and how far a prompt for an already-lit subject persists (the D.8 latch); it follows the same HARD / SOFT convention as `r_lit_px`; derived like `r_lit_px` (recomputed on an output or reach change and in `post_restore`, not snapshotted, not canonical state) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |

**Output Range:** `c` 0..1000, `lux_pm` and `act_lux_pm` 0..1000 (saturating; addition commutes, so there is no order dependence). There is no ambient term (`AMBIENT_PM` 0). The owner's own beam adds its full `output_pm` (the d = 0 apex value) to the owner's ACTOR `lux_pm` and nothing to its `act_lux_pm` (EM-21). **Example:** wide beam `output_pm` 1000, `d2` 43,264 → `d` 208 (208² ≤ 43,264 < 209²), `R` 288: `F` ⌊1000 × 80 / 288⌋ = 277, `f` 250 + ⌊750 × 277 / 1000⌋ = 457, `c` 457; at the rim `c` = `EDGE_FLOOR_PM` 250 (lit), and a beam at `output_pm` 600 has rim `c` 150 (lit) while 599 gives 149 (unlit), the ⌈`LIT_ON_PM` × 1000 / `EDGE_FLOOR_PM`⌉ = 600 consequence. SOFT fixture `R` 144, `output_pm` 1000: `d` 122 → `F` 152 (lit), `d` 123 → 145 (unlit), `r_lit` 122 (the chain: `d` 122 lit, `d` 123 unlit; at output 1000 the closed form ⌊144 × 850 / 1000⌋ = 122 agrees with the chain for every `R` from 24 to 384, but at output 450 the closed form gives 96 where the chain gives 95; over `R` = 24..384 step 24 and `output_pm` 151..1000 the closed form ran 1 px high in 1,405 of 13,600 cases and never low, the review's coarser sweep found 219 of 5,100, so Lighting's drawn pool edge would have sat 1 px outside the actionable edge and used the whole AC-56 tolerance) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **Latch-release radius `r_off_px`** (fixture R 144, from `c(d)` = ⌊output × ⌊1000 × (144 − d) / 144⌋ / 1000⌋ ≥ 120): outputs 1000 / 600 / 300 / 200 / 160 / 150 / 140 / 121 / 120 give `r_off_px` 126 / 115 / 86 / 57 / 36 / 28 / 20 / 1 / 0, against `r_lit_px` 122 / 108 / 72 / 36 / 8 / 0 / −1 / −1 / −1 (at output 1000, `c`(126) = ⌊1000 × 125 / 1000⌋ = 125 ≥ 120 and `c`(127) = ⌊1000 × 118 / 1000⌋ = 118 < 120); output 450 gives 95 and 105; a glowstick R 96 at 1000 gives `r_lit_px` 81 and `r_off_px` 84; the foot spill R 24 at 600 gives 18 and 19. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* Sum: beam 457 + a lamp of `output_pm` 848 at `d` 100 (`F` 305, `c` ⌊848 × 305 / 1000⌋ = 258) → `lux_pm` 715; adding a fixture at `d` 60 (`c` 583) gives 1298 → 1000. Alone with the lamp on, the owner reads `lux_pm` 1000.

### D.8 Lit with hysteresis

The `lit_hysteresis` formula is defined as:

`lit′ = (act_lux_pm ≥ LIT_ON_PM) ∨ (lit ∧ act_lux_pm ≥ LIT_OFF_PM)`; a stateless `lit_on = act_lux_pm ≥ LIT_ON_PM` form is reserved for the VS-A `probe(body_id, point)` (EM-24) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `act_lux_pm` | int | 0..1000 | D.7 | action-gating light (the BEACON does not gate) |
| `lit` | bool | 0/1 | canonical, one bit per subject (EM-22) | last tick's answer; kept across a restore |
| `LIT_ON_PM` | int | 150 (100..300) | placeholder, owner technical-artist (art bible 7.0), confirmed by the Lighting spike | turn-on |
| `LIT_OFF_PM` | int | 120 (60..140), `< LIT_ON_PM` | same | turn-off; there is no dwell (`LIT_OFF_DWELL_TICKS` fixed at 0) |

**Output Range:** bool. The band in px at a soft rim is `r_off_px` − `r_lit_px` (the same integer chains at `LIT_OFF_PM` and `LIT_ON_PM`; never the closed form ⌊`R` × (`LIT_ON_PM` − `LIT_OFF_PM`) / `output_pm`⌋, which runs 1 px low for some outputs), bounded by ⌊`R` × (`LIT_ON_PM` − `LIT_OFF_PM`) / `output_pm`⌋ + 2: 4 px for a fixture (R 144: 126 − 122) and 3 px for a glowstick (R 96: 84 − 81) at output 1000 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; a hard rim is a step (457 → 0 across one pixel), so hysteresis only matters for dimmed or soft light. **Example:** 160 → lit; 130 → still lit; 119 → unlit; 140 → still unlit; 151 → lit. A subject saved lit at 130 is lit after the restore (the bit is canonical). A lamp browning from `served_pm` 950 to 780 moves a subject at `d` 122 from `c` 144 (still lit) to 118 (unlit).

### D.9 Glowstick waning

The `glowstick_waning` formula is defined as:

`reach_px = R_full` if `r ≥ W`; else `f = GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM + ⌊ (1000 − GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM) × r / W ⌋` and `reach_px = ⌊ R_full × f / 1000 ⌋`; removed when `r = 0`; `W = ⌊ L × GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM / 1000 ⌋`, `output_pm = GLOWSTICK_BASE_PM`, curve id `WANE_LINEAR_25`. This is the Item DB's F-7 `reach_px(rem)` verbatim (`design/gdd/item-and-module-database.md`, F-7); a different expression in either GDD is a conflict to surface, not a silent change.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `r` | int ticks | 0..`L` | `life_remaining_ticks` (E3 passes the life remaining at landing) | counted from the throw tick |
| `L` | int ticks | 9000 | `life_total_ticks` = `ticks_for_seconds(GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S)` (Item DB, 150 s) | total life |
| `R_full` | int px | 96 | `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES` 2 × `TILE_PX` 48 (Item DB) | full reach |
| `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM` | int | 400 | Item DB placeholder | the remaining-life fraction below which it wanes |
| `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM` | int | 250 | Item DB placeholder | the reach fraction at the last tick |
| `f` | int permille | 250..999 | derived: `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM + ⌊ (1000 − GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM) × r / W ⌋`, only while `r < W` | the waning factor (1000 while steady) |
| `GLOWSTICK_BASE_PM` | int | 1000 | Item DB `cons_glowstick` `emits.base_pm` (this GDD's name for it) | constant output |

**Output Range:** `reach_px` monotone non-increasing, 96 → 24 (`r` 1 gives `f` 250, reach 24), then removed at `r` 0; `f` steps by one permille every 4.8 ticks and the reach by one px about every 50 ticks. Steady for the first `L − W` = 5400 ticks from the throw. **Example:** thrown stick, E3 receives `r` = 9000 − `THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30 = 8970 → 96; `r` 3600 → 96; `r` 3599 → 95; `r` 1800 → `f` 625, reach 60; `r` 1 → 24; `r` 0 → removed, id retired. Actionable radius (SOFT, output 1000): 81 px steady, 51 at `r` 1800, 20 at `r` 1.

### D.9b Glowstick evict-wane

The `glowstick_evict_wane` formula is defined as (an addition to D.9; D.9 stays the Item DB's F-7 verbatim, and this curve applies only to a stick that a cap eviction selected):

`reach_px(T) = min( reach_F7(r), max( R_floor, ⌊ ( R_0 × E − (R_0 − R_floor) × (T − t_e) ) / E ⌋ ) )` for `t_e ≤ T < t_e + E` — **one non-negative numerator, one floor division** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the first draft wrote ⌊R_0 − (R_0 − R_floor) × (T − t_e) / E⌋, a floor of a rational difference; a literal port `R_0 - IntMath.floor_div((R_0 - R_floor) * (T - t_e), E)` subtracts a floored term and so computes the **ceiling**, which is 1 px high on almost every tick (96 at +1 instead of 95) and breaks the AC-36c2 equality. The numerator is non-negative because `T − t_e < E` and `R_floor ≤ R_0`)*; removed at `T − t_e ≥ E` (or at `r = 0`, whichever is first); `E = ticks_for_seconds(GLOWSTICK_EVICT_WANE_S)`, `R_floor = ⌊ R_full × GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM / 1000 ⌋`, `output_pm = GLOWSTICK_BASE_PM`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_e` | int ticks | kernel clock | canonical `evict_tick` | the tick the eviction began (E3) |
| `R_0` | int px | `R_floor`..96 | canonical `reach_at_evict` | the stick's reach at `t_e` (the D.9 value on that tick) |
| `R_floor` | int px | 24 | derived, I-WANE guarantees ≥ `REACH_MIN_PX` (24) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | the ordinary wane's own last value |
| `E` | int ticks | 300..1200 | `ticks_for_seconds(GLOWSTICK_EVICT_WANE_S)`, 10 s = 600 | the evict-wane length; in seconds because this GDD registers no tick-denominated glowstick constant |
| `reach_F7(r)` | int px | 24..96 | D.9 | the ordinary curve keeps running underneath, so a stick near the end of its life is never lengthened by eviction |

**Output Range:** `reach_px` monotone non-increasing, `R_0` → `R_floor` over `E` ticks, never below `R_floor` before removal, never above the D.9 value. **Example** (`R_full` 96, so `R_floor` 24, `E` 600; for another `R_full` substitute `R_0` = the reach at `t_e` and `R_floor` = ⌊`R_full` × 250 / 1000⌋): a steady stick (`R_0` 96) evicted at `t_e`: at +1 tick → ⌊(57 600 − 72 × 1) / 600⌋ = ⌊95.88⌋ = **95**; at +300 → ⌊(57 600 − 21 600) / 600⌋ = 60; at +599 → ⌊(57 600 − 43 128) / 600⌋ = ⌊24.12⌋ = **24**; at +600 → removed, id retired. The boundary rows (+1 gives 95, +599 gives 24) are AC-36c2's and tell the floor of the difference from the ceiling (which gives 96 and 25). A stick already at reach 40 when evicted falls 40 → 24 over the same 600 ticks. Actionable radius (SOFT, output 1000): 85 % of the reach, so 81 px at the start for a steady stick and 20 px at the end. Eviction never changes `output_pm`, so what the player sees is the real reveal radius shrinking, in the world, with no timer. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

### D.10 Beacon aim

The `beacon_aim` formula is defined as:

`beacon_aim(T) = ( beacon_phase + beacon_dir × ⌊ T × AIM_UNITS / BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS ⌋ ) mod 4096` with a non-negative modulus

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T` | int | 0..2^50 − 1 | kernel clock (persisted by the kernel) | the tick; `T × 4096` stays below 2^62 (I-OVERFLOW; 2^50 × 4096 is exactly 2^62, so the range stops one short) |
| `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` | int | 128 (120..180; any integer ≥ 1 is valid) | placeholder, owner art-director / audio, confirmed by the Lighting spike | ticks per revolution (2.13 s, inside the art bible's 2–3 s) |
| `beacon_phase` | int | 0..4095 | authored data | start angle |
| `beacon_dir` | int | ±1 | authored data | sweep direction |

**Output Range:** 0..4095; a pure function of `T` with no per-tick state, so save-equivalence needs only the kernel clock. At 128 the step is exactly 32 units per tick; at 150 it alternates 27 and 28 and closes exactly at `T` = 150. **Example:** `T` 1000, phase 0, dir +1 → ⌊1000 × 4096 / 128⌋ = 32,000 mod 4096 = 3328; `T` 10, phase 100, dir −1 → 100 − 320 = −220 → 3876; sweep 150, `T` 150 → 4096 mod 4096 = 0. The 456-unit-wide cone covers a point inside its reach for 14 or 15 of every 128 ticks by phase (about 11 %), which is why the beacon does not gate actions (EM-16).

### D.11 Fault glow output

The `fault_glow_output` formula is defined as:

`output_pm = ⌊ GLOW_BASE_PM[key] × SEV_GLOW_PM[sev] / 1000 ⌋`, `reach_px = radius_px[key]` (the F&T row's sev 4 column, the sev 3 column where sev 4 is n/a) else `FAULT_GLOW_REACH_PX`; a record exists only while `sev ≥ sev_min[key]` and `output_pm > 0`; beyond `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` the records evicted are the unprotected ones with the lowest `sev`, ties by the higher descriptor id first (so the kept rows are ordered by `sev` descending, then descriptor id ascending), and a record with age `T − placed_tick` below `ticks_for_seconds(W_MIN_WARNING_S)` is protected and never evicted

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `key` | `mode` + `medium` | the F&T glow-table keys (`1/FUEL`, `1/GAS`, `2`, `3`, `7`, `9`); a descriptor whose key has no row creates no emitter | Failure & Trail glow-table row (e.g. `1/GAS`); `FailureDescriptor.mode` is a failure mode 1..10 or a descriptor-only id 20..26 (Graph LS-14) | a row exists only for a glowing descriptor, and is requested only while `intensity_pm` > 0 |
| `GLOW_BASE_PM[key]` | int | 0..1000 | Failure & Trail V/A.6 ledger column (F&T owns the values); placeholders `2` 800, `7` 600, `3` 500, `9` 500, `1/FUEL` 600, `1/GAS` 400 (owner technical-artist / F&T) | glow at `sev` 4 |
| `sev_min[key]` | int | 1..4 | Failure & Trail V/A.6 `sev_min` column (owner F&T): the first `sev` at which the row requests light (`7` → 3, `3` → 3, others 1) | below it the row requests no light and no record exists |
| `sev` | int | 0..4 | Graph `FailureDescriptor.sev` | severity |
| `SEV_GLOW_PM[sev]` | int | {0, 250, 500, 750, 1000} | placeholder, owner Failure & Trail | monotone |
| `FAULT_GLOW_REACH_PX` | int px | 72 (36..120) | placeholder, owner technical-artist (this GDD; F&T Open Question 20 c asks for its registration) | used when a row carries no radius |
| `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` | int | 10 (≥ ⌈1.25 × authored simultaneous glowing faults on one body⌉, I-CAPS (c); 6..16) | placeholder, owner technical-director, confirmed by the Derelict Authoring fault list | the cap on FAULT_GLOW records |
| `placed_tick`, `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | int ticks, int s | kernel clock; 10 (Graph registry constant, cited) | canonical record field; Graph | a record younger than `ticks_for_seconds(W_MIN_WARNING_S)` = 600 ticks is protected from eviction |

**Output Range:** 0..1000. The record appears on the Graph tick of its descriptor and is removed on the Graph tick it is gone (EM-17). **Example:** `2` SHORT at `sev` 3 → 600; at `sev` 1 → 200, actionable radius `r_lit_px` 18 px by the D.7 chain (reach 72: at d = 18, F = ⌊1000 × 54 / 72⌋ = 750 and c = 150 lit; at d = 19, F = 736 and c = 147 unlit) (the sev 4 column); `7` OVERLOAD at `sev` 2 → no record (`sev_min` 3); `sev` 0 → 0, no record; eleven glowing descriptors with the cap at 10 → the unprotected record with the lowest `sev` (ties: the higher descriptor id) is evicted and the ten others keep theirs; eleven where the lowest-`sev` record is 120 ticks old (protected) → that record stays, the next-lowest unprotected one is evicted instead; eleven all younger than 600 ticks → none is evicted, the table holds 11 and `emitter_fault_glow_overcap` increments (a content defect that I-CAPS (c) exists to prevent). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

### D.12 Section emission

The `section_emission` formula is defined as:

`section_emission(sid) = Σ over emitters e in section sid with output_pm_e > 0 of ⌊ output_pm_e × reach_px_e / 1000 ⌋`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `output_pm_e` | int | 0..1000 | the emitter record | steady output |
| `reach_px_e` | int px | 24..384 | the emitter record | current reach |

**Output Range:** 0..36,864 (`MAX_EMITTERS` 96 × 384). Not an area (the cone fraction is ignored); Attention owns weights and units. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Shape-blind by design — "a focused searchlight is louder"** *(Open Question 23 resolved 2026-10-27: default applied, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20) (D25))*: because the term is output × reach, the narrow beam (384) generates 2.3 times the light stimulus of the fan (168; 384 / 168 = 2.29), so the loadout that reveals the least of a room is the loudest. This is **deliberate**, as the creative-director asked a defensible outcome to be: reach and focus are bought with being heard, the loadout trade is *reveal against exposure* rather than a free pick of the quietest cone, and the Player Fantasy states it. No `emission_weight_pm` column per shape row exists in the MVP (recorded as the rejected alternative: it would make a tight beam and a wide beam cost the same, which flattens the P3 trade). The term stays as written and Attention's F-3 cites it unchanged. The beam's foot spill (row 11, EM-6) is excluded from this sum, so the near-field pool adds no Attention load. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Reach range text: this GDD's range is 24..384 px (I-REACH); Attention F-3 says 8..384 and is to be aligned. It is the *light* stimulus; the Graph's `power_use(section_id).lamps_lit_w` is the *power* stimulus and one lamp is never counted as both (EM-27). **Example:** two fixtures (1000, 144) = 288; a glowstick at full reach (1000, 96) = 96; a wide beam (1000, 288) = 288 → 672; a dock status glow (500, 48) adds ⌊500 × 48 / 1000⌋ = 24; the beam's foot spill (600, 24) adds 0 (excluded). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

### D.13 Staleness bound

The `staleness_bound` formula is defined as:

`age(reader) = 0` if the reader's hook declares `after: [emitter.place]`; `1` if it runs before `emitter.place` in the tick or is P2 `validate`; a render-rate poll reads the committed tick's table

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `age` | int ticks | 0..1 | calculated | how old the subject table a reader sees is |
| source ages | ticks | named | other GDDs | committed aim ≤ 3 ticks behind the drawn aim (PC-5); `served_pm` changes only on Graph ticks (every 6) but is read current; node state and descriptors are current (EM-1) |

**Output Range:** {0, 1} ticks for a table read. There is no 5-tick term (that was the Graph's divisor-6 caveat). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The bound is unconditional, rebuild ticks included: EM-23 makes the static-layer update synchronous, so there is no pending state and a hitch costs time, never staleness; this is the "never stale" half of the contract that Interaction (`validate` at T−1, its six-tick re-check), Attention and Horror rely on. **Example:** `set_switch` applied at `T_a` 121 → `arm_tick` 126; a hook `after: [emitter.place]` at 126 sees the lamp lit; P2 `validate` at 126 sees tick 125's table (unlit); `validate` at 127 sees lit; `suit.vitals` at 126 (divisor 6, before `emitter.place`) reads `emitter.state.lit` as of 125.

### D.14 Exposure budget

The `exposure_budget` formula is defined as:

`T_mand = T_der + T_dyn + T_mov + T_tab + T_pub + T_broad + T_rlit + T_cell`, with **`T_cell = N_cells × W_CELL`** *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync: `T_snap` is retired, there is no snapshot copy (ADR-0017 D4.2); a new unit `CELL` with weight `W_CELL` prices the grid-cell traversal of the candidate step (D10.4, D12.6). `N_cells` is the number of grid cells visited by every candidate query the tick runs (a dynamic emitter's reach-disc query, a mover's or re-evaluated subject's query to the statics, and the queries inside `T_sub`, `T_new` and `T_leaf`), counted by the code as an integer, one count per cell walked, clamped edge cells included; it depends on `EMITTER_CELL_PX` (ADR-0017 D10.4) and on content, so it is a **fixture-pinned counted input**, not a constant (the benchmark records it). — sync knock-on fix.)* *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix: the totals quoted in this section, in the scenario table and in AC-64 were computed before the sync. They are **pre-retirement reference values**: each includes `T_snap` (`E_tot × W_SNAP`, 408 at the typical point) and omits `T_cell`. Where a figure is still quoted it is marked, and the equivalent **cell-free base** (the reference value less `T_snap`, before `T_cell` is added) is given beside it; the full value is `base + N_cells × W_CELL` (ADR-0017 D12.6 does not claim the MVP fits: the typical base is 9,581 of `B` 10,000, which leaves 419 units for `T_cell` (209 cells at `W_CELL` 2), so the typical point fits only if its queries visit at most 209 cells in a tick; the fixture's `N_cells` and the full totals are fixed at the budget pass, EM-SPIKE-1-ACCEPT).)*; **one per-subject cost** `U_sub = E_cand × W_REJ + E_surv × (W_CON + K_OCC × W_SEG + W_RECTS)`, used identically for a static subject (`T_sub = U_sub + W_TAB`) and for a mover (`T_mov = M × U_sub`, the `W_TAB` already sits in `T_tab`); `T_dyn = Σ over dynamic emitters (N_cand × W_REJ + N_r × W_CONE + N_p × (W_CON + K_OCC × W_SEG + W_RECTS))`, `T_der = E_dyn × W_POSE + E_tot × W_DER`, `T_tab = N × W_TAB`, `T_pub = N_dirty × W_PUB`, `T_broad = N_moved × W_BROAD`, `T_rlit = N_rl × W_RLIT` (the pair of chain searches, `r_lit_px` and `r_off_px`, for each emitter whose output or reach changed and stays above zero; an output of 0 sets both −1 with no search; `N_rl` ≤ `N_dirty`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; `B = EXPOSURE_BUDGET_MS × 10⁶ / U_OP_NS`, `C_hot = EXPOSURE_TICK_CEILING_MS × 10⁶ / U_OP_NS`, `C_cold = COLD_REBUILD_CEILING_MS × 10⁶ / U_OP_NS`; static-layer terms `T_out = ρ × W_DELTA + N_keys × W_PUB`, `T_reach = ρ × W_CON + N_keys × W_PUB`, `T_leaf = P_leaf × (W_REJ + K_OCC × W_SEG + W_RECTS)`, `T_new = N_cs × W_REJ + ρ × (W_CON + K_OCC × W_SEG + W_RECTS)` (VS-A creation only: the MVP builds nothing after `init_world`); `T_static` is the sum of the static-layer terms a tick incurs (`T_sub` per registered or moved subject, `T_out` or `T_reach` plus `N_rl × W_RLIT` per emitter change, `T_leaf` per leaf change) and `T_cold` = `N × T_sub` plus `N_rl × W_RLIT` for every emitter; the contract is **steady state** `T_mand + T_static ≤ B`, **hot scenarios** `T_mand + T_static ≤ C_hot`, **cold scenarios** `T_cold ≤ C_cold`; there is no probe term (EM-24). `E_s` (static emitters in range of a mover) is retired: a mover's neighbourhood is priced by the same `E_cand` and `E_surv` as a static subject's *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; re-priced 2026-10-27 — re-review: the first draft priced a mover at `M × E_s × 253` with `E_s` 2 against `E_cand` 6 and `E_surv` 3 for a static subject in the same rooms, so a mover cost 0.69 of a static subject's neighbourhood; the W7 snapshot, the `r_lit_px` search and the `rects_in` call were unpriced)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**Variables:** work units are counted, deterministic and integer; `U_OP_NS` 40 converts them to time. Every cost is a spike parameter (GDScript op cost is NOT SOURCEABLE from the pinned reference).

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `W_REJ`, `W_CONE`, `W_CON`, `W_SEG`, `K_OCC` | int | 10 (5–20), 20 (10–40), 63 (30–120), 40 (20–80), 4 (1–8) | spike parameters (the systems-designer's 0.4 / 0.8 / 2.5 / 1.6 µs at 40 ns; the technical-director's `C_cone` 25 omits `W_CON`) | radius reject; cone test; distance + falloff + accumulate; one rectangle test; rectangles per surviving pair |
| `W_POSE`, `W_DER`, `W_TAB`, `W_PUB` | int | 100 (50–200), 8 (4–16), 4 (2–8), 15 (8–30) | spike parameters | per dynamic emitter pose; per emitter derive; per subject table write; per dirty key |
| `W_DELTA`, `W_BROAD` | int | 8 (4–16), 30 (10–60) | spike parameters *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | one cached-pair delta (recompute `c` from the stored `f_pm`); broadphase maintenance per moved dynamic emitter or subject (uncounted in the first draft) |
| `W_FRAME` | int | 2 (1–4) | spike parameter, model value *(new; 2026-10-28 cross-GDD fix)* | one `section_offset` / `body_of` re-read and rewrite per record or subject (a weld or cut, EM-23 trigger vii) |
| `W_RECTS`, `W_CELL`, `W_RLIT` *(`W_SNAP` retired, ADR-0017 D4.2)* | int | 30 (10–80), 2 (1–4), 230 (120–450) | spike parameters, model values *(new; re-review)* | one `OccluderSource.rects_in(body_id, ax0, ay0, ax1, ay1, out)` call per surviving pair; `W_CELL` is one grid cell visited by a candidate query (ADR-0017 D12.6) and is multiplied by the counted input `N_cells` in `T_cell` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix: `N_cells` is per-tick, per-fixture and has no design-point value in this model; it is pinned by the `exposure_dp.json` counting test and by the benchmark)*; `W_RLIT` is one emitter's **pair** of chain searches: `r_lit_px` (about 9 probes of the D.7 chain) plus `r_off_px` (a search bounded to the window [`r_lit_px`, `r_lit_px` + ⌊`R` × 30 / `output_pm`⌋ + 2] of at most 31 positions at R 144, about 5 probes), raised from 150 (80–300) by +80 when `r_off_px` was added *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `N`, `E_tot`, `E_dyn`, `M`, `N_moved`, `N_dirty` | int | **typical design point: 174, 34, 2, 3, 5, 6; movers at the I-CAPS (d) bound: as typical with `N` 192, `M` 6, `N_moved` 8; worst corner (all caps): 192, 96, 2, 16, 18, 20** | content *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | registered subjects (174 = the registry `subject_budget` sum at S4, 192 = `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS`); emitters (34 = 32 static at rest [21 fixtures, the largest loaded set of `mvp_fixture_counts`, + 3 dock glows + 8 lanterns] + 1 beacon + 1 beam; fault glows and sticks are transients priced in S4/S5; 96 = `MAX_EMITTERS`); beam + beacon; movers registered (3 = the actor + 2 horror subjects; 6 = the I-CAPS (d) bound `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` 2 + `A_max` 4); moved dynamic emitters and subjects (`E_dyn` + `M`); dirty keys per tick. **The typical column is what the benchmark must meet; the ceiling column is what the lint admits and the benchmark records separately.** |
| `N_cand / N_r / N_p` | int | beam 30 / 24 / 8, beacon 20 / 16 / 4 — design point (0..cap: beam 48 / 40 / 12, beacon 32 / 26 / 6; a pair that survives costs `W_CON` + `K_OCC` × `W_SEG` + `W_RECTS` = 253) | content, after the broadphase (ADR-0017 D10) | candidates, radius survivors, cone survivors *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `B` | int | 10,000 (6,250–15,000 across the `EXPOSURE_BUDGET_MS` range) | `EXPOSURE_BUDGET_MS` 0.4 at `U_OP_NS` 40 | the budget in units |
| `E_cand`, `E_surv`, `ρ_E`, `ρ_S`, `P_leaf`, `ρ_tot`, `N_cs`, `N_keys` | int | 6, 3, 7, 4, 6, 224, 14, per scenario — scenario inputs *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; re-review)* | content, after the broadphase | static emitters that are candidates and that survive the radius reject per subject (so `U_sub` = 6 × 10 + 3 × 253 = 819); cached rows per fixture or lantern and per stick; **`P_leaf` 6 = the pairs whose segment bounding box meets one door leaf's changed region: 2 static emitters whose box meets the leaf × 3 subjects beyond it, a content assumption the spike measures**; **`ρ_tot` 224 = 32 static emitters × `ρ_E` 7 (the first draft's 600 was an unexplained pad)**; candidate subjects of a created emitter; dirty keys of the change *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

**Output Range:** *All figures below are re-priced (Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review) with one per-subject cost, the three missing terms, and one stated population per column; they are model values until EM-SPIKE-1.* *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix: every total below that lists `T_snap` is a **pre-retirement reference value** (includes `T_snap`, omits `T_cell`). The cell-free bases, computed as reference minus `T_snap` and exact in the formula's other terms, are: typical `T_mand` **9,581** (0.3832 ms, 95.8 % of `B`; 9,989 − 408), movers at the I-CAPS (d) bound **12,200** (0.488 ms, 122 %; 12,608 − 408), VS-B **21,026** (0.841 ms, 210 %; 21,470 − 444), worst corner **26,954** (1.078 ms, 270 %; 28,106 − 1,152). 2026-10-29 sync knock-on fix)*, `T_rlit` 1,380 (6 × `W_RLIT` 230) → reference `T_mand` **9,989 units = 0.3996 ms (99.9 % of `B`)**, which leaves 11 units of steady-state headroom (9,509, 95 % and 491 units before the `r_off_px` search was priced) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. *Movers at the I-CAPS (d) bound* (`M` 6, `N_moved` 8, `N` 192, the rest typical): `T_mov` 4,914, `T_tab` 768, `T_broad` 240 → **12,608 units = 0.504 ms (126 % of `B`)** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: content at the lint's own bound for movers is over the steady budget, so the bound is a ceiling the spike must clear, not a target, and the first mitigation is the moved-only rule of the decision table. *Named VS-B configuration* (four co-op beams, four actors and two horror subjects, one beacon, no static change; `E_dyn` 5, `E_tot` 37, `M` 6, `N` 192): `T_dyn` 12,748 (4 × 2,804 + 1,532), `T_der` 796, `T_mov` 4,914, `T_tab` 768, `T_pub` 90, `T_broad` 330, `T_snap` 444 *(retired, reference only)*, `T_rlit` 1,380 → reference 21,470 units = 0.859 ms, 215 % of `B` before any static change *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: **the MVP budget does not cover VS-B** (Open Question 27). *Worst corner* (all caps at ceiling: `E_tot` 96, `M` 16 = `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS`, `N` 192, beam 48 / 40 / 12, beacon 32 / 26 / 6, `N_dirty` 20): `T_dyn` 6,674 (4,316 + 2,358), `T_der` 968, `T_mov` 13,104, `T_tab` 768, `T_pub` 300, `T_broad` 540, `T_snap` 1,152 *(retired, reference only)*, `T_rlit` 4,600 (20 × 230) → reference **28,106 units = 1.124 ms, 281 % of `B`** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* (each extra mover adds `U_sub` + `W_BROAD` = 849): not a shipped configuration; the lint holds content to the typical column plus the I-CAPS bounds and the spike measures the worst corner. *Reconciliation:* the technical-director's model scans all `N` per dynamic emitter; the systems-designer's counts broadphase candidates. With candidates = `N` the typical total (reference value with `T_snap`; 12,561 cell-free, equal to 8,385 + 24 × `N`, fitting `B` up to `N` = 67 — sync knock-on fix) becomes 12,969 units (0.519 ms, 130 % of `B`) and equals 8,793 + 24 × `N`, which fits `B` only up to `N` = 50 (⌊(10,000 − 8,793) / 24⌋; 12,489, 8,313 + 24 × `N` and `N` = 70 before the `r_off_px` search was priced) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, so **the broadphase is required**, not optional (ADR-0017 D10.4). The first draft's totals (8,726 and 'a linear scan fits up to N ≈ 151') priced a mover at 0.69 of a static subject and omitted the snapshot, the `r_lit_px` search and the `rects_in` call; the performance-analyst's consistent-pricing estimate (about 10,064 units at `M` 6) is the same model without those three terms. *Derivations (placeholders, owner technical-director, confirmed by the exposure spike and the Interaction / Authoring counts):* `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS`: the worst-case sum of the **per-category budgets** (registry `subject_budget`, owner Interaction: `S_total = F + L_seed + C + P + A + H`, player drops `P` ≤ `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` 24, actors `A` ≤ 4, horror subjects `H` ≤ `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` 2) = **174** at S4 {home+B, A} (`S_int` 144 + P 24 + A 4 + H 2; S3 {home+B} welded 142), with the cap at **192** (18 subjects, 9 %, of headroom, a multiple of 64; the cap was sized at a sum of 167 while the horror budget was a placeholder 12, and is kept; deployables are emitters, not EM-21 subject kinds, and are not in the sum; the budgets are owned by Derelict Authoring, Interaction, Suit, Horror Entities and this GDD and are held by I-CAPS (d)) (of the 174, the moving budget is A 4 + H 2 = 6; the moving registrations are the 1 actor and the 2 horror subjects = 3 at the MVP design point, and the moving budget bound is Horror's `horror_subject_count`, `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` 2 + `A_max` 4 = 6 ≤ `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS` 16, inside which the typical design point prices `M` 3 and the lint bound `M` 6 (D.14, Output Range); ITEM, OBJECT and deployable subjects register static, EM-21; MVP slice of 14 compartments, home-ship 5 / derelict A 4 / derelict B 5; the TD's 128 omitted door faces); `MAX_EMITTERS` = ⌈1.25 × (28 fixtures + 20 lanterns + 10 fault glow + 8 deployables + 4 evicting deployables + 4 seat beams (VS-B sizing; the MVP instantiates one) + 2 beacons)⌉ = ⌈1.25 × 76⌉ = 95 → **96** (the margin is the 25 % of CD-GDD-ALIGN concern 1; the earlier 1.3 × 70 = 91 counted 8 fault glows and no evicting tail), below `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256. Fallbacks, in order (**the numeric decision table below governs**; this list and EM-24's closing sentence are its summary) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: (1) price and evaluate only movers that changed cell this tick, re-using the pair rows of an idle mover (a stationary mover is a static subject; AC-69 already proves the skip changes no answer), which cuts `T_mov` to the moved fraction; (2) cut candidates by spatial pre-bucketing; (3) raise `EXPOSURE_BUDGET_MS` toward its 0.6 ms bound (`B` 15,000, under which the worst-corner point is still over, 187 %, and the movers-at-bound point is 84 %) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; (4) bake a per-room illumination grid for the static layer; (5) raise `U_OP_NS` honestly after the spike. *(Lowering `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS` to 6, listed in the first draft, did nothing at the design point, where the I-CAPS (d) bound is already 6.)* **Example** *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix: the tick totals in this example and in the scenario table are pre-retirement reference values, with `T_snap`, without `T_cell`; the cell-free tick totals, reference minus the typical `T_snap` 408, are S1 10,781, S2 9,794, S3 11,853, S4 19,213, S5 13,557, S8 weld 20,433, S8 cut 12,989; each adds the tick's `N_cells × W_CELL`)*: the door-travel tick of S1 at the typical design point costs 9,989 + 1,200 = 11,189 units (0.448 ms, 112 % of `B`); the ship-blackout tick of S3 costs 12,261 units (0.490 ms), over `B` for that one tick and well inside `C_hot` (25,000 units, 1.0 ms); the S4 inrush tick costs 19,621 (0.785 ms); the MVP weld tick of S8 costs 20,841 (0.834 ms) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. **Run EM-SPIKE-1 before the exposure stories are sized:** the model's own design point sits at 99.9 % of the budget and its mover-bound point at 126 %, so the spike is the most important schedule risk in this document. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Static-layer rebuild scenarios** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the first draft did not price the rebuild, and it collides with D.13's "age ≤ 1 tick" unless the contract is chosen; EM-23 chooses it)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The rebuild is **synchronous**: every update below finishes inside the tick, so the table is never stale, and the cost is held by a ceiling instead of a deferral. Inputs (the typical column): `E_cand` 6 and `E_surv` 3 static emitters per subject, `ρ_E` 7 rows per fixture or lantern, `ρ_S` 4 per stick, `P_leaf` 6, 32 static emitters ship-wide (21 fixtures + 3 dock glows + 8 lanterns), `ρ_tot` 224 (32 × 7), 14 subjects per room, `N` 174; hence `U_sub` = 6 × 10 + 3 × 253 = 819, `T_sub` = 823, a room 14 × 823 = 11,522 and the ship 174 × 823 = 143,202. `C_hot` = `EXPOSURE_TICK_CEILING_MS` 1.0 = 25,000 units; `C_cold` = `COLD_REBUILD_CEILING_MS` 8 = 200,000 units; **the ceilings' owner is the technical-director.** The totals add each scenario's static-layer cost to the typical `T_mand` 9,989 (9,509 before the `r_off_px` search was priced) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix: the table's tick totals and the S6 and S8b figures are pre-retirement reference values (they include the retired `T_snap`: 408 in the typical `T_mand` and in the S6 build, 1,152 in the S6 ceiling figure, and omit `T_cell`); the cell-free S6 figures are 174 × 823 + 34 × 230 = **151,022** (6.04 ms) and at the ceiling 192 × 823 + 96 × 230 = **180,096** (7.20 ms), the cell-free S8b hitch tick 151,022 + 9,581 = **160,603** (6.42 ms); the static-layer scenario terms of the "Designed" column are unchanged, and each scenario also pays the cells its queries visit, `N_cells × W_CELL`, counted per scenario by the fixture; the cell-free tick totals are listed in the Example below)*. **S7 (section instantiate on a pod hop) is removed** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: E1 has no caller after `init_world` (EM-5, WM-24), so the only instantiate is the `init_world` build inside LOADING, which S6 prices.
