The naive policy fits none of them (S1 alone is 11,522 per bump against `B` 10,000), which is why the pair rows, the zero-output store and the narrowing rule are rules and not optimisations. S1 to S5 and S8 exceed `B` for single ticks and are held to `C_hot` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; how often they occur is what the benchmark's P99.9 and max measure (AC-53). **Decision table against the benchmark's numbers** (RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK, release export, delegated ruling R12): *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*

| Measured outcome | Decision |
|---|---|
| steady P99 ≤ 0.4 ms, every hot max ≤ 1.0 ms, cold ≤ 8 ms | ship as designed |
| steady P99 in 0.4–0.6 ms | raise `EXPOSURE_BUDGET_MS` within its range only while P3's total stays ≤ 2.0 ms P99; otherwise cut candidates by spatial pre-bucketing |
| steady P99 > 0.6 ms | evaluate only movers that changed cell (idle movers re-use their rows), then spatial pre-bucketing, then bake the per-room illumination grid for the static layer *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (lowering `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS` to 6 was the first draft's step; it is already 6 at the lint bound and does nothing) |
| a hot max in 1.0–2.0 ms | accept if the scenario occurs less than once a minute of play; otherwise narrow its invalidation further (ADR-0017 D11.5) |
| a hot max above 2.0 ms | blocking: move the static layer to the baked per-room grid and re-derive S1–S5 |
| the worst-corner point (all caps) is measured | record it whatever the result; it is the figure the lint ceilings are re-derived from (it is expected to exceed `B`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| cold above 8 ms | split the cold build across LOADING's verification window (never across ticks) or move the static layer to a GDExtension (technical-director) |

**Calibration — the spike is a story, not a footnote.** Story EM-SPIKE-1 measures each model parameter on a release export (Godot 4.7.2 release export template, `forward_plus`, vsync on, 120-frame warm-up, startup read-back recorded) on **RM-DESKTOP** (minimum-spec desktop: 6-core i5-9400F / Ryzen 5 2600 class, 8 GB, GTX 1050 Ti / RX 570 class, 1080p60, P99 CPU frame ceiling 16.6 ms) and on **RM-DECK** (Steam Deck LCD, 1280x800, 30 fps target, ceiling 33.3 ms), the reference machines of `project.yaml` `qa.min_spec`; it fills the table below with a column per machine, re-derives `B` (a machine's own `U_OP_NS` gives its own `B`), the caps and the S1 to S6 and S8 totals, and files the numbers in the ADR 4 record. A pass needs whole-frame P99 at or below 90 % of the machine's ceiling (R12), inside which P3's ≤ 2.0 ms and `EXPOSURE_BUDGET_MS` hold. Until then every unit cost is a model value pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK (GDScript op cost is not sourceable from the pinned reference); the developer's machine is indicative only. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*

| `W_POSE`, `W_DER`, `W_TAB`, `W_PUB` | 100, 8, 4, 15 | — | same, per emitter and per subject |
| `W_DELTA`, `W_BROAD`, `W_FRAME` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | 8, 30, 2 | — | one cached-pair delta; one grid cell update; one `section_offset` / `body_of` re-read and rewrite |

| per-call read-view allocation | 0 by design (EM-25: live typed columns and caller-owned buffers, no snapshot copy; ADR-0017 D4.2) | — | allocation counter over a 60-tick render-rate poll |

### D.15 Boot invariants

The `boot_invariants` formula is defined as:

`I-EDGE ∧ I-HYST ∧ I-WEDGE ∧ I-OVERFLOW ∧ I-MIRROR ∧ I-CAPS ∧ I-WANE ∧ I-REACH ∧ I-DOCK ∧ I-SPACING` *(I-DOCK and I-SPACING added 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* — every one is checked at data load; a violated invariant refuses the build or the boot (no run-time fallback), **except I-SPACING, which is a build warning** (it depends on a value the Player Controller owns and is promoted to a refusal when that value is raised), and each has an acceptance criterion *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the first draft said every violated invariant refuses; AC-52 now carries a Result column of REFUSE, WARN, FALLBACK and PASS)*. **Implemented as explicit fault checks, never as `assert()`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: GDScript `assert()` is stripped from Release export templates, so an invariant written as an assert disappears in the shipped build and a test on a Debug build cannot see it. Each invariant is a function in the boot-check file that returns a fault code; the composition root refuses to start (and the build lint fails) on any non-OK code in Debug and Release alike; a lint bans `assert(` in the boot-check file; the checks take an injected Diagnostics seam carrying a `build_is_debug` flag, so a test runs the same code with the flag false (as in a Release build); and one Release-template smoke criterion (AC-52f) proves the checks run in the real export

**Variables:**

| Invariant | Statement | Defaults | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| I-EDGE | `BEAM_BASE_PM × EDGE_FLOOR_PM ≥ 1000 × LIT_ON_PM` (and for every HARD kind with `gates_actions`) | 1000 × 250 = 250,000 ≥ 150,000 | a full-output beam is actionable to its last drawn pixel; the knob ranges are not jointly valid (`EDGE_FLOOR_PM` 160 with `LIT_ON_PM` 300 fails) |
| I-HYST | `0 < LIT_OFF_PM < LIT_ON_PM` | 120 < 150 | the hysteresis band exists |
| I-WEDGE | `2 × SPLIT_AXIS_UNITS − 2 × SPLIT_HALF_UNITS ≥ 183` over the encoded integers | 412 − 228 = 184 (16.17°); 205 gives 182 (15.996°) and fails | the split's dark wedge is at least the art bible's 16° and never closes (183 = ⌈16 × 4096 / 360⌉) |
| I-OVERFLOW | every product `< 2^62`: `D²`, `d2 × COS2_Q28`, `T × AIM_UNITS` (beacon), segment cross-products | 3.96 × 10¹³; `T < 2^50` | integer-only determinism (Graph LS-1 precedent) |
| I-MIRROR | `DIR[(2048 − a) mod 4096] = (−ux, uy)` for every `a` | exact | mirrored facings give mirrored light |
| I-CAPS | (a) `MAX_EMITTERS ≥` authored emitters + `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` + `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` + `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_EVICTING` + bound seats (VS-B sizing 4); (b) `MAX_EMITTERS ≤ MAX_DIRTY_KEYS`; (c) `MAX_FAULT_GLOW ≥ ⌈ 125 × F_body / 100 ⌉` where `F_body` is the largest number of authored faults with a glow row that can be active at once on one body, including declared cascade hops (Derelict Authoring publishes the list: registry `mvp_fault_allocation`, `F_body` 6 in the MVP); (d) `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS ≥` authored static subjects + Σ the dynamic per-category budgets (player drops ≤ `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`, actors, horror subjects; registry `subject_budget`; deployables are emitters, not subjects), and the horror plus actor budgets ≤ `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS`; (e) **I-SUPPLY** (cross-GDD, checked against Suit & Equipment's data, Suit A11): the total glowsticks one seat can carry across all stacks × bound seats ≤ `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` — a build failure at one bound seat (the MVP); above one seat a breach is a boot warning, not a failure, and the surplus sticks enter the visible evict-wane (D.9b; Suit A11; the VS-B choice is Open Question 22) | (a) 50 + 10 + 8 + 4 + 4 = 76 ≤ 96, 28 + 20 + 2 = 50 authored; (b) 96 ≤ 256; (c) ⌈125 × 6 / 100⌉ = 8 ≤ 10 (`F_body` 6); (d) 174 ≤ 192 (S4; S3 142), 2 + 4 = 6 ≤ 16 (`HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` + `A_max`, Horror's `horror_subject_count`; 3 registered at the MVP design point); (e) 4 × 1 ≤ 8 (MVP: one stack of `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` 4, one seat); four seats give 16 > 8, a warning | no overflow by content (static or dynamic); no collapse to `ALL`; a fault's light does not disappear because the cap was reached by design content alone (a welded MVP body carries at most 6 simultaneous glow rows of the 8 live faults world-wide); eviction of a glowstick is unreachable by supply in the MVP. A violated clause refuses the build or the boot. The runtime counters are telemetry for a breach that got past the lint, never a design allowance |
| I-WANE | `0 < GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM ≤ 1000`, `0 < GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM < 1000`, `⌊ R_full × GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM / 1000 ⌋ ≥ REACH_MIN_PX` (it was `≥ 1`, which let a 1.5-tile Item DB radius through: ⌊72 × 250 / 1000⌋ = 18 < 24, which I-REACH forbids for every reach, so the old clause was vacuous; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), and `ticks_for_seconds(GLOWSTICK_EVICT_WANE_S) ≥ 1` (D.9b) | 400, 250, ⌊96 × 250 / 1000⌋ = 24 ≥ 24; 600 (the examples are parameterised by `R_full`: a radius of 1.5 tiles, 72 px, fails; the smallest legal `R_full` at floor 250 is 96, 2 tiles) | the curves are well-formed and the waning stick never falls below the minimum legal reach |
| I-REACH *(new; Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `REACH_MIN_PX ≤ R ≤ REACH_MAX_PX` for every declared reach: shape-table rows 1–10 (and row 11 when `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` > 0), every def `emits` reach, every Failure & Trail glow-row `radius_px`, `LANTERN_REACH_PX`, `FAULT_GLOW_REACH_PX`, `FIXTURE_REACH_PX`, `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` and, when non-zero, `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` (so 1–23 is refused: the spill is 0 or 24–48) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; checked at data load, and again at creation (a creation with an out-of-range reach is refused as in EM-17: the record falls back to its kind's default reach and `emitter_reach_invalid` counts); a waning stick's reach never falls below the I-WANE floor of at least `REACH_MIN_PX` (24), so no divide by `R` sees 0 and no reach is below the I-REACH minimum *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | 24 ≤ 72, 96, 144, 168, 288, 336, 384 ≤ 384 | D.7's `F(d, R)` divides by `R` (a zero reach would divide by zero), a reach above 384 breaks the D.5 and I-OVERFLOW bounds, and a fault glow with no lower bound had no guard |
| I-DOCK *(new; Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X2; extended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | **all of** (C1) `REACH_MIN_PX ≤ DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX`; **`DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX < DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX`** (false whenever the bound lies at or beyond the reach: the domain hole); (C2) `c(DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX) ≥ LIT_ON_PM` over the D.7 integer chain, with `R` = `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` and `output_pm` = `DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM` (D.1b); (C3) `⌊ DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM × DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX / 1000 ⌋ ≤ 24` (one sixth of a fixture's 144 `section_emission` term) | 24 < 48; c(24) = ⌊500 × 500 / 1000⌋ = 250 ≥ 150; ⌊500 × 48 / 1000⌋ = 24 ≤ 24 (the boundary) | the pod console is lit by its own glow at the farthest allowed placement, so `pod_hop`'s lit gate is satisfied by construction; `LIT_ON_PM` 300 would fail at base 500 and needs 600 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| I-SPACING *(new; Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D32; **provisional on the Player Controller**)* | `BEAM_TOGGLE_MIN_INTERVAL_TICKS ≥ SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` and `⌊60 / SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS⌋ + 1 ≤ 6` (the window closed at both ends, D.2b; the first draft's `⌈60 / s⌉ ≤ 6` accepted s = 10, whose closed-window count is 7, four flashes, and which the derivation rejects) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | 10 ≥ 12 **fails until the Controller raises its registry value to 12** (registry request filed); ⌊60 / 12⌋ + 1 = 6 ≤ 6, and s = 11 gives 6 (passes), s = 10 gives 7 (fails) | an Adapter-compliant human stream is never refused by the sim (EM-7), and one emitter never exceeds three flashes per second; reported as a build warning and never a boot refusal: it is permanently the one warning exception of D.15 (it depends on a value the Player Controller owns, and the registry change precedes AC-90d's story, so no promotion step exists) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

*Variables: every operand is a named constant of the fixture-constants table (Tuning Knobs); the invariant table replaces the five-column form because each row is a predicate, not a quantity.*

(The `CHEST_POINT` x range of ±2 px is the Controller's lint and is not re-owned here.)

**Output Range:** pass / fail at build and boot. **Example:** a data file setting `SPLIT_AXIS_UNITS` 205 fails I-WEDGE (410 − 228 = 182 < 183); `LIT_ON_PM` 300 with `EDGE_FLOOR_PM` 160 fails I-EDGE (160,000 < 300,000); a glow row with `radius_px` 0 or 400 fails I-REACH. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

## Edge Cases

**Switching, power and lamps**

- **If `set_switch` applies on a Graph tick (`T mod 6 = 0`)**: the lamp is lit by that tick's `emitter.place`; on any other tick it is lit at the next multiple of 6, at most 5 ticks later (D.2). One latency story, no hidden term.
- **If a switched-on lamp's `served_pm` is 0 (brownout to zero, SCRAM, shed)**: the lamp is STARVED, `output_pm` 0, `switch_on` unchanged and its request still queued; it lights again with no new command when power returns.
- **If a lamp's `output_pm` is at or below `LIT_ON_PM` (a deep brownout, or the 150 permille pump lantern)**: it lights nothing but its own anchor, still adds to sums, and Lighting draws it dim; an honest dim, never a prompt.
- **If a lamp's node is SALVAGED, ABSENT, dormant or tombstoned (not structurally present)**: M14 is a no-op, `live` is false, `output_pm` is 0, a `SYSTEM`-origin `set_switch` or `set_beacon` is rejected `NOT_LIVE`, a `PLAYER`-origin `set_switch` is accepted and flips `switch_on` (IA-11(a): no node read, no oracle) and the lamp stays dark, and the record stays until its section is discarded. A FAILED or DESTROYED node is different: both origins are accepted (next bullet). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **If a lamp's node goes FAILED or DESTROYED**: `output_pm` is 0 on that Graph tick (the hook is after the failure phase); `switch_on` is retained, so a repair re-lights the lamp without a command. A `set_switch` of either origin on such a node is accepted (it is structurally present, EM-7) and goes STARVED. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **If eight lamps are switched on in one tick on a marginal island**: all read the same inrush dip of `served_pm` for `INRUSH_STEPS` runs (945 in the D.2 example); ascending-id application makes the result order-free.
- **If a lamp is switched off while ARMING**: `switch_on` is false at once, M14 `(node_ref, 0)` is queued and the lamp is never lit; the pending `arm_tick` is discarded.
- **If `set_switch` targets a non-FIXTURE (a lantern, the beam)**: rejected `NOT_SWITCHABLE`; no state change and no M14 call.
- **If a FIXTURE has `node_ref` 0 and is neither the declared cryo-bay emergency lamp nor a dock room's DOCK_STATUS glow**: the authoring lint and boot refuse it (`SELF_POWERED_LAMP_COUNT`); the declared lamps are armed always, fixed `base_pm`, no M14 (Derelict Authoring is designed: DA-23 declares `fixture_subkind_emergency`). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; DOCK_STATUS added 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X2)*
- **If a dock room has no DOCK_STATUS glow, or two**: the authoring lint refuses it (exactly one per dock room, Derelict Authoring's rule); at run time the pod console would read unlit and `pod_hop` would refuse `NOT_LIT`, which the lint exists to make unreachable. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X2)*
- **If the pod leaves a dock or parks at one**: Web Map's apply that writes `pod_at` calls E10 off for the dock it leaves and on for the dock it parks at; the glow changes with no ARMING and no commit-spacing guard, so there is no `COOLDOWN` to retry and no case in which a refused call leaves the glow lit over an empty cradle; a call for an unknown section, or one with no glow, changes nothing, returns OK with `out.code` `NO_DOCK_GLOW` and counts `emitter_dock_status_missing` (a defect the authoring lint exists to prevent). At `init_world` the home dock's glow is on after step 7 and the derelict docks' glows are off.  *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X2, D32)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **If a lamp is toggled again inside `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` of its last realised transition**: the command is rejected `COOLDOWN` (a beam `TOO_SOON`), `switch_on` is unchanged and nothing is lit or darkened; an OFF during ARMING is exempt and cancels (D.2b). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D32)*
- **If a lantern's machine is switched off (requested load 0) or starved (breaker open, or served below its request)**: output follows the Graph's `run_pm` clamped to 0..1000 — 0 for a switched-off machine, `base_pm × the served fraction` for a starved or browned-out one; there is no fallback form (D.3). **If a REACTOR reports `run_pm` above 1000** (output above rated): the clamp holds the lantern at `base_pm`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Beam and pose**

- **If `toggle_beam` applies**: `switch_on` flips and the beam is lit on the apply tick; a turn-ON at zero charge is rejected `CHARGE_EMPTY` and the light does not change (the pre-played click is the Presenter's).
- **If charge reaches zero mid-channel**: Suit's `force_off` lands before the same tick's `emitter.place`; the owner's 6-tick re-check then finds the target unlit and aborts, so at most five ticks of channel run on a dark target.
- **If the actor is DOWNED**: the beam keeps its committed state, aim frozen at the last committed value, origin from the down row's chest point; a downed crewmate is a locator. **If DEAD**: forced off at that tick, unobservable in the MVP because the death stub restarts from the last autosave at that tick's P6.
- **If the actor is STAGGERED**: the beam is unchanged and `toggle_beam` is rejected `NOT_ACTIONABLE` (a defensive double of the Controller's input-ignore).
- **If the player runs with aim different from facing**: the beam follows the committed aim; the split axes are ±206 units off aim, not facing.
- **If an interactable lies between the chest and the feet in screen-y while the player faces up-screen**: it is behind the apex, unlit and unprompted unless it lies within the foot spill: the sim adds no hidden compensation beyond the ruled spill (EM-6). `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` ships at 24 (user ruling D24, 2026-10-27; Open Question 17), so an item within 18 px of the feet is lit and prompts while the beam is on, an item a full tile (48 px) to the side is not, and with the beam off there is no spill; AC-10b tests the ruled default and the spill-off boundary. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **If the feet stand 1–44 px south of the first legal row in front of a north wall** (the band over all `CHEST_POINT` rows: `h − 24 ≤ s ≤ h − 1`, 10–33 px for the walk row): the chest point is inside the footprint, the apex stays at the chest, and the occluder segment starts at the foot, so a north-aimed beam is blocked at once and a south-aimed beam is unaffected; whether Lighting draws the same (a beam that rendered black here would be a P1 lie) is AC-57 and the pre-implementation gate of EM-6. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If the avatar is mirrored (`flip_h`)**: the chest point's x moves by up to 4 px and the cones are recomputed from the new apex in the same tick; a mirrored scene yields the exact mirror image of every `lux_pm` and `lit` (I-MIRROR).
- **If any animation row changes the chest point (run lean, carry, work, stagger, down)**: the apex moves by the data delta in the same tick with no smoothing.
- **If the mouse flicks the aim**: the committed aim lags the drawn aim by up to 2.8° and 3 ticks; a subject in that sliver may disagree with the drawn edge for up to 3 ticks, tolerated by the 6-tick re-check.
- **If a subject lies in the split beam's dark wedge**: it is in neither cone and contributes 0 at every aim (the wedge is at least 16° and never closes); dead ahead of a split beam is dark, by design.
- **If a subject is exactly on the hard edge (`d2 = R²`) or exactly on a cone boundary ray**: it is inside (the edge is closed and the cone test is `≥`); one pixel farther it is outside. **If a subject is at the apex (`d2 = 0`)**: it is inside every cone of that emitter.
- **If the owner is alone with the lamp on**: the owner's ACTOR `lux_pm` is the beam's full `output_pm` (1000), facing- and occlusion-independent, so Horror sees a lit player and Attention counts the beam once from the emitter list; the owner's `act_lux_pm` gains nothing and Attention must not add the beam again.
- **If `bind_beam` changes the shape**: it takes effect at the next `emitter.place`; there is no input for it, and a shape change is a loadout event.

**Occlusion, queries and caps**

- **If a segment only touches a rectangle (corner graze), crosses a seam between abutting rectangles (wall run, door-leaf/jamb joint) or passes through a corner shared by two diagonal rectangles**: it is blocked, because cells are unit squares and abutting boxes share a face (EM-20 a, D.6); an oblique ray never slips through a seam. An anchor within `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` of a footprint fails the registration lint, and an anchor found strictly inside a footprint at run time is blocked from every emitter and reads dark. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a door closes mid-channel**: the next `emitter.place` observes the `OccluderSource.version(section_id)` change, rebuilds the static layer for the section, the target reads dark and the owner's re-check aborts the channel within 6 ticks.
- **If a door is partly open**: the door-leaf rectangle is the Layout's `door_leaf_gap` projection (`leaf_w` continuous in the Graph's opening area; `leaf_w` 0 yields no rectangle), so light passes exactly the gap the leaf leaves; `DOOR_LIGHT_OPEN_PM` is deprecated and unused.
- **If a horror or an actor stands between an emitter and a subject**: nothing is occluded and the subject beyond stays lit; a shadow with no occluder is a stage-2 symptom and must stay an unambiguous tell.
- **If a carried item is dropped**: it re-registers as a static ITEM subject at the drop anchor and is evaluated at the next `emitter.place` (it was unregistered at pickup, EM-21).
- **If the subject is an unregistered ref (including a Perception Filter phantom target)**: `lux` 0 and `lit` false, indistinguishable from a registered unlit subject in release, so the query is no existence oracle; `registered(ref)` exists in debug builds only.
- **If registrations exceed `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS`, or moving registrations exceed `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS`**: `register_subject` reports `OVER_CAP` (or `OVER_MOVING_CAP`) in `out.code`, `emitter_subjects_overflow` (or `emitter_moving_overflow`) increments in release builds as telemetry, the subject reads dark (fails safe) and a debug assertion fires. **This is a content or budget bug and never a gameplay state**: a real object inside a beam that refuses to prompt would break the honest edge (design test 1) and read as a hallucination (the P4 "reach for it" falsifier), so the per-category budgets (Derelict Authoring: authored objects, items, dropped loot and spawned salvage; Horror Entities: horror subjects; Interaction: dropped items) are held by I-CAPS (d), a breach seen in telemetry is fixed in the owning budget, and no design may rely on the refusal. **If a source calls `probe()` or `lit_by()` in the MVP**: the lint fails the build (EM-24; both are deferred to VS-A). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a subject's `body_id` differs from an emitter's (an unwelded section, a section cut off, a pod)**: that emitter contributes 0 (EM-30); cross-body light between unwelded bodies is a Ship Body question for VS-A. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*
- **If a weld joins two bodies (the MVP home+B weld)**: in the tick the weld applies every record and subject of the absorbed body re-derives to the surviving (lower-id) body (EM-4, EM-23 W0); the beam and ACTOR (already on the home body via Body) now light B's objects, and B's fixtures, lanterns, glowsticks, fault glows and objects count toward the player; the new hatch leaf starts closed (`leaf_w` = `width_px`, LY-16), so the hatch blocks light until the opening area is > 0 and no light leaks through a closed leaf. **If a cut separates them**: records and subjects of the new component re-derive to the new `body_id`, the cross-seam rows are dropped in the same tick, and objects that were lit across the seam go dark that tick (hysteresis: `act_lux_pm` 0 < `LIT_OFF_PM` 120). A glowstick in a cut-off section keeps its section and follows its section's body. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*
- **If in-view emitters exceed Lighting's budget (24 non-shadow + 6 shadow, 8 lanterns)**: the sim never culls by view, a cut light still exposes (P3), a debug assertion reports it and `priority` makes Lighting's cut reproducible; the authoring lint should have refused the content.

**Deployables, beacon and fault glow**

- **If a glowstick lands on a dormant or unknown section**: `spawn_deployable` is a no-op reporting `NO_SECTION`, Interaction has already spent the item and no light ever existed. **If it lands inside a closed room**: it lights that room only (the walls occlude).
- **If two glowsticks overlap**: contributions saturate at 1000 and each wanes on its own clock. **If a spawn exceeds `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE`**: the new stick spawns in full (Class B never rejects) and the lowest-id non-evicting deployable of the same kind enters a visible evict-wane (D.9b): its reach falls from where it is to 24 px over `GLOWSTICK_EVICT_WANE_S` (10 s) and only then is it removed, so no light dies with no physical cause; `emitter_deployable_evictions` increments. **If an eviction would exceed `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_EVICTING`**: the oldest evicting stick completes at once, `emitter_deployable_evict_overflow` increments and a debug assertion fires (a budget defect, I-SUPPLY). **If a section is discarded with live deployables**: they are removed with it.
- **If `life_remaining_ticks` reaches 0**: the record is removed that tick and its id retired, the subjects it lit re-read next tick, and there is no fade (the waning already took the reach to 24 px).
- **If `set_beacon` turns the beacon on**: it starts from the angle the pure function gives at that tick, not phase 0; off, it contributes 0 from the next tick. **If its bus is dead**: `output_pm` is 0 (D.1) while the aim stays a pure function of `T`.
- **If the beacon sweeps a subject in an otherwise dark room**: `lux_pm` is above 0 for 14 or 15 of every 128 ticks (by phase; the same count in two runs and across a restore) and `lit` stays false, so no prompt strobes and no channel aborts at the sweep rate.
- **If more than `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` descriptors have glow rows**: the unprotected records with the lowest `sev` (ties: the higher descriptor id first) are evicted and `emitter_fault_glow_evictions` increments; a record younger than `ticks_for_seconds(W_MIN_WARNING_S)` (600 ticks) is never evicted, and if every candidate is protected nothing is evicted, the table stays over the cap, `emitter_fault_glow_overcap` increments and a debug assertion fires, with the table trimmed on the first Graph tick that has an unprotected candidate. The MVP slice does not reach the cap: a welded body carries at most 6 simultaneous glow rows of the 8 live faults (registry `mvp_fault_allocation`), and 10 ≥ ⌈1.25 × 6⌉ = 8 (I-CAPS (c)). An evicted glow removes only the light and exposure; the Failure & Trail overlay on the sprite still shows the fault. **If a descriptor's `sev` falls to 0 or its `intensity_pm` ends**: the record is removed on that Graph tick.
- **If a long-aged fault (a SCRAM held for minutes) keeps its descriptor while Failure & Trail's overlay decays** (Derelict Authoring OQ-V3): the FAULT_GLOW `output_pm` follows the descriptor's `sev` alone (D.11) and never the overlay's age; overlay decay is Failure & Trail's presentation and changes neither `output_pm` nor exposure, so a glow that should fade with age must be expressed by F&T as a falling `sev` or `intensity_pm`.
- **If a `mod_lamp` sits on the lowest `power_tier`** (Derelict Authoring OQ-V4): its output is `base_pm × served_pm / 1000` (D.1); the lowest tier is the first shed under a shortfall, so it reads dimmer only while its tier is under-served (`served_pm` < 1000) and identical to any other lamp at full supply; whether a given `served_pm` reads *visibly* dimmer is the Lighting spike's to confirm.
- **If a sprite carries an emissive `_msk` trail glow**: it is an unshaded overlay, not an emitter, and exposes nothing.

**Save, restore, boot and co-op**

- **If a game is restored with a subject between `LIT_OFF_PM` and `LIT_ON_PM`**: its `lit` bit is canonical and copied verbatim (the hysteresis is never called in `post_restore`), so `validate` at T+1 reads the same answer as in the uninterrupted run (kernel G4).
- **If a save is taken at a Graph tick and restored**: `served_pm` is a persisted hook product read as-is after restore and the Graph's `post_restore` rebuilds only the descriptor list and derived caches (ADR-0011 §6.1) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0011 sync)*, so the subject table and every lamp `output_pm` match uninterrupted play at the next five ticks; the Graph now does so (LS-21 and its restore edge case, 2026-10-27), and AC-41 fails if a regression removes the rebuild (lamps would read dark for up to 5 ticks). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a restore completes (the kernel binding table is rebuilt)**: the kernel's LOADING rebuild re-binds the seat beams from `beam_bindings()` before the first RUNNING tick (KRN-BIND-01); there is no window in which `toggle_beam` meets an empty table. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*
- **If a registrant's own window differs from the restored registrations after a restore**: nothing is lost (registrations are canonical and restore verbatim); its `post_restore` only compares and writes nothing, its first ordinary hook run repairs the drift through E6 / E7 / E8 with at most a one-run lag, and a repeat registration is a no-op reporting `DUPLICATE` (ADR-0017 D10.2, CT-38) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*
- **If a hook id is unknown or creates a cycle at boot** (for example someone registers `after: [emitter.update]`): boot fails loudly (CR-3); only `emitter.place` exists. **If the kernel is FAULTED during a mutator apply**: standard CR-9 handling; every mutator here is Class B or validated, so a fault is a defect.
- **If the kernel is `MIRROR`**: it never ticks `emitter.place`; the replica reads `SHARED_SUMMARY`, which in VS-B must carry each beam's origin, aim, shape and switch so a remote crewmate's beam can be drawn.
- **If zoom changes**: nothing in the sim changes; an off-screen lit object stays lit and exposes, and the HUD draws no off-screen prompt.

**Hallucination and degenerate strategies**

- **If the Perception Filter shows a lamp as broken or a dead lamp as working, or adds a decoy light**: `emitter.state` still holds the real `output_pm`, so the room is as lit as it was and the prompt follows the real light; the false lamp is a Lighting visual that exposes nothing, and walking to the real edge is the falsifier.
- **If the player (or a replay, a script or a remote submitter) strobes the beam**: every accepted toggle is committed state and visible to Attention, and the sim rejects a toggle applied fewer than `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` (12) after the last accepted one with `TOO_SOON` in both directions (EM-7, D.2b), so the beam flips at most five times in a rolling second, at most three flashes (ceil unit), and Lighting's flash governor never has to delay a committed flip; the Adapter's debounce (to be at least 12) still drops the press before it is submitted. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; user rulings: D32)*
- **If the player goes dark (beam off, no fixtures)**: `lux_pm` is 0 everywhere except where a self-powered fixture shines (the cryo-bay lamp; a dock room's DOCK_STATUS glow while the pod is parked there, X2), nothing else is lit and no lit-only verb is possible outside those pools; darkness is never free (only `drop`, `toggle_beam`, `throw_glowstick` and the inspect card of an already-lit instrument remain, registry `act_gate`). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **If the player lays glowsticks everywhere or abandons a lit fixture**: each still adds to `section_emission` and Attention's cost, the live cap is 8 and each wanes; light is a spend, never a safe state.
- **If Horror wants a light query beyond `lit(ref)`, `lux(ref)` and `emitters_near`**: there is none in the MVP — `probe` and `lit_by` are deferred to VS-A (EM-24), so no consumer can starve the mandatory work through a probe budget. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

## Dependencies

*Status as of 2026-10-27 (refreshed by the design review): every counterpart is Status Designed — the Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Player Controller & Input, Item DB, Save/Load, Failure & Trail, and now Layout, Ship Body, Suit, Interaction, 2D Lighting, Attention, Horror, Derelict Authoring, Assembly and Web Map. A tag that still says provisional waits on a named sentence of a counterpart, not on a missing GDD.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Upstream — this GDD depends on:**

| System | Direction | Hard / Soft | Interface | Counterpart text names this system? | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Command & Simulation Layer | this depends on it | Hard | block registry contract (CR-8), hook `emitter.place` (CR-3), command registration and `actor_binding` (CR-4, CR-5, CR-7), `WriteScope` (CR-9), dirty keys (CR-10), isolation (CR-12), determinism (CR-13), H10 | yes — C.3 rows 4, 7, 9, 13 and the dependency row "Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query \| Hard \| Registry, pure-read query rules \| Emitter block and commands \| Yes" | designed |
| Life-Support Graph | this depends on it | Hard | M14 `set_load_request`, `served_pm(node_id)`, `node(id)`, `failures(filter)`, `section_of`, `nodes_in(cid)`; `node(id).run_pm` for lanterns (delivered, LS-19 additions; clamped to 0..1000 here); hook `graph.propagate.failure` (the last Graph hook, which `emitter.place` is `after:`); `door_state` (through Layout's projection); `served_pm` is a persisted hook product read as-is after restore and the Graph's `post_restore` rebuilds only the descriptor list and derived caches (ADR-0011 §6.1; LS-21; the `served_pm_last` fallback is not needed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0011 sync)* | yes — Interactions table; Dependencies downstream row; M14 row | designed |
| Player Controller & Input | this depends on it | Hard | `ControllerView.get_chest_x` / `get_chest_y`, `get_aim_angle`, `get_loco_state`; `body.state` `pos_curr`; `toggle_beam`; the `lit` field name; `CHEST_POINT` | yes — Interactions row 4; Dependencies row (stale wording, refresh owed) | designed |
| Ship Section & Layout Data Model | this depends on it | **Hard** (occluding rectangles, fixture anchors, door leaves) | `OccluderSource` (`rects_in(body_id, ax0, ay0, ax1, ay1, out)` over a `QueryBuffer`, `version(section_id)`, `version_epoch()`, `occludes_light`); `body_of`, `section_offset`, `static_version`, `changed_region` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | yes — Layout LY-12 (the `OccluderSource` contract) and its Interactions row for this system | designed. **Not an index edge**: the index lists only three systems under this system's Depends On; the edge is routed to the index owner (Open Question 15) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

| Web Map & Transit | depends on this | Hard | E1, E2, E10 `set_dock_status` (EM-5, EM-11), `lit(ref)` for the pod console subject *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X2)* | designed (`web-map-and-transit.md`); its text to be refreshed by its own editor *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| Perception Filter, Sanity, Adaptive Audio, HUD, Debug Overlay, Failure & Trail, Save/Load | read or are bounded by this | Soft | read view, the closed consequence-channel list, dirty keys | designed (each counterpart GDD is Status Designed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Circularity check.** The index's circular-dependency note says "Lighting exposes an emitter registry; Attention reads it and publishes an attention level; the filament renderer reads both. Lighting never reads Attention." This GDD resolves the first clause: the registry is **`emitter.state`'s read view**, which Lighting and Attention both read, and Lighting never writes it (a wording refresh owed to the index, Cross-References). No cycle exists: Emitter State reads the Graph, the Controller, Body and Layout and none of them reads it back (the Controller's PC-6 *calls* the exposure query, a read, from `validate`, and the Graph reads nothing from here); Attention's `set_beacon` is a `SYSTEM` command applied at T+1.

**Engine reference gaps (Godot 4.7.2, pinned).** `docs/engine-reference/godot/` has **no `scripting.md` or `core.md`** (its `modules/` holds animation, audio, input, navigation, networking, physics, rendering and ui): GDScript integer-math cost, `RefCounted` state blocks and packed-array performance are **NOT SOURCEABLE** from the reference and every cost number here is a model parameter for the spike. The 2D physics ray API is likewise **NOT SOURCEABLE** (`modules/physics.md` carries only a 3D example); EM-20 removes the dependency. 2D physics is unchanged since 4.3 (the reference says so for 4.6). Sourced breaking changes that bite this module (`breaking-changes.md`): 4.7 typed-return overrides need an explicit `return` on every path (a typed-return virtual in an emitter-kind base class would break — avoid virtual dispatch on the hot path); a write to one element of a packed array does not call the property setter, so dirty keys are marked explicitly in the mutators, never in setters (kernel CR-8, Graph LS-18); `duplicate()` is not deep, so snapshot copies use plain-data copies or `duplicate_deep()`. Light2D behaviour in 4.5–4.7 is Lighting's HIGH-risk gap, not this system's (it never reads one). No ADR exists yet; candidates are listed in the Overview.

The Lighting spike is art bible 8.12 step 2.*

*Not knobs of this GDD (cite, never restate with another value) — registry (locked):* `TICK_RATE` 60, `TILE_PX` 48, `AIM_UNITS` 4096, `REACH_PX` 72, `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` 24, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4, `THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30, `THROW_RANGE_TILES` 3, `flash_count`, `BEAM_TOGGLE_MIN_INTERVAL_TICKS` 10 (Player Controller, range 10–20; the Adapter's debounce, which must rise to at least `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` 12, I-SPACING) and `TAP_REARM_TICKS` 12 (Interaction) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, the Graph's `INRUSH_STEPS` 5 and `INRUSH_PM` 1500; *owner-GDD tuning rows (not registry entries), cited by name:* the Controller's `AIM_SEND_THRESHOLD` 32 units, `AIM_SETTLE_TICKS` and `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN`. *Knob interactions that matter:* the pair `LIT_ON_PM` / `EDGE_FLOOR_PM` (I-EDGE) decides whether the drawn hard edge is the actionable edge; `LIT_ON_PM` / `LIT_OFF_PM` / the soft radii decide how wide the hysteresis band is in px (4 px at a fixture, 3 px at a glowstick) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS`, `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` and `MAX_EMITTERS` trade against each other inside `EXPOSURE_BUDGET_MS` through D.14, and nothing in the table raises one without the spike result.

## Visual/Audio Requirements

*This block draws and plays nothing. It publishes facts (the emitter read view, the shape table, dirty keys) that the renderer and the audio mixer conform to; every item below is owned by the GDD named in its row and is listed so that the owner's brief is complete. A sentence that needs `Light2D` to be true belongs to 2D Lighting & Occlusion (undesigned; sibling stage), never to this document.*

**What is visible.** Light is the only thing this system shows. A beam is a wedge with a **hard outer edge exactly at its reach** (3 screen px of edge, then a soft interior falloff) in the Tungsten hue every human light uses (art bible 4.2); the four shapes, the fan, the deployable pool, the glowstick, the fixture and the beacon are the art bible 8.3 rows (lines 1079–1086) carried verbatim into the shape table, with two rows this GDD adds (lantern, fault glow) and the HARD / SOFT curve classes owed back to 8.3 (Cross-References). A glowstick is Chemlight `#86B896`, steady and then visibly smaller (art bible 3.2: "steady, then waning"); a fixture never flickers (the cryo-bay lamp's gutter is Lighting's, art bible 8.11 flag 2); the dock status glow is a small, dim, steady fixture whose luminance and hue the art-director keeps apart from the visible-unknown lamp (a Lighting `hue_class` token and the luminance separation are owed by the art-director and art bible 7.0 note; X2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; a node lantern is the glow of a running machine in that loop's own hue at its machinery node (art bible 8.3 emission rule, 8.10 flag 8); fault glows are point sources (ember, power arc, cyan O₂ mist) taken from the Failure & Trail descriptor-to-glow table. Everything is drawn from `emitter.state` and nothing else.

**What Lighting reads, and must not do.** Lighting reads the per-emitter read view (`id, kind, subkind, shape_id, origin_px, aim_units, half_angle_units, reach_px, r_lit_px, r_off_px, curve_class, output_pm, hue_class, lit, section_id, body_id, priority`, AC-46b) and `shapes()`, and it conforms to them: a texture's reach equals `reach_px` ± 1 px for every hard-edged row; a soft pool's boundary is drawn at max(`r_off_px`, `POOL_RESIDUAL_PX`) ± 1 px (D.7, exact sim values), so that the prompt of an already-lit subject ends exactly where the pool visibly ends and an approaching subject meets its prompt `r_off_px − r_lit_px` px inside it *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* (the sim/render agreement, AC-55 and AC-56, the load-bearing P1 test of the lighting stack); a dimmed emitter is drawn dimmer in proportion to `output_pm` (a lamp never renders brighter than the power it is served, Graph D.6). Lighting **owns** the light textures, ambient and `CanvasModulate`, shadow-slot assignment (lint hypotheses: at most 6 shadow-casting, 24 non-shadow and 8 node lanterns in view, art bible 8.10 and 8.11 flags 2 and 8; "emitters are cut before occlusion"), flicker and gutter, the per-light flash clamp (`flash_count` ≤ 3 per rolling 1 s, pipeline PR-13), and the decoy visuals of the Perception Filter. Lighting **must not**: write an emitter or any block; tell the sim a light is "culled"; give a decoy, a false lamp state or a flicker any query effect (the query reads `output_pm`, not the picture; AC-5a); move the true edge of a real light (art bible Principle 1; the stage-2 "cone seems to reach further" symptom is a **decoy glow past the true edge** with a palette leak, never a wider revealing radius, Cross-References refresh to 4.4); or draw a shadow with no occluder from a horror (actors and horrors never occlude, art bible 3.2, EM-20 c). Occlusion shadows follow the same footprint rectangles the query uses (one source for collision, light and shadow), and the same cell convention: Lighting's occluder polygon for a stored rectangle `[x0, x1) × [y0, y1)` is the continuous rectangle covering exactly those pixel cells, and an integer position is the centre of its cell (EM-20 a), so a drawn shadow has no seam where the query has none *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the shadow origin versus the sim's foot segment (EM-6, CP-5) is Open Question 5.

**What Audio hears.** Audio keys off the `E/<emitter id>` dirty keys (EM-26) and the Presenter's own pre-play, and reads **no exposure** (it does not call `lux` or `lit`). The beam switch click and the wall-switch click pre-play on input (PC-5; the light itself lands on commit); a lamp hum rises across the 0–5 tick switch latency so the warm-up reads as a ship lamp, not lag; a bank switched together draws inrush and the neighbouring dip is a legitimate, audible, player-caused flicker (Graph `INRUSH_PM`); a glowstick may thin a faint chemical hiss as it wanes and ends with a soft quiet; the beacon's sweep has a rotation cadence at `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` 128 (2.13 s); fault glows share their descriptor's audio (Failure & Trail). **Light itself is silent**: this system emits no noise into Attention's intake (noise is a tool property, PC-10).

| Event | Visual feedback (owner) | Audio feedback (owner) | Priority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chest beam toggled on or off | switch animation and click pre-play; the cone appears on the committed tick, at the exact `reach_px`, never predicted (Presenter, Lighting) | switch click on input (Audio) | MVP, must-have |
| Fixture switched on | warms across 0–5 ticks to `base_pm × served_pm / 1000` (Lighting) | relay clunk, hum rise (Audio) | MVP |
| Bank inrush or brownout | neighbouring lamps dim honestly with `served_pm` (Lighting) | strained hum (Audio) | MVP |
| Glowstick lands, wanes, ends (an evicted stick takes the same shrink over 10 s, never a pop; EM-15) | Chemlight pool blooms, holds, shrinks in integer steps, vanishes (Lighting) | landing tick, faint hiss, thinning, quiet (Audio) | MVP |
| Beacon on | 20° sweep, 2.13 s period, hard edge (Lighting) | alarm cadence keyed to the sweep (Audio, Attention) | MVP |
| Fault glow appears | ember, arc or mist point source on the Graph tick of its descriptor (Lighting, Failure & Trail) | descriptor audio (Failure & Trail) | MVP |
| Prompt crosses the lit edge | HUD prompt present or absent at `lit(ref)` (HUD, Interaction); no exposure overlay of any kind | none | MVP, must-have |
| Debug Overlay | cones, occlusion segments, subject `lux_pm`, `unknown_subject_queries` (DIAGNOSTIC subscriber, absent from release) | none | tooling |

**Art bible sections that apply:** 3.2 (silhouette philosophy: no occluding shadow for horrors, steady-then-waning glowstick), 4.1 and 4.2 (Tungsten human light, emission hues), 4.4 (the sanity palette: a decoy past the true edge needs a palette leak), 4.5 (luminance ladder: the edge must read in greyscale), 7.0 (the illumination threshold that enables prompt drawing is the technical-artist's), 8.3 (the beam table), 8.10 (lighting hypotheses and lint) and 8.11 flags 2 and 8 (shadow slots, node lanterns), 8.12 step 2 (the Lighting spike that confirms `LIT_ON_PM` and `EDGE_FLOOR_PM` against the rendered edge).

📌 **Asset Spec** — Visual/Audio requirements are defined. After the art bible is approved, run `/asset-spec system:emitter-state-and-light-exposure-query` to produce per-asset visual descriptions, dimensions, and generation prompts from this section.

## Game Feel

*A real note, not a template fill: this system has no animation, no input of its own and no hit; its feel is the **honesty and timing of light**. The feel of the toggle's input belongs to the Player Controller (PC-5 and its debounce; the sim-side `TOO_SOON` floor beneath it is EM-7), the feel of how light looks to 2D Lighting & Occlusion, the feel of every sound to Audio; this GDD owns the numbers underneath them, which are below.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Feel reference.** The chest lamp should feel like Pandorum's helmet light and the Alien: Isolation torch in one respect only: it is *yours*, hands-free, always where you are facing, and it is the one thing in the game that never lies. The ship's lamps should feel like a machine that has to be served: they come up with the ship, not ahead of it.

**Input responsiveness (beam).** The `toggle_beam` press applies at P2 and the light is committed in the same tick's `emitter.place`: zero internal latency, at most one tick (16.7 ms) from apply to the lit state the renderer sees, inside the Controller's ≤ 17 ms click-to-commit budget (PC-5). The click and the switch animation pre-play; the **light never does** (the Presenter never predicts beam state: the lamp is the human-channel anchor and may not lie for one frame). Turn-off is immediate. Feel target: decisive, mechanical, slightly heavy; never a lag the player reads as the game being slow, never a flash the player reads as the game being wrong.

**Ship lamps warm; the chest lamp snaps.** A fixture switched on is ARMING until the Graph serves it: 0 to `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` − 1 ticks (0–83 ms), below perception on its own, and Audio's relay clunk and hum rise fill it so it reads as a ship lamp, not as lag. Under a weak bus a bank of lamps switched together draws `INRUSH_PM` 1500 for `INRUSH_STEPS` 5 Graph runs (0.5 s): the neighbouring lamps dip (the Graph example is 945 permille) and recover. That dip is an honest, audible, **player-caused** flicker and the best single lesson the ship teaches about load; it must never be smoothed away.

**The rim.** At full output the edge of a HARD emitter is a step (about 252 permille inside, 0 outside; `EDGE_FLOOR_PM` 250 with `LIT_ON_PM` 150), so an object pops into reach at an exact pixel. The 150 / 120 hysteresis never sees that step; it bites only on **dimmed** emitters (brownout, waning glowsticks) whose interior crosses the threshold band, so a prompt does not strobe in a fade. While the aim is sweeping, committed-versus-drawn divergence of up to 2.8° and ≤ 3 ticks (PC-5) moves the rim by about 14 px at 288 px (WIDE) and 19 px at 384 px (NARROW); a rim object may prompt a few ticks late and rim chatter is held by the Controller's `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN`, not by this block. The edge-agreement test (design test 1) holds **at rest and for full-output emitters**; a dimmed light's actionable area shrinks honestly (a fixture at 500 permille loses prompts for objects within 22 px of its full-output readable edge (`r_lit` falls from 122 to 100 px): "the junction box at the edge went out of reach when the scrubber drew").

**Glowstick wane and beacon.** A glowstick is steady for its first 60 % of life, then shrinks over the rest in visible integer steps; the shrinking pool is the only clock and no number is ever shown. A stick put out by the live cap is never snuffed: it takes the same visible shrink, faster (10 s, D.9b), so the pool still only ever dies by shrinking. The beacon lights a point for 14 or 15 of every 128 ticks, so it is *seen* and never gates a prompt (EM-16): nothing strobes at the sweep rate.

**The split beam's dark wedge is its identity.** Two cones at ±18° off the aim with a wedge of at least 16° that never closes: the one shape that cannot see straight ahead. It is a loadout personality, not a bug; nobody should "fix" it.

**Impact moments.** (1) A brownout takes the corridor lamps down and, when power returns, the corridor beyond is exactly where it was (the world never lied). (2) A glowstick thrown into the next room: the pool blooms Chemlight green on landing, not in flight. (3) A lamp bank clicking on and the whole deck sagging for half a second. (4) The tell: filaments resolving at the cone edge, which needs this stable edge to resolve at.

**Feel acceptance.** AC-FEEL (manual, retained screenshots, technical-artist and game-designer sign-off) covers the toggle, the inrush dip, the rim at rest at three zoom rungs, and the glowstick wane. Weight and responsiveness profile: instant for the beam and turn-off, 0–5 ticks for a fixture, 0.5 s for a bank dip, 150 s for a glowstick.

## UI Requirements

**No UI of its own.** This system draws no HUD element, menu, instrument or world-space marker, and none is permitted: no exposure bar, ring, icon, "light level" or visibility readout, and no marker on a lit or unlit object (P3: shown in the world, never a meter; Attention is likewise a thing the world does). The only gameplay surface that follows from this block is a **prompt that is present or absent**, and its drawing is owned elsewhere. The surfaces below are the readers of this block's answers; each states what it needs and from where.

| Information | Display location (owner) | Update frequency | Condition |
|---|---|---|---|
| Verb prompt for a target | HUD, anchored with `world_to_hud(target)` (pixel-perfect pipeline; Interaction decides the verb) | every presentation frame from the last committed table | drawn iff `lit(ref)`; absent otherwise; the inspect card of an already-lit instrument is the Controller's exception (PC-6) |
| Rejection feedback for a lit-only verb | the failed-action cue (Interaction; `NOT_LIT` is Interaction's reason) | on the rejected command | `lit(ref)` was false at `validate` |
| Beam and tool charge reading | the suit HUD gauge (Suit & Equipment; art bible HUD set, 7.0) | Suit's divisor-6 vitals | the charge belongs to Suit; this block supplies only `emitter.state.lit` on the beam to drain it |
| Beam shape and toggle state | the tool-readout surface (Suit loadout; Controller draws the hand animation) | on `bind_beam` and on commit | the shape is loadout, never input (EM-8); the lamp itself is the readout |
| Debug Overlay | developer overlay (DIAGNOSTIC subscriber; absent from release) | per frame | cones, occlusion segments and origins, subject `lux_pm` and `lit`, `unknown_subject_queries`, `emitter_subjects_overflow`, `emitter_moving_overflow`, `emitter_rebuild_over_ceiling` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

*Requirements placed on the owners (not drawn here).* The HUD and Interaction never draw a prompt where `lit(subject)` is false, **including for a phantom salvage target** (kernel Open Question 14; an unregistered ref answers like an unlit one, EM-21), because "reach for it" is only a falsifier if the prompt follows the true edge. The prompt must not flicker faster than the Controller's focus stickiness allows (hysteresis is this block's, rim stickiness the Controller's). The inspect card appears only on a lit instrument. Accessibility: nothing about exposure is conveyed by colour alone (there is no exposure display); the edge must read in greyscale (art bible 4.5); a strobe of the chest lamp is bounded by the Adapter's debounce for the local player and by this block's `TOO_SOON` guard for every submitter (EM-7). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

No UX Flag is raised: there is no screen or HUD element of this system to specify with `/ux-design`. The prompt, the failed-action cue and the charge gauge are specified in the HUD, Interaction and Suit UX work (`design/ux/hud.md`; none yet exists), which cite this table.

## Acceptance Criteria

*One-line GIVEN / WHEN / THEN, written so a tester can run each from the constant block below plus the line alone. Tags: `(auto)` automated test in `tests/unit/emitter/` (Logic, BLOCKING by default); `(auto-int)` automated integration test in `tests/integration/emitter/` against a real counterpart block or a named stub (Integration, BLOCKING); `(lint)` static or build lint (Logic); `(manual)` launched build with a retained screenshot under `production/qa/evidence/` plus technical-artist and game-designer sign-off (Visual/Feel and UI, BLOCKING; never waived by `qa.level`); `(spike)` needs a prototype or a system that does not exist yet (2D Lighting, Suit, Interaction, Layout); `(review)` a named-lead document review, mapped to Config/Data and ADVISORY (it is not an evidence class of its own in the testing standard). `qa.level` resolves to `full` from `modes.rigor: full` in `project.yaml` (the config-resolution rigor table), so no Logic or Integration evidence in this section is waivable, and the screenshots never are (Open Question 18 is closed). **Ids are stable, not ordered:** other GDDs cite AC-24, AC-60 and AC-90 by number, so criteria added or split by the 2026-10-27 design review take the next free id or a letter suffix and nothing is renumbered. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* No Logic or Integration criterion is ever recorded as passing by a screenshot. Brackets name the rule or formula covered. Criteria whose counterpart is undesigned are marked "(provisional — <system> undesigned)" and are not BLOCKING until it lands.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Spike-tagged criteria** (`(spike)`: AC-53, AC-55 to AC-57) are neither BLOCKING nor ADVISORY while their spike has not run: each is reported **NOT ASSESSED** (never passed, never failed) by `/gate-check` and `/smoke-check`, names its spike story and pass line, and becomes BLOCKING the day the spike reports (EM-SPIKE-1 for AC-53, the Lighting spike for AC-55 to AC-57). **Debug assertions are not observable headless**: wherever a criterion says a debug build "asserts", the test observes the assertion through the Diagnostics seam (`FakeDiagnostics.assert_reports`, a list of codes), because a real `assert()` would halt the headless runner and is stripped from Release exports.

**Fixture constants** (constant data files, never inline magic numbers outside boundary tests):

| Name | Value | Name | Value |
|---|---|---|---|
| `TICK_RATE` | 60 | `LIT_ON_PM` / `LIT_OFF_PM` | 150 / 120 |
| `TILE_PX` | 48 | `EDGE_FLOOR_PM` | 250 |
| `AIM_UNITS` | 4096 (0 = E, counter-clockwise, 3072 = S) | `BEAM_BASE_PM` | 1000 |
| `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` | 6 (the power-hook tick is `T mod 6 = 0`, the failure-hook tick `T mod 6 = 4`; EM-1) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` | 1 |
| `INRUSH_PM` / `INRUSH_STEPS` | 1500 / 5 | `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` | 24 |
| `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` | 192 | `MAX_EMITTERS` | 96 |
| `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS` | 16 | `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` | 8 |
| `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` | 10 | `REACH_MIN_PX` / `REACH_MAX_PX` | 24 / 384 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_EVICTING` | 4 | `GLOWSTICK_EVICT_WANE_S` | 10 (600 ticks) |
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (Graph) | 10 (600 ticks) | per-category subject budgets (Σ, registry `subject_budget`, S4) | 174 |
| `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` | 256 | `OUTPUT_DIRTY_QUANTUM_PM` | 50 |
| `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` | 128 | `SPLIT_AXIS_UNITS` | 206 |
| `SPLIT_HALF_UNITS` | 114 (the split row's `half_angle_units`) | `LANTERN_REACH_PX` | 96 |
| `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` | 150 (9000 ticks by `ticks_for_seconds`; life at landing 9000 − `THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30 = 8970) | `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM` / `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM` | 400 / 250 |
| `THROW_ARC_TICKS` | 30 | `GLOWSTICK_BASE_PM` | 1000 |

Shape reach in px (half-angle in 1/4096 turn): WIDE 288 (341), NARROW 384 (137), SPLIT 336 (114 each, axes aim ± 206), FAN 168 (683), BEACON 384 (228), FIXTURE 144 (SOFT), LANTERN 96 (SOFT), GLOWSTICK 96 (SOFT), FAULT_GLOW default 72 (SOFT). Fault glow: `GLOW_BASE_PM` `2` 800, `sev_min[2]` 1, `SEV_GLOW_PM` {0, 250, 500, 750, 1000}. Curve classes: SOFT `f = 1000 (R − d) / R`; HARD `f = 250 + 750 (R − d) / R`. The actionable radius of a SOFT output-1000 emitter is `R × 850 / 1000` (exact at output 1000 for every `R` from 24 to 384; for any other output use the published `r_lit_px`, D.7). Also fixed for the tests: `BEAM_TOGGLE_MIN_INTERVAL_TICKS` 12 (Player Controller, registry, once raised from 10; the sim guard itself is `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS`), `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` 24 with `BEAM_SPILL_PM` 600 (the shipped default; 0 in the spill-off branch of AC-10b only), `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` 12, `DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM` 500, `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` 48 and `DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX` 24 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, `REACH_MIN_PX` 24 and `REACH_MAX_PX` 384, `W_DELTA` 8, `W_BROAD` 30, `W_FRAME` 2 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, `EXPOSURE_TICK_CEILING_MS` 1.0 and `COLD_REBUILD_CEILING_MS` 8. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Test seams, fakes and the harness story** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the first draft named no seams, so most criteria could not run in isolation)*. Every criterion below runs in a headless fixture with zero scene nodes against these injected seams (dependency injection, never a singleton): (1) `FakeClock` — the kernel tick `T` and `ticks_for_seconds`, steppable one tick at a time; (2) `FakeLayout` — `OccluderSource` (`rects_in` over a `QueryBuffer`, `version`, `version_epoch`, `changed_region_*`), `anchor_geometry`, `body_of`, `section_offset`, scripted from Layout's own fixtures (`fixture_cryo_excerpt`, `fixture_airlock`, the C-1 wall); (3) `FakeGraph` — `served_pm`, `node(id)` (`state`, `run_pm`), `failures(filter)`, `section_of`, with an M14 spy, plus a real-Graph mode for the `(auto-int)` criteria; (4) `FakeBody` — `pos_curr`, `actor_section`, `body_of_actor`; (5) `FakeController` — `get_chest_x` / `get_chest_y`, `get_aim_angle`, `get_loco_state`; (6) `FakeSuit` — the charge view and a `force_off` caller; (7) `FakeDiagnostics` — the release counters of EM-24 as readable integers and the `build_is_debug` flag (D.15); (8) a `CommandBus` stub that submits `toggle_beam`, `set_switch` and `set_beacon` with an origin and a PlayerId, so replay, script and remote submitters are testable with no Adapter; (9) a `WorkCounter` exposing `emitter_work_units` per tick (AC-64b; injected through a `WorkCounter` port: a counting double in `tests/support/`, `NullWorkCounter` in every shipped and benchmarked build; GDScript has no conditional compilation, ADR-0010 section 4.4) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0010 sync)*; (10) a `FlashGovernorStub` that implements the registry `flash_count` definition (a rolling 60-tick half-open window, ⌈n / 2⌉ flashes per light) and exposes its defect counter, the declared seam for the "governor records a defect of 0" clauses of AC-90 and AC-90b *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Harness story EM-H0** (Logic, BLOCKING, first in the epic): build `tests/unit/emitter/` with these nine seams, a scripted-tick driver (`run(ticks, script)`), a from-scratch recompute oracle (AC-69, AC-69b), brute-force oracles for D.6 (AC-22e) and D.7 (AC-20b), and a snapshot-and-restore helper that restores at every tick of a script (AC-30, AC-54); no other emitter story starts before EM-H0 passes.

### Determinism and isolation

- **AC-1** **GIVEN** a headless fixture with zero scene nodes, **WHEN** the kernel steps 600 ticks with a seat beam, eight fixtures, a beacon, a glowstick and a fault glow, **THEN** two runs give an identical `state_hash` and no `Node` exists. `(auto)` [EM-1, EM-29, EM-30]
- **AC-2** **GIVEN** every `.gd` file under `src/sim/emitter/` (the data-tool outputs are loaded files, not code), **WHEN** a grep lint runs, **THEN** it finds none of `Light2D`, `LightOccluder2D`, `PhysicsServer2D`, `extends Node`, `get_tree`, `PERCEPTION_OUT`; none of the float, trig and RNG tokens `float`, `randf`, `randi`, `randomize`, `sqrt`, `sin(`, `cos(`, `atan`, `Vector2(` (`Vector2i` is allowed); no bare integer-division operator `/` and no bare modulus operator `%` outside the two named helpers `IntMath.floor_div` (non-negative operands, checked) and `IntMath.posmod_i` (a wrapper over the engine's `posmod`; `src/shared/int_math.gd`; ADR-0005 owns the list, ADR-0010 section 5.4 the path and the single site) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0010 sync)*: GDScript's integer `/` and `%` behaviour on negative operands is not sourceable from the pinned reference, so a possibly negative operand always goes through a helper; and no identifier containing `phantom`, `perceived`, `decoy` or `hallucinated`. **Lint fixtures** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the lint ships with `tests/unit/emitter/lint_fixtures/`, one `bad_<token>.gd` per banned token (each holding exactly that token in code, and the test expects the lint to fail naming it), and `good_allowed.gd` (holding `Vector2i`, `IntMath.floor_div`, `IntMath.posmod_i`, and every banned token inside comments and string literals, which the lint ignores); the lint passes only the good fixture, and the test fails if any bad fixture passes. `(lint)` [EM-3, EM-29, EM-30, H10, CR-12] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-3** **GIVEN** a scene and its mirror (x → −x, aim `a` → `2048 − a`, `flip_h` toggled), **WHEN** every subject is queried, **THEN** `lux_pm` and `lit` are the exact mirror for five drawn and three mirrored facings, every `CHEST_POINT` row and all four beam shapes. `(auto)` [EM-19, D.4, D.5]
- **AC-4** **GIVEN** reversed registration order of subjects and emitters, **WHEN** the same ticks run, **THEN** every `lux_pm`, `lit` and `top_emitter_id` is unchanged. `(auto)` [EM-4, D.7]
- **AC-5a** **GIVEN** a fake PRESENTATION subscriber that receives the read view and mutates what it received (lamp colour, flicker, decoy state and every field of a returned record), **WHEN** 600 ticks run, **THEN** the mutations never reach the block — the block's snapshot buffer is byte-identical to a run without the subscriber and the next read returns the unmutated values (no-mutation isolation; the view is read-only and copy-free, EM-25, so the criterion tests that a write through it has no effect and that the read allocates nothing: allocation counter 0) — and `state_hash` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* and every `lux`, `lit` and `top_emitter` answer equal the run without the subscriber. `(auto)` **AC-5b** **GIVEN** the `src/sim/emitter/` import graph, **THEN** nothing imports a Lighting, Presenter or Perception Filter module. `(lint)` **AC-5c** **GIVEN** the read-view surface, **THEN** no public function or property setter can write an emitter or subject field (the only writers are the mutators E1–E9, the commands and `emitter.place`; no `set_` accessor exists on a record), and a reader that retains the view or a slice of it beyond the call fails the no-retain lint (EM-25). `(lint)` [EM-25, EM-26, EM-29, P4] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-97** **GIVEN** `src/sim/emitter/`, **THEN** it holds no `signal` subscription to any block's change notification and no `connect` call, and its one hook declares only `after` entries naming registered hook ids. `(lint)` [EM-23, CR-10]

### Placement, pose and the beam

- **AC-6** **GIVEN** `pos_curr` (302, 200), facing W, `CHEST_POINT[E][walk]` (1, −34), **WHEN** the pose is read, **THEN** origin is (301, 166), occlusion origin (302, 200) and the hand point is never read. `(auto)` [EM-6, D.4]
- **AC-7** **GIVEN** a WIDE beam at (300, 166), aim 0, **WHEN** subjects at (540, 200), (540, 28), (540, 27) and the apex are tested, **THEN** inside, inside, outside, inside. `(auto)` [D.5]
- **AC-8** **GIVEN** a full-output WIDE beam at (0, 0), aim 0, **WHEN** subjects at (288, 0) and (288, 1) are queried (`d2` = 82,944 = R², and 82,945), **THEN** the first has `c` 250 and `lit` true; the second contributes 0 and `lit` is false. `(auto)` [D.5, D.7]
- **AC-9** **GIVEN** the SPLIT shape at every `aim_angle` 0..4095, **WHEN** the wedge is sampled at the reach, **THEN** the gap is at least 183 units (184 = 16.17°) and no point is in both cones. `(auto)` [EM-8, I-WEDGE]
- **AC-10** **GIVEN** feet 20 px south of a north wall (x 200..400, y 160..184), **WHEN** the beam is aimed N then S, **THEN** all subjects beyond the wall are unlit for N and the south subjects are lit for S. `(auto)` [EM-6, EM-20 g]
- **AC-10b** *(user ruling D24, 2026-10-27: 24 is the shipped default; the spill-off value 0 is the boundary branch)* **GIVEN** a loose item 12 px south of the feet in an otherwise unlit room, the beam on and aimed N, **THEN** at the shipped `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` 24 with `BEAM_SPILL_PM` 600 it is lit (actionable radius 18 px by the D.7 chain: lit at 18 px, unlit at 19 px) and prompts; an item 48 px to the side is unlit; with the beam off the item is unlit (the spill exists only while the beam is on); `section_emission` is identical to a build with `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` 0, in which the same item is unlit and has no prompt (the spill-off branch, the only value the dark-at-feet playtest metric may switch to); `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` 12 is refused at data load (I-REACH). `(auto)` [EM-6, EM-8 row 11] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **AC-10c** *(one row per subject kind the spill applies to, EM-6)* **GIVEN** the shipped spill (24 px, 600), the beam on and aimed N in an otherwise unlit room, a subject 12 px south of the feet, **THEN** (row 1, OBJECT) `lux_pm` and `act_lux_pm` are 300 (F = 500, c = 300) and `lit` is true; (row 2, ITEM) the same; (row 3, HORROR subject) `lux_pm` is 300 and `lit` is true (the spill is honest exposure, Horror reads it; no special case); (row 4, a second seat's ACTOR, VS-B, `(provisional — Co-op undesigned)`) `lux_pm` 300 and `act_lux_pm` 300; (row 5, the owner's own ACTOR) `lux_pm` stays the beam apex value 1000 and `act_lux_pm` stays 0 (the spill is never added to the owner's self-lux); and in every row a subject at 18 px is lit, one at 19 px is unlit, and with the beam off all five read 0. `(auto)` [EM-6, EM-21] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **AC-11** **GIVEN** a running player whose aim differs from facing, **WHEN** the beam is read, **THEN** the axis is the committed `aim_angle` and the SPLIT axes are ±206 units off it. `(auto)` [EM-6, D.4]
- **AC-12a** **GIVEN** `loco_state` IDLE, WALK, RUN, CARRY_WALK and CHANNELLING and a charge view above 0, **WHEN** `toggle_beam` applies at tick T, **THEN** `emitter.state.lit` is true after T's `emitter.place`. **AC-12b** **GIVEN** charge view 0, **THEN** a turn-ON is rejected `CHARGE_EMPTY` with state unchanged and a turn-OFF outside the strobe window is accepted. **AC-12c** **GIVEN** STAGGERED, DOWNED or DEAD, **THEN** `toggle_beam` is rejected `NOT_ACTIONABLE`. **AC-12d** **GIVEN** a beam id that does not exist, **THEN** `NO_SUCH_EMITTER`. **AC-12e** **GIVEN** a `toggle_beam` accepted at tick T, **THEN** a `toggle_beam` applied at any tick T+1 to T+(S−1), ON or OFF, where S = `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` (the constant, never a literal; 12 at the default, so T+1..T+11), is rejected `TOO_SOON` with `switch_on` and `last_toggle_tick` unchanged, one applied at T+S is accepted (parameterised over S = 11, 12 and 20, the range of the knob), the validate order is `NO_SUCH_EMITTER`, `NOT_ACTIONABLE`, `TOO_SOON`, `CHARGE_EMPTY` (a turn-ON at zero charge inside the window reports `TOO_SOON`), and a stream submitted by a replay, a script or a second PlayerId obeys the same guard with no Adapter present. `(auto)` [EM-7, EM-9, EM-10] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **AC-13a** **GIVEN** DOWNED, **WHEN** `emitter.place` runs, **THEN** `switch_on` is kept, aim is frozen at the last committed value and the origin is the chest point of the down row. **AC-13b** **GIVEN** STAGGERED, **THEN** `switch_on`, aim and origin are unchanged. **AC-13c** **GIVEN** the tick `loco_state` reads DEAD, **THEN** `switch_on` is false after `emitter.place` with no command in the log (tested on the emitter in isolation: the death stub restarts from the last autosave in the full game). `(auto)` [EM-9]
- **AC-14a** **GIVEN** Suit's `force_off(beam_id)` in `suit.vitals` on tick T, **WHEN** T's `emitter.place` runs, **THEN** the beam `output_pm` is 0 and `lit(ref)` of a subject lit only by that beam reads false at T+1 in P2 `validate`. `(auto)` **AC-14b** the owner's re-check at the first 6-tick boundary after the dark tick aborts the channel, so at most 5 channel ticks elapse on a dark target. `(auto-int: Interaction's IA-7 channel and a Suit `force_off` stub; both designed)` [EM-10, PC-7] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-15a** **GIVEN** `toggle_beam` issued by PlayerId 2 for PlayerId 1's beam, **THEN** `OWNED_BY_ISSUER` rejects it and `switch_on` is unchanged. **AC-15b** **GIVEN** a restore, **THEN** a `toggle_beam` queued for the first RUNNING tick is accepted (no refusal window), and `beam_bindings()` equals the flat array `[1, beam_id]` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. **AC-15c** **GIVEN** a `toggle_beam` accepted at T and a snapshot taken at T+3, **WHEN** restored and a `toggle_beam` applies at T+5, **THEN** it is rejected `TOO_SOON` exactly as in the uninterrupted run (`last_toggle_tick` is canonical). `(auto)` [EM-7, EM-28] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-16a** **GIVEN** the owner alone with the beam on, **THEN** the owner's ACTOR `lux_pm` is 1000 at all five facings and behind a wall, and `act_lux_pm` is 0; the zero `act_lux_pm` is correct by design: the self-patch is exempt and does not read it (Interaction AC-IA-33b) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. **AC-16b** **GIVEN** a second subject lit only by that beam through the ordinary cone test, **THEN** it is unaffected by the owner special case. **AC-16c** **THEN** `section_emission` for the section is 288 (WIDE, output 1000) and the beam appears once in `emitters()`. **AC-16d** **GIVEN** the owner in section A with the beam on (WIDE: `section_emission` 288 in A and 0 in B), **WHEN** the foot origin crosses the door into section B on tick T (Body's `actor_section` changes), **THEN** on that tick the beam record's and the ACTOR subject's `section_id` are B's, `section_emission` is 0 in A and 288 in B in that tick's table, `body_id` follows `body_of_actor` when a weld moves the actor's body, neither derived field is in `state_hash`, and an `actor_section` of NONE keeps the previous values and increments `emitter_actor_section_none`. `(auto-int with a Body fake)` [EM-4, EM-21, EM-27, D.12] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20))* **GIVEN** a fixture whose last realised OFF was at tick 120, **WHEN** `set_switch` ON is submitted at `T_a` 121, 126, 127 and 132 in four separate runs, **THEN** the first two are rejected `COOLDOWN` (`arm_tick` 126, 126 − 120 = 6) and the last two are accepted (`arm_tick` 132, 132 − 120 = 12); **AND GIVEN** an ON at 1201 (`arm_tick` 1206) **in three separate runs**, **THEN** in run A an OFF at 1203 is accepted and writes no `last_toggle_tick` (it cancels; the lamp never lights, AC-80), in run B an OFF at 1213 is rejected `COOLDOWN` (1213 − 1206 = 7), and in run C an OFF at 1218 is accepted (12) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; until they are, the story that carries AC-90d is not started, rather than started and failing *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. `(auto)` [I-SPACING] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the first draft asserted that no cooldown exists and routed the guard to the Adapter alone)*

### Exposure and occlusion

- **AC-18** **GIVEN** a full-output WIDE beam (1000, 288) and a subject at `d` 208 (`d2` 43,264), **THEN** `c` = 457. **GIVEN** also a fixture of output 848, reach 144, at `d` 100, **THEN** its `c` = 258 and `lux_pm` = 715. **GIVEN** also a fixture of output 1000, reach 144, at `d` 60, **THEN** its `c` = 583, the raw sum 1298 and `lux_pm` 1000 (saturated). **GIVEN** the beam alone and the subject at `d` 288, **THEN** `c` = 250 and `lit` true. Permuting registration order changes no value. `(auto)` [D.7, EM-18]
- **AC-19** **GIVEN** a HARD-edged emitter at `output_pm` 600 and 599 and a rim subject starting unlit, **THEN** lit at 600 (`c` 150) and unlit at 599. `(auto)` [D.7, I-EDGE]

### Serialization and restore

- **AC-39** **GIVEN** the block, **WHEN** `snapshot` → `restore` round-trips, **THEN** the canonical bytes (`canonical_encode` of the snapshot payload, a kernel-side read) are equal, the kernel's `block_hash(&"emitter.state", uncached = true)` is equal and `state_hash` is equal (the block exposes neither `canonical_bytes()` nor `block_hash()`; ADR-0005 sections 1, 4.3 and 5.3) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)* — and the derived set is rebuilt in ascending id; an unsupported `schema_version` is a reported failure. `(auto)` [EM-28, CR-8]
- **AC-40** **GIVEN** a subject at `act_lux_pm` 130 and `lit` true, **WHEN** saved, restored and run 600 ticks, **THEN** it is lit after the restore, `validate` at T+1 matches the uninterrupted run and hashes are equal at every tick. `(auto)` [EM-22, CP-2, G4]
- **AC-41** **GIVEN** a save at a Graph tick, **WHEN** restored, **THEN** the subject table, every lamp `output_pm` and the FAULT_GLOW set over the next five ticks equal uninterrupted play. `served_pm` is a persisted hook product read as-is after restore and the Graph's `post_restore` rebuilds only the descriptor list and derived caches (ADR-0011 §6.1; LS-21, CP-4), so the criterion is unblocked and is a regression gate against the real Graph; the `served_pm_last` fallback is retired. `(auto-int)` [EM-28, CP-4, G4] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-113** *(promoted; a Graph integration test, not a feel criterion)* **GIVEN** the real Graph, eight lamps switched on in one tick on a tier-3 island with 700 W free and a neighbouring 500 W heater, **THEN** `served_pm` reads ⌊1000 × 700 / 740⌋ = 945 for `INRUSH_STEPS` 5 Graph runs and then 1000, and each lamp's `output_pm` follows it (D.2); the test fails if the Graph's inrush constants change. `(auto-int)` [D.2, EM-11] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **AC-42** **GIVEN** registrations restored verbatim and registrants whose `post_restore` only compares them with their own window (no write), **WHEN** the restore completes, **THEN** the registration set equals the pre-save set and `state_hash` is unchanged, and a registrant whose window differs repairs it through E6 / E7 / E8 on its first hook run, with at most a one-run lag (ADR-0017 D10.2) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0017 sync)*. `(auto)` **AC-42b** a saved beacon's aim at every tick equals the uninterrupted run. `(auto)` [EM-21, D.10]
- **AC-42c (mid-ARMING)** **GIVEN** a lamp switched on at tick 1201 (ARMING, `arm_tick` 1206) and a snapshot taken at 1203, **WHEN** restored and run, **THEN** it lights at 1206 exactly as the uninterrupted run (`arm_tick` is canonical), a switch-off applied at 1204 cancels it identically in both runs, and `state_hash` is equal at every tick. `(auto)` [EM-12, EM-28] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-42d (FAULT_GLOW protection)** **GIVEN** eleven glowing descriptors (cap 10) whose lowest-`sev` record is 120 ticks old (protected for 480 more) and a snapshot taken then, **WHEN** restored, **THEN** `placed_tick` survives, that record stays protected for the remaining 480 ticks and the same unprotected record is evicted on the same Graph tick as in the uninterrupted run. `(auto-int)` [EM-17, EM-28] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-42e (STARVED)** **GIVEN** a lamp with `switch_on` true and `served_pm` 0 (STARVED) at the snapshot, **WHEN** restored and `served_pm` returns to 1000 on the next Graph tick, **THEN** it lights with no new command on the same tick as in the uninterrupted run. `(auto-int)` [EM-13, EM-28] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **AC-73** **GIVEN** the registered block, **THEN** its descriptor has `block_id emitter.state`, `class SIM`, `persist` true, `persist_scope WORLD`, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, `schema_version` 1, **`supported_versions` exactly [1], `added_in_format` 1** and `restore_after` exactly `[layout.state, graph.state, body.state, controller.state]` (Layout's and Ship Body's blocks are designed; the Graph's `restore_after: [layout.state]` and the Controller's PC-14 are the precedents); **AND GIVEN** fixtures that omit `supported_versions`, list it without `schema_version`, or lack the `added_in_format` entry in the format data file, **THEN** Save/Load's boot lints refuse to start with `SL_BOOT_NO_SUPPORTED_VERSIONS` or `SL_BOOT_NO_ADDED_IN_FORMAT` (AC-SL-64). `(auto)` [EM-1, CR-8, SL-15] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
